TOCLOCO Inc

The Gantt module

createGantt: the scheduling engine and the critical path, the four dependency links, milestones and summaries, and editable bars.

All 25 pages Everything on one page → Developer guide →

The Gantt module

modules/gantt is a separate, opt-in project-planning module — its own bundle, imported only when you want it, changing nothing in the grid core. It turns a task list into a real schedule: a CPM (Critical Path Method) engine computes each task's early/late start and finish, its slack (total float), and the zero-float critical path, recomputing on every edit. computeSchedule(tasks, deps) is the pure engine; createGantt(opts) is a controller that holds the model, recomputes on setTasks/setDependencies/applyEdit, and emits schedule (or error). Dependencies are the four standard link types — LINK_TYPES is ['FS','SS','FF','SF'] — each with optional lag/lead. A milestone is a zero-duration task scheduled as a point; a summary task (any task named as another's parent) is derived from its children (start = earliest child, end = latest child, duration-weighted progress) and is not scheduled itself. Bad input never throws or loops: a dependency cycle is refused and reported with a code from SCHEDULE_ERROR, and findViolations flags any task placed earlier than its predecessors allow. toISODate converts an engine day-number back to a calendar date for display.

Feed it the rows you already have. fields names your own task properties for the scheduler — { id: 'taskId', start: 'startDate', name: 'jobName', duration: 'dur' }, each a field name or a reader (row) => value — so a task list arrives as it is rather than being renamed first. The vocabulary is id, name, start, end, duration, milestone, percentComplete, parent, baselineStart, baselineEnd, constraint and constraintDate; anything you leave unmapped reads its canonical name, so an existing plan is unaffected. rowKey reaches the scheduler too, so a row carrying taskId and no id is identified by it — a task's own id still wins where it has one. It is a read mapping: applyEdit and level() write the canonical property, so each says so plainly rather than writing to a field the schedule is not read from, and assignee/cost/actualCost belong to the resource and earned-value layers rather than to this list.

import { createGantt, computeSchedule } from '@toclocoinc/lattice-grid/modules/gantt';

const plan = createGantt({
  tasks: [
    { id: 'design', duration: 5 },
    { id: 'build', duration: 6 },
    { id: 'launch', milestone: true },
  ],
  dependencies: [
    { from: 'design', to: 'build', type: 'FS' },
    { from: 'build', to: 'launch', type: 'FS' },
  ],
});
plan.on('schedule', (s) => console.log(s.critical, s.projectDuration));
plan.applyEdit({ id: 'design', duration: 7 }); // recomputes; the critical path shifts

Effort-driven scheduling (BACKLOG-0001862). A task's schedulingMode — 'fixedDuration' (the default), 'fixedEffort' or 'fixedUnits' — decides which of the three quantities in effort = duration × Σ units × hours​PerDay is held when another changes, set per task or as the createGantt default. On a fixedEffort task the duration is derived from the stated effort and the assigned resources' hours, so adding a resource shortens it; the duration is recomputed once, before the critical path, so the schedule, level(), the workload band and earned value all read the one number. reconcileEffort is the pure rule every edit funnels through; MODE 0/1/2 and EffortDriven round-trip through MSPDI.

const { createGantt, computeSchedule, reconcileEffort, resolveSchedulingMode, SCHEDULING_MODES } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

// SCHEDULING_MODES lists the three; a host spelling resolves to one of them.
const mode = resolveSchedulingMode('fixed-effort'); // 'fixedEffort'
if (!SCHEDULING_MODES.includes(mode)) throw new Error('unknown mode');

// A 40-hour fixed-effort task: a full-timer (8h/day) finishes it in 5 days.
const full = createGantt({
  tasks: [{ id: 'A', schedulingMode: mode, effort: 40, assignments: [{ resource: 'ren', units: 1 }] }],
  resources: [{ id: 'ren', calendar: { hoursPerDay: 8 } }],
  hoursPerDay: 8,
});
const fullDays = full.compute().tasks.get('A').duration;

// A part-timer (4h/day) accrues the same 40 hours over twice as long — 10 days.
const part = createGantt({
  tasks: [{ id: 'A', schedulingMode: mode, effort: 40, assignments: [{ resource: 'sam', units: 1 }] }],
  resources: [{ id: 'sam', calendar: { hoursPerDay: 4 } }],
  hoursPerDay: 8,
});
const partDays = part.compute().tasks.get('A').duration;

// Adding a second full unit to the full-timer's task halves its duration.
const added = reconcileEffort({ mode, duration: 5, units: 1, hoursPerDay: 8 }, { field: 'addResource', units: 1 });

// computeSchedule is the pure engine behind the controller.
const direct = computeSchedule(
  [{ id: 'A', schedulingMode: mode, effort: 40, assignments: [{ resource: 'ren', units: 1 }] }],
  [], { hoursPerDay: 8, resourceHours: new Map([['ren', 8]]) },
).tasks.get('A').duration;

full.destroy(); part.destroy();
return `full ${fullDays}, part ${partDays}, +resource ${added.duration}`;

Assigning resources (BACKLOG-0001868). In the split view the assignee cell is a button that opens an assignment picker: a searchable, multi-select list of resources, each showing its load over the task's dates, a units percentage per assignment (100 by default) and a remove button. The load comes from the same computeResourceLoad as the workload band — previewAssignments(id, roster) runs it with that one task's roster swapped, so an over-allocating choice is announced (in a polite live region) before it is committed. Apply sends the whole roster as one applyEdit({ id, assignments }): the task reschedules once per its schedulingMode, the write-back follows the plan's columns and fields, and it is a single change event. The picker is a listbox you can drive from the keyboard (arrows, Enter, Escape, Tab stays inside). Add 'effort' and 'units' to the split view's columns for two optional columns that edit through the same applyEdit rule.

Single-assignee mode (BACKLOG-0001938). A task states assignmentPicker: { max, units } to shape its own picker. max: 1 makes the roster single-assignee: choosing a second resource replaces the first (a larger max drops the oldest entry once the roster is full), and the list's aria-multiselectable reflects it. units shows the per-assignment percent field — the default since the picker shipped, so units: false renders rows without it. The roster is still committed as one applyEdit({ assignments }).

const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const plan = createGantt({
  projectStart: '2027-01-04',
  resources: { Ann: 1, Bob: 1 },
  tasks: [
    { id: 'bob-busy', name: 'Bob is busy', start: '2027-01-04', duration: 4, assignee: 'Bob' },
    { id: 'build', name: 'Build', start: '2027-01-04', duration: 4, schedulingMode: 'fixedEffort',
      assignments: [{ resource: 'Ann', units: 1 }] },
  ],
});
let events = 0;
plan.on('schedule', () => { events += 1; });

// What the picker shows before anything is committed: the load with Bob added.
const roster = [{ resource: 'Ann', units: 1 }, { resource: 'Bob', units: 1 }];
const preview = plan.previewAssignments('build', roster);
const over = preview.overAllocations.find((o) => o.resource === 'Bob');
const warning = `over ${over.resource} ${over.load * 100}% of ${over.capacity * 100}%`;

// Apply: one edit, one change event; fixed-effort, so two people halve the duration.
plan.applyEdit({ id: 'build', assignments: roster });
const build = plan.schedule.tasks.get('build');
const out = `${warning}; duration ${build.duration}, effort ${build.effort}, events ${events}`;
plan.destroy();
return out;
const { createTestDom, TestEvent } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root, document } = createTestDom();
const gantt = createGantt({
  projectStart: '2027-01-04',
  resources: { Ann: 1, Bob: 1 },
  tasks: [
    { id: 'build', name: 'Build', start: '2027-01-04', duration: 4,
      assignmentPicker: { max: 1, units: true }, assignee: 'Ann' },
  ],
});
const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, { columns: ['name', 'assignee', 'units'] });
const NS = 'lat-gantt-split';
const click = (el) => el.dispatchEvent(new TestEvent('click', { bubbles: true }));
// Open the picker and choose Bob: max: 1 replaces Ann rather than adding to her.
click(root.querySelector('[data-picker-for="build"]'));
const option = (name) => [...document.querySelectorAll('[role="option"]')]
  .find((o) => o.getAttribute('data-resource') === name);
click(option('Bob'));
const chosen = [...document.querySelectorAll('.' + NS + '__picker-row')]
  .map((row) => row.getAttribute('data-resource'));
const units = document.querySelector('.' + NS + '__picker-units') ? 'shown' : 'hidden';
click(document.querySelector('.' + NS + '__picker-apply'));
const roster = gantt.taskById('build').assignments;
view.destroy();
return `chosen ${chosen.join('+')}; units ${units}; roster ${roster.map((a) => `${a.resource} ${a.units * 100}%`).join('+')}`;

A standalone resource-load band (BACKLOG-0001949). createResourceView(container, opts) draws the workload band on its own — resources and bookings, with no task Gantt, no plan and no CPM — for a resource-availability view by department, portfolio or scenario. It reuses the split view's workload band rather than forking it, so day/week/month/quarter buckets with the scale header and zoom, hours/percent/cost units with utilisation thresholds, expand-to-bookings, the workload:click bucket event, its own grid-backed column definitions and width round-trip, and SVG export all come across unchanged. Bookings are { resource, start, end, units | hours, label, group } (the workload.external shape), and a resource expands into its bookings grouped by group — one summed sub-row per group; setBookings applies a changed set live, and the time-bucket columns never carry a grid control.

const { createTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGrid } = await import('../packages/dom/src/index.js');
const { createResourceView } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom();
const rv = createResourceView(root, {
  createGrid,
  resources: [{ id: 'Ann', name: 'Ann', capacity: 1 }, { id: 'Bob', name: 'Bob', capacity: 1 }],
  bookings: [
    { resource: 'Ann', start: '2027-01-04', end: '2027-01-09', hours: 40, label: 'Project X', group: 'Portfolio A' },
    { resource: 'Bob', start: '2027-01-04', end: '2027-01-09', hours: 120, label: 'Project Y', group: 'Portfolio B' },
  ],
  zoom: 'week',
  unit: 'percent',
  thresholds: [{ at: 1.0, colour: 'red' }],
});
const countRows = () => { let n = 0; rv.grid.rows.forEach(() => { n += 1; }); return n; };
const rows = countRows();                       // Ann, Bob, totals
// Bob's 120h single week is over any capacity-1 week: its bucket cell flags.
// The buckets are grid cells (BACKLOG-0002097), read through the bucket
// column's own cell spec — the class function the grid itself paints with.
const bucketIds = rv.grid.columns.visible().map((c) => c.id).filter((id) => /^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/.test(id));
const cellClass = (key, colId) => {
  const row = rv.grid.rows.byKey(key);
  return rv.grid.columns.get(colId).cell.class({ row, value: rv.grid.rows.value(key, colId), data: row.data });
};
const over = bucketIds.some((id) => cellClass('Bob\u0000', id).includes('lat-gantt-split__wl-bucket--over')) ? 'yes' : 'no';
rv.setBookings([{ resource: 'Ann', start: '2027-01-04', end: '2027-01-09', hours: 40, group: 'Portfolio A' }]);
const after = countRows();                       // Ann, totals — Bob's gone
const svg = rv.toSVG().includes('<svg') ? 'yes' : 'no';
const unit = rv.getUnit();
rv.destroy();
return `unit ${unit}; rows ${rows}; after ${after}; over ${over}; svg ${svg}`;

Grouping the resources by a field. groupBy groups the resource rows by one of the resources' own fields — 'department', a business unit, a division — or by a function of the resource. Each group draws a collapsible header row carrying the group's summed load and available hours, so the thresholds and the percent unit read the group's own utilisation; resources that name no group (and Unassigned) collect last. A sort on the left-hand columns keeps every resource under its group; toggleGroup(value) collapses or expands a group.

const { createTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGrid } = await import('../packages/dom/src/index.js');
const { createResourceView } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom();
const rv = createResourceView(root, {
  createGrid,
  groupBy: 'department',
  resources: [
    { id: 'ann', name: 'Ann', capacity: 1, department: 'Engineering' },
    { id: 'bob', name: 'Bob', capacity: 1, department: 'Engineering' },
    { id: 'cat', name: 'Cat', capacity: 1, department: 'Design' },
  ],
  bookings: [
    { resource: 'ann', start: '2027-01-04', end: '2027-01-09', hours: 40, label: 'Project X' },
    { resource: 'bob', start: '2027-01-04', end: '2027-01-09', hours: 30, label: 'Project Y' },
    { resource: 'cat', start: '2027-01-04', end: '2027-01-09', hours: 10, label: 'Project Z' },
  ],
  zoom: 'week',
});
const labels = () => { const out = []; rv.grid.rows.forEach((r) => out.push(rv.grid.rows.text(r.key, 'resource'))); return out.join(', '); };
const shown = labels();
// The Engineering group header is a grid row (BACKLOG-0002097); its bucket
// cells are grid cells whose values sum the group's resources: 40 + 30 = 70 h.
let engKey = null;
rv.grid.rows.forEach((r) => { if (rv.grid.rows.text(r.key, 'resource') === 'Engineering') engKey = r.key; });
const bucketIds = rv.grid.columns.visible().map((c) => c.id).filter((id) => /^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/.test(id));
const engLoad = bucketIds.map((id) => rv.grid.rows.value(engKey, id)).filter((v) => v != null && v > 0).join('');
rv.toggleGroup('Engineering');
const collapsed = labels();
rv.destroy();
return `${shown} | Engineering ${engLoad} h | collapsed ${collapsed}`;

Plan-table defaults for the split view's grids (BACKLOG-0002074). The task table and the band resource tables are plan tables, not data-entry grids, so mountSplit builds them with no cell-range selection and no fill handle (the task table keeps its row selection, keyboard navigation and editing); a band cell shows the focus outline only for keyboard focus. gridConfig is laid over those defaults and applies to every table, so gridConfig: { selection: { ranges: true, fillHandle: true } } restores both, and gridConfig.band is laid over it again for the workload and histogram band tables only.

const { createTestDom, resetTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGrid } = await import('../packages/dom/src/index.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const plan = { projectStart: '2027-01-04', tasks: [{ id: 'a', name: 'A', duration: 2, assignee: 'Ann' }] };
const probe = (gridConfig) => {
  const { root } = createTestDom();
  const gantt = createGantt(plan);
  const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, { createGrid, workload: {}, gridConfig });
  const sel = (g) => g.get('selection');
  const out = { tasks: sel(view.grids.tasks), band: sel(view.grids.workload) };
  view.destroy();
  return out;
};
const plain = probe(undefined);
const host = probe({ selection: { ranges: true, fillHandle: true } });
const bandOnly = probe({ band: { selection: { ranges: true } } });
return `default ranges ${plain.band.ranges} fill ${plain.band.fillHandle} | host ranges ${host.band.ranges} fill ${host.tasks.fillHandle} | band-only tasks ${bandOnly.tasks.ranges} band ${bandOnly.band.ranges}`;

Selecting tasks (BACKLOG-0001982, BACKLOG-0001995). The selection is the task table's row selection, extended to the timeline bars: a click selects a task (as a row or a bar), Ctrl/Cmd-click toggles it, Shift-click selects a range, and the arrow keys move it; the selected rows and bars carry a theme token. It is driven from code with select(ids), deselect(ids) and read with selected(), each firing one selectionChange with { selectedIds, added, removed, cause }. It survives redraws, zoom and filters, and a selected task that a collapse or a filter hides stays selected — selectedHidden() reports which. Expanding or collapsing a summary row (by chevron, keyboard or view.toggle) raises the vetoable beforeTaskExpand/beforeTaskCollapse and then taskExpand/taskCollapse with the new expandedIds; the workload band's resource rows raise resourceExpand/resourceCollapse.

const { createTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom();
const gantt = createGantt({
  projectStart: '2027-01-04',
  tasks: [
    { id: 'phase', name: 'Phase' },
    { id: 'A', name: 'A', parent: 'phase', duration: 2 },
    { id: 'B', name: 'B', parent: 'phase', duration: 2 },
    { id: 'C', name: 'C', parent: 'phase', duration: 2 },
  ],
});
const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, {});
const changes = [];
gantt.on('selectionChange', (e) => changes.push(e.cause));
gantt.select('A');                 // one selectionChange, cause 'api'
gantt.select(['B', 'C']);          // adds B and C in one more
const picked = gantt.selected().join('+');
view.toggle('phase');              // collapse: A, B, C hide but stay selected
const hidden = gantt.selectedHidden().length;
gantt.deselect(['B']);             // remove B, leaving A and C
view.destroy();
return `selected ${picked} | hidden ${hidden} | after deselect ${gantt.selected().join('+')} | changes ${changes.length}`;

Copy, cut and paste (BACKLOG-0002012). Ctrl/Cmd+C copies the selected tasks with their subtrees, Ctrl/Cmd+X cuts them, Ctrl/Cmd+V pastes below the focused row and Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+V pastes as its children. Each copy has a new id and keeps its assignments; links between two copied tasks are re-created and links to an outside task are dropped. From code: copyTasks(ids), cutTasks(ids) and pasteTasks({ below: id } | { childOf: id }). A paste is gated on the vetoable beforeTaskPaste (carrying the tasks about to be inserted; a veto raises taskPaste:cancelled) and is one undo step, so a cut then a paste is two, each reversible. The system clipboard also receives a tab-separated text form (name, start, end, duration, percent) for a spreadsheet, and tab-separated text pasted from outside (pasteTasks({ below: id, text }), or a browser paste event) creates tasks the same way.

const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const gantt = createGantt({
  projectStart: '2027-01-04',
  tasks: [
    { id: 'X', name: 'Outside', duration: 2 },
    { id: 'S', name: 'Summary' },
    { id: 'S1', name: 'One', parent: 'S', duration: 2, assignee: 'Ann' },
    { id: 'S2', name: 'Two', parent: 'S', duration: 3 },
  ],
  dependencies: [{ from: 'S1', to: 'S2' }, { from: 'X', to: 'S1' }],
});
gantt.on('beforeTaskPaste', (e) => { if (e.tasks.length > 10) e.preventDefault('too many'); });
gantt.copyTasks(['S']);
const ids = gantt.pasteTasks({ below: 'X' });
const [, s1, s2] = ids;
const deps = gantt.dependencies;
const kept = deps.some((d) => d.from === s1 && d.to === s2) ? 'link kept' : 'link lost';
const external = deps.some((d) => d.to === s1 && d.from === 'X') ? 'external kept' : 'external dropped';
gantt.undo();
gantt.cutTasks(['S']);
const left = gantt.schedule.order.length;
gantt.undo();
return `copies ${ids.length} | ${kept} | ${external} | cut left ${left} | undo restores ${gantt.schedule.order.length}`;

Task actions (BACKLOG-0002000). addTask(task?, { above | below | child | successorOf | predecessorOf: id }) returns the new id; convertToMilestone(id) / convertToTask(id) turn a task into a zero-duration diamond and back; deleteTask(id) removes a task and its links. Each is one undo step and gated by a vetoable event — beforeTaskAdd, beforeMilestoneConvert and the existing beforeTaskDelete — whose veto raises taskAdd:cancelled, milestoneConvert:cancelled or taskDelete:cancelled with the reason. A new task takes a catalogue name ("New task"), a duration of 1 and its sibling's start (or the project start); a successor or predecessor is linked finish-to-start, and the WBS code follows the new position. The split view's contextMenu: true offers them as default items (add task above / below, add subtask, add successor, add predecessor, convert to milestone, delete), and a contextMenu(task, event, defaults) function receives those items as its third argument to keep, reorder, hide or extend. From the keyboard, Insert adds a task below the focused row, Shift+Insert adds a child and Delete deletes (vetoable). A read-only task refuses an interface-driven child, successor, predecessor, convert or delete on itself (reason: 'readOnly'); a plain API call still works.

const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const gantt = createGantt({
  projectStart: '2027-01-04',
  tasks: [
    { id: 'A', name: 'Design', duration: 3 },
    { id: 'B', name: 'Build', duration: 2 },
    { id: 'C', name: 'Ship', duration: 1 },
  ],
});
const below = gantt.addTask({}, { below: 'A' });
const next = gantt.addTask({ name: 'Review' }, { successorOf: 'B' });
const linked = gantt.dependencies.some((d) => d.from === 'B' && d.to === next && d.type === 'FS');
gantt.convertToMilestone('C');
const milestone = `milestone duration ${gantt.taskById('C').duration}`;
gantt.convertToTask('C');
const stop = gantt.on('beforeTaskDelete', (e) => e.preventDefault('keep'));
gantt.deleteTask('A');
const kept = gantt.schedule.order.length;
stop();
gantt.undo();   // reverts the Review task and its link together
gantt.undo();   // reverts the conversion back to a task
gantt.undo();   // reverts the milestone conversion
gantt.undo();   // reverts the first add
return `added ${below} | successor ${linked ? 'linked FS' : 'unlinked'} | ${milestone} | vetoed delete kept ${kept} | can undo ${gantt.canUndo()}`;

Scheduling conflicts (BACKLOG-0002004). After each recompute the controller derives a normalised conflict set — a dependency cycle, an unhonoured dated constraint, a hard lock a predecessor would move, or a task past its deadline — and fires schedulingConflict only when that set changed, carrying { conflicts, added, resolved }, each conflict a { kind, tasks, links, message }. Read it any time from gantt.schedulingConflicts. A host load (setTasks, rows.apply) seeds the set silently. The split view's issuesPanel: true lists the set in a collapsible panel with a count badge, keyboard operable; a row click scrolls to and highlights its tasks, and each row offers the fix actions safe for its kind — remove the offending link, set the constraint to as-soon-as-possible, unlock the task, clear the deadline — each one undo step through the controller's own gated path.

const { createTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom();
const gantt = createGantt({
  projectStart: '2027-01-04',
  tasks: [{ id: 'a', name: 'A', duration: 2 }, { id: 'b', name: 'B', duration: 2 }],
  dependencies: [{ from: 'a', to: 'b', type: 'FS' }],
});
const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, { issuesPanel: true });
const seen = [];
gantt.on('schedulingConflict', (e) => seen.push(e));

// Close a loop a -> b -> a: one event names the cycle.
gantt.setDependencies([
  { from: 'a', to: 'b', type: 'FS' },
  { from: 'b', to: 'a', type: 'FS' },
]);
const added = `${seen[0].added[0].kind} | tasks ${[...seen[0].conflicts[0].tasks].sort().join(',')}`;

// Remove the offending link: the cycle is resolved.
gantt.deleteDependency({ from: 'b', to: 'a', type: 'FS' });
const resolved = seen[1].resolved[0].kind;
const panelRows = root.querySelectorAll('.lat-gantt-split__issues-row').length;
view.destroy();
return `added ${added} | resolved ${resolved} | panel ${panelRows}`;

Linked data bands (BACKLOG-0001958). mountSplit(el, { dataBands: [...] }) stacks one or more collapsible bands under the gantt and the resource bands, each showing a different measure per task per timeline bucket — forecast cost per week, say — laid out like the workload band and scroll- and zoom-locked to the timeline. A band reads value(task, bucketStart, bucketEnd, ctx) per leaf task, or a series field on the task (task.cost = [{ date, value }] at any granularity), re-bucketed to the current zoom: interpolate: 'none' puts each point in its bucket, 'spread' spreads it over its days (a monthly figure over its month) and re-sums. Rows follow the task tree (collapsing in step with the gantt; rows: 'summaries', 'resources' or a host grouping function are the alternatives), summaries roll up by aggregate ('sum', 'avg', 'last', 'min', 'max' or a function), with a totals row and column (totals: { row, column }), a format ('currency', 'number', 'percent' or a function), thresholds (theme-token tones) and cellStyle(value, ctx). With createGrid injected the left-hand columns are a real grid (resize, sort, filter, state); the date (bucket) columns never carry a header control under any config. A moved or resized task onReschedule: 'shift' (default), 'stretch'es or 'keep's its series and fires dataBand:change with the new series to save; an editable band opens a cell editor on double-click (or Enter/F2), gated by the cancellable beforeDataBandEdit (dataBandEdit:cancelled when refused). The view's getDataBandState/setDataBandState (also under getState().dataBands), toggleDataBand(id) and getDataBandTable(id) (one column per bucket, for an Excel/CSV writer) round it out; toSVG and print carry every band.

const { createTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGrid } = await import('../packages/dom/src/index.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom();
const gantt = createGantt({
  projectStart: '2027-01-04',
  tasks: [
    { id: 'p', name: 'Phase' },
    { id: 'a', name: 'Design', parent: 'p', start: '2027-01-04', duration: 5,
      cost: [{ date: '2027-01-04', value: 100 }, { date: '2027-01-11', value: 30 }] },
    { id: 'b', name: 'Build', parent: 'p', start: '2027-01-11', duration: 5, cost: [{ date: '2027-01-12', value: 200 }] },
    { id: 'c', name: 'Alpha', start: '2027-01-04', duration: 3, cost: [{ date: '2027-01-05', value: 60 }] },
  ],
});
const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, {
  createGrid, zoom: 'week', weekStartDay: 1, locale: 'en-US',
  dataBands: [{ id: 'cost', title: 'Forecast cost', series: 'cost', format: 'currency', editable: true }],
});
const table = () => view.getDataBandTable('cost');
const week = table().columns.findIndex((c) => c.id === '2027-01-04');
const total = table().rows.find((r) => r[0] === 'Total');
const phase = table().rows.find((r) => r[0] === 'Phase');

// Move Build a week later: its cost moves with it.
let moved = '';
gantt.on('dataBand:change', (e) => { moved = e.series[0].date; });
gantt.applyEdit({ id: 'b', start: gantt.schedule.tasks.get('b').es + 7 }, { event: { type: 'pointerup' } });

// A host veto keeps the bucket as it was.
gantt.on('beforeDataBandEdit', (e) => e.preventDefault('locked'));
// Each band is a real grid (view.grids.dataBands[id]); its bucket cells edit
// through the grid, gated by beforeDataBandEdit. A refused edit leaves $30.
const bandGrid = view.grids.dataBands.cost;
bandGrid.edit.start('a', '2027-01-11');
bandGrid.edit.stop(false, { colId: '2027-01-11', value: 75 });
const col11 = table().columns.findIndex((c) => c.id === '2027-01-11');
const after = table().rows.find((r) => r[0] === 'Design')[col11];
view.destroy();
return `week of 4 Jan $${total[week]} | phase ${phase[1]} | moved to ${moved} | vetoed $${after}`;

Task-bar pointer events and the progress handle (BACKLOG-0001985 … BACKLOG-0001992, BACKLOG-0001999). Every bar gesture raises its own events on the controller (gantt.on), with id, rawTask (the task as you supplied it), the scheduled task, the view and the originating DOM event. Dates are ISO dates and end is the scheduled finish boundary. The before-events are cancellable with preventDefault(reason) and pair with a :cancelled event; a gesture cannot wait, so a handler that returns a Promise cancels it as 'deferred'. Keyboard edits raise the same start events as pointer drags (and a resize / progress edit its end / drop too); none fire for your own setTasks or rows.apply. A leaf bar (in both mount and mountSplit) shows a progress handle at its percent-complete point on hover or focus — a 16px target on the bar's bottom edge; dragging it previews the fill live and commits one percentComplete edit through beforeProgressChange and the undo history, snapped to progressStep (default 5) and clamped to 0–100. Alt+Shift+Left/Right on a focused bar change it by one step (Shift+arrows keep resizing). Milestones have no handle and summaries are read-only. The Lattice names, against Bryntum Gantt's:

LatticeBryntumWhen, and what it carries
beforeTaskDragbeforeTaskDragA move drag passed the 3px threshold (or a keyboard move is about to apply); preventDefault(reason) cancels it before anything moves and fires taskDrag:cancelled.
taskDragStarttaskDragStartOnce per move drag, before any position changes: { id, rawTask, task, start, end, pointer }.
taskDragtaskDragAt most once per animation frame: { from, to, deltaDays, valid, pointer, pushed } — to is the snapped landing, valid false for a lock or a constraint that would put the task elsewhere, pushed the successors that would move.
taskDrag:cancelledtaskDragAbortA refused start (reason from the veto) or Escape ('escape').
beforeTaskResizeStartbeforeTaskResizeA resize is about to start; vetoable, the refusal fires taskResize:cancelled with phase: 'start'. Lattice's own beforeTaskResize is still the commit gate.
taskResizeStart / taskPartialResize / taskResizeEndtaskResizeStart / taskPartialResize / taskResizeEnd{ edge, from: { start, end, duration }, to, valid }; the partial event per frame, the end on release (before the commit). Escape fires taskResize:cancelled with phase: 'drag'.
beforePercentBarDrag / percentBarDragStartpercentBarDragStartThe progress handle's drag start, vetoable through the before-event (percentBarDrag:cancelled).
percentBarDrag / percentBarDroppercentBarDrag / percentBarDrop{ from, to } in percent, snapped to progressStep; the drop is raised on release and beforeProgressChange still gates the commit.
percentBarDrag:cancelledpercentBarDragAbortA refused start or Escape.
taskClicktaskClick{ part, date, event } — part is bar, progress, label, baseline, milestone or summary, date the day under the pointer. Never the release of a drag. Enter / Space on a focused bar raise it too.
taskDblClicktaskDblClickThe same payload; preventDefault() claims it so a host opens its own editor. Two taskClick precede it.
taskMouseOver / taskMouseOuttaskMouseOver / taskMouseOutOnce per entry / exit of a bar, milestone or summary bar (not empty row space). Keyboard focus reaching or leaving a bar raises the same pair, with the focus event.
taskContextMenutaskContextMenuA right-click, or the Menu key / Shift+F10 on a focused row (BACKLOG-0001980): { part, event }. preventDefault() claims it — the split view's built-in menu (when contextMenu is set) does not open, and the browser's own is suppressed either way, freeing a host to show its own.
 
const { createTestDom, resetTestDom, TestEvent } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom();
const gantt = createGantt({
  projectStart: '2027-01-04',
  tasks: [{ id: 'a', name: 'Alpha', duration: 6, percentComplete: 20 }],
});
gantt.mountSplit(root, { progressStep: 10 });
const seen = [];
for (const name of ['beforePercentBarDrag', 'percentBarDragStart', 'percentBarDrop', 'beforeTaskDrag', 'taskDragStart', 'taskClick']) {
  gantt.on(name, (e) => seen.push(name === 'percentBarDrop' ? `${name}(${e.from}→${e.to})` : name));
}
// Each edit redraws the timeline, so the bar is looked up afresh every time.
const row = () => root.querySelector('.lat-gantt-split__barrow[data-task="a"]');
const key = (k, extra = {}) => row().dispatchEvent(new TestEvent('keydown', { key: k, bubbles: true, ...extra }));

// Alt+Shift+ArrowRight: one progressStep, through beforeProgressChange and the undo history.
key('ArrowRight', { shiftKey: true, altKey: true });
const pct = gantt.tasks[0].percentComplete;
// Enter on a focused bar is a click.
key('Enter');
// A host refuses moves at the drag's start; the keyboard move raises the same gate.
gantt.on('beforeTaskDrag', (e) => e.preventDefault('frozen'));
let why = null;
gantt.on('taskDrag:cancelled', (e) => { why = e.reason; });
key('ArrowRight');
const moved = gantt.tasks[0].start != null;
const handle = root.querySelector('.lat-gantt-split__progresshandle') !== null;

gantt.destroy();
resetTestDom();

return `${seen.join(' ')} | ${pct}% | ${why} moved ${moved} | handle ${handle}`;

Dependency-link pointer events and the task tooltip (BACKLOG-0001980). A link's arrow raises its own click / double-click / context-menu / hover, each carrying { from, to, linkType, lag, view, event } — the link's type is named linkType, not type, because every Gantt event payload's type is the event's own name; lag is read back from gantt.dependencies since it is not drawn on the arrow itself (the visible one is a separate label). A double click, or Enter/Space on a focused link, is the cancellable linkDblClick: claiming it stops the built-in type/lag editor from opening (the pointer route runs no default of its own, the same shape taskDblClick already has). linkContextMenu is cancellable the same way as taskContextMenu — neither view draws a menu for a link, so claiming it only suppresses the browser's own. The task tooltip's own tooltipShow is vetoable — preventDefault() keeps the built-in card from showing at all, for a host that wants its own in its place — fired once per distinct task shown, and its pair tooltipHide notifies only when a shown one is actually dismissed. The Lattice names, against Bryntum's:

LatticeBryntumWhen, and what it carries
linkClickdependencyClickA single click on the link's arrow: { from, to, linkType, lag, view, event }.
linkDblClickdependencyDblClickThe same payload, plus preventDefault; two linkClick precede the pointer route.
linkContextMenudependencyContextMenuThe same payload, plus preventDefault.
linkHoverdependencyMouseOverOnce per entry of a link's arrow (moving onto its own arrowhead is not a re-entry).
tooltipShowtaskTooltipShow{ id, rawTask, task, view, text, event }; preventDefault() vetoes the built-in card.
tooltipHidetaskTooltipHideThe same context, without text; raised only when a tooltip was actually shown.
 
const { createTestDom, resetTestDom, TestEvent } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom();
const gantt = createGantt({
  projectStart: '2027-01-04',
  tasks: [{ id: 'a', name: 'A', duration: 2 }, { id: 'b', name: 'B', duration: 2 }],
  dependencies: [{ from: 'a', to: 'b', type: 'FS', lag: 3 }],
});
const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, { tooltip: true });
const seen = [];
for (const name of ['taskContextMenu', 'linkClick', 'linkDblClick', 'linkContextMenu', 'linkHover', 'tooltipShow', 'tooltipHide']) {
  gantt.on(name, () => seen.push(name));
}
let lag = null;
let linkType = null;
gantt.on('linkClick', (e) => { lag = e.lag; linkType = e.linkType; });

// The link's own click / double-click / context menu / hover.
const link = root.querySelector('.lat-gantt-split__linkgroup[data-from="a"][data-to="b"]');
link.dispatchEvent(new TestEvent('pointerover', { bubbles: true }));
link.dispatchEvent(new TestEvent('click', { bubbles: true }));
link.dispatchEvent(new TestEvent('dblclick', { bubbles: true, cancelable: true }));
link.dispatchEvent(new TestEvent('contextmenu', { bubbles: true, cancelable: true }));

// A right-click on the bar's row: taskContextMenu (no built-in menu configured here).
root.querySelector('.lat-gantt-split__barrow[data-task="a"]')
  .dispatchEvent(new TestEvent('contextmenu', { bubbles: true, cancelable: true }));

// The task tooltip: shown, hidden on pointerleave, then vetoed.
const row = () => root.querySelector('.lat-gantt-split__barrow[data-task="a"]');
const bar = () => row().querySelector('.lat-gantt-split__bar');
const tip = () => root.querySelector('.lat-gantt-split__tooltip');
bar().dispatchEvent(new TestEvent('pointermove', { bubbles: true, clientX: 10, clientY: 10 }));
const shown = !tip().hasAttribute('hidden');
root.querySelector('.lat-gantt-split__time-body').dispatchEvent(new TestEvent('pointerleave', {}));
const hiddenAfterLeave = tip().hasAttribute('hidden');
gantt.on('tooltipShow', (e) => e.preventDefault('replaced'));
bar().dispatchEvent(new TestEvent('pointermove', { bubbles: true, clientX: 10, clientY: 10 }));
const stillHidden = tip().hasAttribute('hidden');

view.destroy();
resetTestDom();
return `${seen.join(' ')} | ${linkType} lag ${lag} | tooltip ${shown},${hiddenAfterLeave},${stillHidden}`;

Previewing a drag (BACKLOG-0001934). While a bar is dragged or resized, both views redraw the dependency arrows into and out of it on every frame and shift the successors the scheduler will push. previewEdit({ id, start?, duration? }) is what they call: it dry-runs the edit and returns the schedule it would produce, without changing the model or emitting schedule, and returns null when the edit cannot be scheduled.

const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const plan = createGantt({
  projectStart: '2027-01-04',
  tasks: [{ id: 'A', duration: 5 }, { id: 'B', duration: 4 }],
  dependencies: [{ from: 'A', to: 'B', type: 'FS' }],
});
let events = 0;
plan.on('schedule', () => { events += 1; });

// Stretch A by two days: B would be pushed two days right.
const base = plan.schedule.tasks.get('B').es;
const preview = plan.previewEdit({ id: 'A', duration: 7 });
const out = `preview B +${preview.tasks.get('B').es - base} | live B +${plan.schedule.tasks.get('B').es - base} | events ${events}`;
plan.destroy();
return out;

Reordering and re-parenting rows (BACKLOG-0001937, BACKLOG-0001993). In the split view, a drag handle in the name column drags a row above or below a sibling to reorder it, or onto another row to make it a child; by keyboard, Alt+Up/Alt+Down move a focused row among its current siblings, and Tab/Shift+Tab indent or outdent it. Every gesture funnels through moveRow(id, { parent, index }), the same gated, undoable choke point: the cancellable beforeRowMove fires first — a handler may refuse with preventDefault(reason), which fires rowMove:cancelled and leaves the plan untouched — and once allowed, the task's parent and its position among the plan's own task order are written through the live re-parent path (BACKLOG-0001933) as one undo step, then rowMove fires once with { id, oldParent, newParent, oldIndex, newIndex }, so a host can store its own sort key. The same drop also raises the beforeRowDrop/rowDrop/rowDrop:cancelled trio — rowDrop is rowMove plus position ('before'/'after'/'inside') and target (the row the drop anchored to), the Lattice name for Bryntum's gridRowDrop; beforeRowDrop gates first, so a drop-specific refusal reports rowDrop:cancelled rather than rowMove:cancelled. A drop onto the task itself or onto one of its own descendants (which would cycle the tree) is refused before anything is gated; a move that changes neither parent nor position is a silent no-op.

const { createTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom();
const gantt = createGantt({
  projectStart: '2027-01-04',
  tasks: [
    { id: 'phase', name: 'Phase' },
    { id: 'A', name: 'A', parent: 'phase', duration: 2 },
    { id: 'B', name: 'B', parent: 'phase', duration: 2 },
    { id: 'C', name: 'C', parent: 'phase', duration: 2 },
  ],
});
const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, { keyboard: true });
const order = () => [...root.querySelectorAll('.lat-gantt-split__row')].map((r) => r.getAttribute('data-task'));

let moves = 0;
let drops = [];
gantt.on('rowMove', () => { moves += 1; });
gantt.on('rowDrop', (e) => { drops.push(`${e.position}/${e.target}`); });

// Alt+ArrowUp on C's row moves it up one, ahead of B — a plain sibling reorder,
// and the drop reports position 'before' the row it landed on.
const rowC = root.querySelector('.lat-gantt-split__row[data-task="C"]');
rowC.dispatchEvent({ type: 'keydown', target: rowC, bubbles: true, key: 'ArrowUp', altKey: true });
const afterAlt = order().join(',');

// Tab on B (now the last sibling) indents it under its new previous sibling, C
// — position 'inside' C.
const rowB = root.querySelector('.lat-gantt-split__row[data-task="B"]');
rowB.dispatchEvent({ type: 'keydown', target: rowB, bubbles: true, key: 'Tab' });
const bParent = gantt.schedule.tasks.get('B').parent;

// A vetoed drop changes nothing and fires rowDrop:cancelled instead of rowDrop.
gantt.on('beforeRowDrop', (e) => e.preventDefault('locked'));
let cancelled = false;
gantt.on('rowDrop:cancelled', () => { cancelled = true; });
gantt.moveRow('A', { parent: 'C' });

view.destroy();
return `order ${afterAlt} | B under ${bParent} | moves ${moves} | drops ${drops.join(' ')} | veto cancelled ${cancelled}`;

WBS outline codes (BACKLOG-0002008). Every scheduled task carries a derived wbs string — '1', '1.1', '1.2', '2', … — from its position in the tree, read off gantt.schedule.tasks.get(id).wbs; it renumbers on every reorder, indent/outdent, add or delete, with nothing written onto the task itself, so renumbering never adds an undo step of its own. gantt.taskByWbs(code) resolves a task by its current code — the lookup a predecessor reference by WBS (BACKLOG-0002007) will use. The built-in kind: 'wbs' split-view column shows it, read-only and sortable under the same tree-aware sort (BACKLOG-0001997) as every other column. An MSPDI task's own <WBS>, when the source plan supplied one, is kept verbatim by importMSPDI rather than overridden by the derived number, and exportMSPDI writes it back.

const { createGantt, importMSPDI, exportMSPDI } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const plan = createGantt({
  tasks: [
    { id: 'p', name: 'Phase 1' },
    { id: 'a', name: 'A', parent: 'p', duration: 2 },
    { id: 'b', name: 'B', parent: 'p', duration: 2 },
    { id: 'q', name: 'Phase 2', duration: 1 },
  ],
});
const codes = ['p', 'a', 'b', 'q'].map((id) => plan.schedule.tasks.get(id).wbs).join(',');

// Moving b above a renumbers both — nothing stored, so nothing stale to fix.
plan.moveRow('b', { parent: 'p', index: 0 });
const moved = plan.schedule.tasks.get('b').wbs;
const lookup = plan.taskByWbs('1.2').id;
plan.destroy();

// A supplied, non-standard WBS (Project lets a plan carry a custom mask) is
// kept verbatim on import and written back unchanged on export.
const xml = '<Project xmlns="http://schemas.microsoft.com/project"><Tasks>' +
  '<Task><UID>1</UID><Name>Alpha</Name><OutlineLevel>1</OutlineLevel><WBS>A</WBS></Task>' +
  '<Task><UID>2</UID><Name>Beta</Name><OutlineLevel>1</OutlineLevel><WBS>B</WBS></Task>' +
  '</Tasks></Project>';
const back = importMSPDI(xml);
const custom = back.tasks.map((t) => t.wbs).join(',');
const xmlOut = exportMSPDI({ tasks: back.tasks });
const roundTrips = xmlOut.includes('<WBS>A</WBS>') && xmlOut.includes('<WBS>B</WBS>');

return `${codes} | moved ${moved} | lookup ${lookup} | custom ${custom} | roundtrip ${roundTrips}`;

The task tooltip (BACKLOG-0001921). Hovering a bar, or focusing it by keyboard, in either view, shows a card anchored to it — above the bar by default, flipping below or sideways to stay inside the view, dismissed on pointer leave, blur, Escape or scroll, and suppressed mid-drag. The default content is the task name as a title, then labelled Start/End/Duration/Complete rows (dates in the grid's own locale), plus Effort, Assigned, Cost and Slack when the task has them; a milestone shows a single Date row instead. tooltip: { fields } picks and orders the rows from 'start', 'end', 'duration', 'percentComplete', 'effort', 'resources', 'cost', 'slack'; tooltip: (task, ctx) => ... renders your own string (always text) or DOM node (attached as it is), with ctx.formatDate the same locale formatter the default rows use; tooltip: false turns it off. The hovered or focused bar's aria-description always matches the card's own text exactly, whichever of the three produced it.

const { createTestDom, resetTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom();
const plan = createGantt({
  tasks: [
    { id: 'design', name: 'Design', duration: 5, percentComplete: 40, cost: 1200 },
    { id: 'ship', name: 'Ship', milestone: true },
  ],
});
// `fields` picks and orders the rows; a milestone always shows just its Date.
plan.mount(root, { dateAxis: false, tooltip: { fields: ['start', 'percentComplete', 'cost'] } });
const hover = (sel) => {
  const row = root.querySelector(sel);
  const bar = row.querySelector('.lat-gantt__bar') || row.querySelector('.lat-gantt__milestone');
  bar.dispatchEvent({ type: 'pointermove', target: bar, bubbles: true });
  return row.getAttribute('aria-description').replace(/\n/g, ' / ');
};
const picked = hover('.lat-gantt__row[data-task="design"]');
const milestone = hover('.lat-gantt__row[data-task="ship"]');
plan.destroy();

// A host render function: a plain string is always text, never markup.
const { root: root2 } = createTestDom();
const custom = createGantt({ tasks: [{ id: 'a', name: 'Design', duration: 5 }] });
custom.mount(root2, { dateAxis: false, tooltip: (task, ctx) => `${task.name}: ${ctx.formatDate(task.es)}` });
const row2 = root2.querySelector('.lat-gantt__row[data-task="a"]');
const bar2 = row2.querySelector('.lat-gantt__bar');
bar2.dispatchEvent({ type: 'pointermove', target: bar2, bubbles: true });
const customText = row2.getAttribute('aria-description');
custom.destroy();
resetTestDom();

return `picked [${picked}] | milestone [${milestone}] | custom [${customText}]`;

Per-task bar colour (BACKLOG-0001944). A task's colour field paints its bar, summary bar or milestone, and the bar's progress fill is the same hue darkened so the completed part still reads against it; the barColour(task) option on mount/mountSplit wins over the field. The colour is a data colour — written literally, not through a --lattice-* token — so it reads the same in dark mode, and it is re-applied on every redraw.

const { createTestDom, resetTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

// A task `colour` field paints its bar and its milestone; the progress fill is
// the same hue darkened.
const { root } = createTestDom();
const plan = createGantt({
  tasks: [
    { id: 'design', name: 'Design', duration: 5, percentComplete: 40, colour: '#3366cc' },
    { id: 'ship', name: 'Ship', milestone: true, colour: '#cc3333' },
  ],
});
plan.mount(root, { dateAxis: false });
const designRow = root.querySelector('.lat-gantt__row[data-task="design"]');
const shipRow = root.querySelector('.lat-gantt__row[data-task="ship"]');
const fills = `${designRow.querySelector('.lat-gantt__bar').style.fill} ${designRow.querySelector('.lat-gantt__progress').style.fill} ${shipRow.querySelector('.lat-gantt__milestone').style.fill}`;
plan.destroy();

// `barColour(task)` wins over the field.
const { root: root2 } = createTestDom();
const plan2 = createGantt({ tasks: [{ id: 'a', duration: 3, colour: '#3366cc' }] });
plan2.mount(root2, { dateAxis: false, barColour: (task) => (task.id === 'a' ? '#00aa00' : null) });
const overridden = root2.querySelector('.lat-gantt__row[data-task="a"]').querySelector('.lat-gantt__bar').style.fill;
plan2.destroy();
resetTestDom();

return `${fills} | ${overridden}`;

Bar label position (BACKLOG-0001952). The split view's barLabelPosition — 'inside', 'right' or 'auto' — says where a task or summary bar's own label sits. 'right' (the default) keeps the label just past the bar's right edge, the behaviour the view shipped with; 'inside' centres it on the bar and swaps it to a contrasting fill so it stays readable over the solid bar in light and dark themes alike — a per-task colour picks its own text colour (near-black on a light fill, white on a dark one) because the token contrast is only guaranteed against the theme's own fills, not an arbitrary data colour; 'auto' centres the label only when it fits, estimating its width the same way the axis labels are thinned, and falls back to the right otherwise. A label too wide to read inside its bar falls back to the right under either 'inside' or 'auto', so it is never cut off at the bar's edges. Milestones draw no bar and so no label.

const { createTestDom, resetTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const NS = 'lat-gantt-split';
const tasks = [
  { id: 'go', name: 'Go', duration: 8 },
  { id: 'long', name: 'A very long task name that will not fit inside a narrow bar', duration: 1 },
];
const px = (v) => Number(String(v || '0'));

// `inside` centres the label on the bar and marks it with the contrasting fill.
const { root } = createTestDom();
const inside = createGantt({ projectStart: '2027-01-04', tasks });
inside.mountSplit(root, { barLabelPosition: 'inside' });
const goRow = root.querySelector(`.${NS}__barrow[data-task="go"]`);
const goBar = goRow.querySelector(`.${NS}__bar`);
const goLabel = goRow.querySelector(`.${NS}__barlabel`);
const centred = goLabel.getAttribute('data-inside') === 'true' && goLabel.getAttribute('text-anchor') === 'middle'
  && Math.abs(px(goLabel.getAttribute('x')) - (px(goBar.getAttribute('x')) + px(goBar.getAttribute('width')) / 2)) < 2;
inside.destroy();

// `auto` keeps a short label inside and pushes a too-wide one to the right.
const { root: root2 } = createTestDom();
const auto = createGantt({ projectStart: '2027-01-04', tasks });
auto.mountSplit(root2, { barLabelPosition: 'auto' });
const goAutoRow = root2.querySelector(`.${NS}__barrow[data-task="go"]`);
const longAutoRow = root2.querySelector(`.${NS}__barrow[data-task="long"]`);
const autoInside = goAutoRow.querySelector(`.${NS}__barlabel`).getAttribute('data-inside') === 'true';
const autoRight = longAutoRow.querySelector(`.${NS}__barlabel`).getAttribute('data-inside') === null;
auto.destroy();
resetTestDom();

return `inside centred ${centred} | auto short ${autoInside} long ${autoRight}`;
FunctionWhat it does
createGantt({ tasks?, dependencies?, projectStart?, autoSchedule?, honourDates?, grid?, fields?, rowKey? })Create a controller over a task list and a dependency list. Computes the CPM schedule immediately and on every edit; on('schedule'|'error', fn) subscribes; applyEdit/setTasks/setDependencies mutate and recompute; grid is kept for the write-back binding. honourDates derives each link's lag from the stored task dates so a loaded plan recomputes to those dates (BACKLOG-0001948). fields maps your own task property names onto the ones the scheduler reads, and rowKey identifies a task for both rows.apply and the schedule.
rows.apply(change) / rows.forEach(fn) / rows.countThe live consumer surface, the same keyed diff a grid, a board and a KPI panel accept, so one feed — or one tab strip — drives them all: apply({ add, update, remove }) upserts by rowKey and recomputes, forEach visits every task, and count is how many the plan holds.
computeSchedule(tasks, deps?, { projectStart? })The pure CPM engine: forward/backward passes over the leaf tasks honouring FS/SS/FF/SF + lag, slack/float and the zero-float critical path, with summaries derived and cycles refused. Returns { ok, tasks, critical, criticalPaths, projectDuration, ... } or { ok:false, error }.
findViolations(tasks, schedule)The tasks whose user-placed start begins earlier than CPM allows (the manual-with-validation flag). Summaries, whose dates are derived, are skipped.
toISODate(day)Format an engine day-number as an ISO calendar date (YYYY-MM-DD, UTC).
LINK_TYPESThe four dependency link types, in order: ['FS','SS','FF','SF'].
CONSTRAINT_TYPESThe eight scheduling constraint codes, in MS Project order: ['ASAP','MSO','MFO','ALAP','SNET','SNLT','FNET','FNLT']. A task's constraint takes any of them.
SCHEDULE_ERRORThe error codes the engine reports rather than throwing (cycle, duplicate-id, unknown-task, bad-duration, bad-link-type, unknown-parent, parent-cycle, …).
importMSPDI(xml, { hoursPerDay? })Import a Microsoft Project (MSPDI) .xml document into a { tasks, dependencies, resources, projectStart, calendar } model ready for createGantt: the task tree, typed dependencies with lag, constraints, baseline, %complete, resources with capacity and the resource assignments.
exportMSPDI(model, { hoursPerDay?, projectName? })Serialise a Gantt model (optionally a scheduled one) back to Microsoft Project (MSPDI) XML — the same fields, round-tripping with importMSPDI. The controller offers gantt.toMSPDI() as a shortcut over the current plan.
computeEarnedValue(tasks, schedule, { statusDate?, costField?, actualCostField? })Earned-value management (EVM) from the baseline and %complete at a status date: Planned Value (PV/BCWS), Earned Value (EV/BCWP), Actual Cost (AC/ACWP, from a per-task actualCost), plus Schedule Variance (EV−PV), Cost Variance (EV−AC), SPI (EV/PV) and CPI (EV/AC) — per task, rolled up to summaries and the project. Budget (BAC) is the task's cost, or its duration when no cost is given. The controller exposes gantt.earnedValue({ statusDate }) as the shortcut; the split view surfaces the metrics through kind: 'evm' columns.
getState() / setState(snapshot)Serialise and restore the Gantt's own VIEWER state (BACKLOG-0001042) — which view is mounted ('plain'/'split'/null), zoom, the arrow/progress/baseline toggles, calendar/non-working shading, the plain view's swimlane grouping, the split view's left-panel width and its collapsed summary rows. Not the task data (already covered by setTasks/rows.apply). Versioned, JSON-safe, and setState tolerates an unknown, wrong-typed or newer-version snapshot without throwing, applying only what it recognises.
 
const { computeSchedule, findViolations, createGantt, LINK_TYPES, SCHEDULE_ERROR, toISODate } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');
const tasks = [
  { id: 'design', duration: 5, percentComplete: 100 },
  { id: 'build', duration: 6, percentComplete: 50 },
  { id: 'test', duration: 4 },
  { id: 'launch', milestone: true },
];
const deps = [
  { from: 'design', to: 'build' },
  { from: 'build', to: 'test' },
  { from: 'test', to: 'launch' },
];
const s = computeSchedule(tasks, deps);
const plan = createGantt({ tasks, dependencies: deps });
const cyc = computeSchedule([{ id: 'a', duration: 1 }, { id: 'b', duration: 1 }], [{ from: 'a', to: 'b' }, { from: 'b', to: 'a' }]);
return [plan.schedule.projectDuration, s.critical.join('-'), toISODate(0), LINK_TYPES.join(','), cyc.error.code === SCHEDULE_ERROR.CYCLE, findViolations(tasks, s).length].join(' | ');

Milestones and summary (WBS) tasks: a summary is derived from its children and a dependency may target it.

const { computeSchedule } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');
const tasks = [
  { id: 'phase', name: 'Phase 1' },
  { id: 'a', duration: 3, parent: 'phase', percentComplete: 100 },
  { id: 'b', duration: 2, parent: 'phase', percentComplete: 0 },
  { id: 'ship', milestone: true },
];
const s = computeSchedule(tasks, [{ from: 'a', to: 'b' }, { from: 'phase', to: 'ship' }]);
const phase = s.tasks.get('phase');
return [phase.es, phase.ef, phase.percentComplete, phase.isSummary, s.tasks.get('ship').es].join(' | ');

Locked tasks and hard constraints (BACKLOG-0001954). A dated constraint like MSO is reported as a conflict when a predecessor would force it later, but the date still floats to wherever the predecessor pushed it — a completed task's own placement is not safe from a late-running predecessor. locked: true (or the equivalent constraintMode: 'hard') is the stronger guarantee: the scheduler never moves the task, full stop. A violating predecessor link is instead left unhonoured and reported in schedule.conflicts as a 'LOCKED' conflict naming the exact from/to edge, which a mounted view draws as a conflict arrow; dragging the locked task itself is refused by default. The plan-wide lockWhen(task) predicate (on createGantt and computeSchedule) locks by rule — typically "100% complete" — without setting locked on every finished task by hand. The same rule is exported as isTaskLocked(task, lockWhen) for a host that needs to ask before offering a drag or an edit: it reads locked, constraintMode: 'hard' and the plan's lockWhen predicate off the raw task, and a predicate that throws locks nothing.

const { computeSchedule, createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');
const deps = [{ from: 'design', to: 'build', type: 'FS' }];
// `design` now overruns into what was `build`'s placed window.
const locked = computeSchedule(
  [{ id: 'design', duration: 10 }, { id: 'build', duration: 4, locked: true, start: 5 }],
  deps,
);
const hard = computeSchedule(
  [{ id: 'design', duration: 10 }, { id: 'build', duration: 4, constraintMode: 'hard', start: 5 }],
  deps,
);
const byRule = computeSchedule(
  [{ id: 'design', duration: 10 }, { id: 'build', duration: 4, percentComplete: 100, start: 5 }],
  deps,
  { lockWhen: (t) => t.percentComplete === 100 },
);
const plan = createGantt({ tasks: [{ id: 'build', duration: 4, locked: true, start: 5 }] });
const refused = plan.applyEdit({ id: 'build', start: 20 }); // beforeTaskMove refuses a locked task by default
return [
  locked.tasks.get('build').es, locked.conflicts[0].type, locked.conflicts[0].from,
  hard.tasks.get('build').es, byRule.tasks.get('build').es, refused === undefined,
].join(' | ');

Read-only tasks (BACKLOG-0002011). task.readOnly: true (or the plan-wide readOnlyWhen(task) option on createGantt) refuses every user-driven edit on the task — pointer and keyboard drag, resize, progress drag, a dependency create/delete touching it, and a task-table cell edit on it — before the gesture's own before* event ever fires: the paired :cancelled event fires with reason 'readOnly', but no host handler is ever asked to veto an attempt that was never going to happen. It is a weaker promise than locked in two ways: the scheduler still pushes a read-only task's dates when a predecessor moves (unless the task is also locked), and applyEdit/setDependencies/deleteDependency called directly from code — carrying no originating DOM event — still work, because readOnly governs interactive editing, not the API. A read-only row and bar carry a --readonly class (a muted progress handle, no drag cursor) and an accessible description, in both the plain and split view.

const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');
const plan = createGantt({
  tasks: [{ id: 'design', duration: 5 }, { id: 'build', duration: 4, readOnly: true }],
  dependencies: [{ from: 'design', to: 'build', type: 'FS' }],
});
let beforeFired = false;
plan.on('beforeTaskMove', () => { beforeFired = true; });
let reason = null;
plan.on('taskMove:cancelled', (e) => { reason = e.reason; });
// A UI-driven edit (one carrying an originating event, as a gesture commit or a
// task-table cell edit does) refuses before beforeTaskMove ever fires.
const refused = plan.applyEdit({ id: 'build', start: 1 }, { event: {} });
const pushedStart = plan.schedule.tasks.get('build').es;
// The scheduler still pushes the read-only task when its predecessor moves.
plan.applyEdit({ id: 'design', duration: 8 });
const pushedAgain = plan.schedule.tasks.get('build').es;
// A bare applyEdit call from the host (no originating event) still works.
const apiEdit = plan.applyEdit({ id: 'build', duration: 2 });
return [
  refused === undefined, beforeFired, reason,
  pushedStart, pushedAgain, apiEdit.ok, plan.schedule.tasks.get('build').duration,
].join(' | ');

Extra task-table columns (BACKLOG-0002010). The split view’s columns list also takes constraint (an editable select over the constraint types, with catalogue labels), constraintDate (an editable date), the read-only earlyStart, lateStart, lateFinish, baselineStart and baselineEnd dates, variance (finish variance in days, signed, a late value drawn in the warning token), note (editable text) and sequence (the 1-based row number, renumbered as the table is sorted or filtered). Each is an ordinary grid column definition, so a host overrides it by id, the dates honour dateFormat, every one exports with the table, and the editable ones commit through applyEdit — one undo step, the usual before* vetoes.

const { createTestDom, flushFrames } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGrid } = await import('../packages/dom/src/index.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom({ width: 1200, height: 300 });
const gantt = createGantt({
  projectStart: '2027-01-04',
  tasks: [
    { id: 'a', name: 'Design', duration: 3, baselineStart: '2027-01-04', baselineEnd: '2027-01-05' },
    { id: 'b', name: 'Build', duration: 2, note: 'after design' },
  ],
  dependencies: [{ from: 'a', to: 'b' }],
});
const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, {
  createGrid, editable: true,
  columns: ['name', 'constraint', 'constraintDate', 'variance', 'sequence'],
});
flushFrames();
const grid = view.grids.tasks;
// A constraint and its date commit through applyEdit and reschedule the plan.
await grid.edit.setCells([{ key: 'b', colId: 'constraint', value: 'SNET' }], 'cell', { origin: 'user' });
await grid.edit.setCells([{ key: 'b', colId: 'constraintDate', value: '2027-01-11' }], 'cell', { origin: 'user' });
flushFrames();
await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 5));
const out = [
  gantt.tasks[1].constraint, gantt.tasks[1].constraintDate,
  grid.rows.text('a', 'variance'), grid.rows.text('b', 'sequence'),
  (gantt.undo(), gantt.tasks[1].constraintDate ?? 'none'), // one undo step reverts the date edit
].join(' | ');
view.destroy();
return out;

Honouring stored dates (BACKLOG-0001948). A host migrating an existing plan already has every task's dates, but the links may carry a wrong or absent lag. With honourDates: true the controller derives each link's lag from its two endpoints' start/duration on setTasks/setDependencies, so the computed schedule reproduces the stored dates and nothing moves; the derived lags are reflected in gantt.dependencies.

const { createGantt, computeSchedule } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

// Stored dates, and a link whose lag is wrong — 5, where the dates imply 1.
const tasks = [
  { id: 'design', start: 0, duration: 5 },
  { id: 'build', start: 6, duration: 4 },
];
const deps = [{ from: 'design', to: 'build', type: 'FS', lag: 5 }];

// Without honourDates the CPM obeys the wrong lag and pushes build to day 10.
const plain = createGantt({ tasks, dependencies: deps });
const moved = plain.schedule.tasks.get('build').es;
plain.destroy();

// With honourDates the lag is re-derived from the dates, so build stays on 6.
const plan = createGantt({ honourDates: true, tasks, dependencies: deps });
const kept = plan.schedule.tasks.get('build').es;
const lag = plan.dependencies[0].lag;
plan.destroy();

// computeSchedule accepts the option directly and returns the derived deps.
const direct = computeSchedule(tasks, deps, { honourDates: true });
if (direct.dependencies[0].lag !== 1) throw new Error('lag not derived');

return `moved ${moved}, kept ${kept}, lag ${lag}`;

Undo and redo (BACKLOG-0001864). Every user or API edit — a move, resize, delete, link add/remove/lag, assignment or units change, a workload-band hour edit, a baseline applied to the plan, a task add through rows.apply, even a whole level() run — is one undo step, because they all funnel through the same controller mutators the recorder sits on. undo() restores the exact prior plan (tasks, dependencies, assignments and work contours) and re-runs scheduling to the identical result; redo() re-applies it. canUndo()/canRedo() drive a toolbar, and a history event { canUndo, canRedo, label } fires on every change. In a mounted view Ctrl/Cmd+Z undoes and Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+Z (or Ctrl+Y) redoes, scoped to the focused Gantt — and a Ctrl+Z typed into the split table's open cell editor is left to the grid's own text undo, never hijacked. History is session only: kept while the page is open, cleared by setTasks/setState and on reload, and NOT part of getState/setState. The depth is historyDepth (default 100), and clearHistory() empties it.

const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');
const plan = createGantt({
  tasks: [{ id: 'design', duration: 5 }, { id: 'build', duration: 4 }],
  dependencies: [{ from: 'design', to: 'build', type: 'FS' }],
});
plan.applyEdit({ id: 'design', start: 3 });         // one edit = one undo step
const moved = plan.schedule.tasks.get('build').es;  // 8 — build (FS successor) followed
plan.undo();                                        // restore the exact prior plan + schedule
const undone = plan.schedule.tasks.get('build').es; // back to 5
plan.redo();                                         // and forward again
const redone = plan.schedule.tasks.get('build').es; // 8
// History is session only: clearing it (as setTasks/setState also do) empties both stacks.
plan.clearHistory();
return [moved, undone, redone, plan.canUndo(), plan.canRedo()].join(' | ');

Dirty tracking (BACKLOG-0002003). gantt.changes() is the accumulated diff since the last commit() or load — { tasks: { added, updated, removed }, dependencies: { added, updated, removed }, assignments: { updated } } — and hasChanges is the boolean a Save button binds to. Both are computed fresh against a held committed baseline rather than an edit counter, so an add immediately removed nets to nothing and undoing back to the baseline clears hasChanges exactly as a fresh commit() would. The dirtyChange event { hasChanges } fires only the instant the flag flips, never on a second edit made while already dirty. commit() takes the plan as it stands now as the new baseline (leaving undo/redo untouched); setTasks/rows.apply reset the baseline to the load unless called with { keepChanges: true }, which keeps the load accumulating against whatever baseline was already held — the shape a live feed wants if its own upserts should still count as unsaved changes. mountSplit's dirtyMarks: true draws a small corner mark on every built-in task-table cell whose field is part of the diff, cleared the moment it is not.

const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');
const plan = createGantt({ tasks: [{ id: 'a', name: 'A', duration: 5 }] });
const flips = [];
plan.on('dirtyChange', (e) => flips.push(e.hasChanges));

plan.applyEdit({ id: 'a', name: 'A renamed' });
const dirtyAfterEdit = plan.hasChanges;                          // true
const fields = Object.keys(plan.changes().tasks.updated[0].fields); // ['name']

plan.commit();                                                   // the rename is now the baseline
const dirtyAfterCommit = plan.hasChanges;                        // false

// A live-feed upsert that should still count as an unsaved change joins the
// baseline already held rather than resetting to it.
plan.rows.apply({ add: [{ id: 'b', name: 'B', duration: 2 }] }, { keepChanges: true });
const added = plan.changes().tasks.added.length;                 // 1

// ...and removing the same row nets back to nothing, diffed fresh rather
// than counted.
plan.rows.apply({ remove: ['b'] }, { keepChanges: true });
const nets = plan.hasChanges;                                     // false

plan.destroy();
return [dirtyAfterEdit, fields.join(','), dirtyAfterCommit, added, nets, flips.join(',')].join(' | ');

Resource management. Assign people (or machines) to tasks with assignee/assignees or explicit assignments: [{ resource, units }], and give each resource a capacity through the resources option. The controller then reports overAllocations — where one resource is booked beyond its capacity across concurrent tasks — and the full resourceLoad; the split view rings the over-booked avatars and outlines the clashing bars. gantt.level() auto-shifts the lower-priority tasks later to clear the over-allocation, honouring the critical path and the working-time calendar (pass { dryRun: true } to preview the moves).

Per-resource working calendars. A resource may carry its own calendar — workingDays (0=Sun…6=Sat), hoursPerDay (a flat number or a per-weekday map like { 1: 7.5, 5: 4 }), and holidays/leave (ISO dates, day-numbers or inclusive { from, to } ranges) — and a capacity in hours per day (BACKLOG-0001861). Load, the workload band, the over-allocation marks and level() then measure that resource against ITS calendar: it has zero capacity on a day it does not work (so a booking there is wholly over-allocation), and level() never delays a task onto one of its non-working days. Anything the resource calendar omits is inherited from the plan calendar, and a resource with no calendar keeps the historical unitless capacity (1 = a full-time booking, a fraction of the plan's hoursPerDay), so a plan with no resource calendars is unchanged. The plan's full working day is the hoursPerDay option (default 8). Resource calendars round-trip through MSPDI as a Resource → CalendarUID → Calendar.

const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');
const plan = createGantt({
  projectStart: '2027-01-04',   // a Monday
  calendar: 'weekends',
  // Pat works Mon/Tue/Wed at 7.5 h; the plan's full day is 8 h.
  resources: [{ id: 'Pat', calendar: { workingDays: [1, 2, 3], hoursPerDay: 7.5 } }],
  tasks: [{ id: 'T', duration: 5, assignee: 'Pat' }],
});
const load = plan.resources();
const over = load.overAllocations;
// Mon–Wed: over by the half hour Pat cannot cover (capacity 7.5/8 = 0.9375 u).
// Thu–Fri: Pat does not work, so capacity is 0 and the whole booking is over.
return [over.length, over[0].capacity, over[1].capacity, load.byResource.get('Pat').capacity].join(' | ');

Per-task and per-resource calendars (BACKLOG-0002009). A task's calendar takes the plan calendar's shape (workdays, holidays or exceptions, hoursPerDay) or the id of an entry in the calendars: { id: spec } option; a resource's calendar likewise. The scheduler counts that task's durations and lags in its own working days, so five days on a seven-day calendar span five calendar days where the Monday–Friday plan spans seven. The split view shades each bar by its own calendar, the workload band and level() book the days that calendar works, schedule.tasks.get(id).calendar reports the resolved id ('plan' when the task follows the plan), and MSPDI export and import round-trip the calendars. An id that names no calendar refuses the schedule with an unknown-calendar entry in errors that names the task (or resource) and the id.

const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');
const { computeSchedule } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/schedule.js');
const plan = createGantt({
  projectStart: '2027-01-04',   // a Monday
  calendar: 'weekends',
  calendars: { seven: { workdays: [0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6] } },
  tasks: [
    { id: 'office', duration: 5 },
    { id: 'site', duration: 5, calendar: 'seven' },
  ],
});
const t = plan.schedule.tasks;
const span = (id) => t.get(id).ef - t.get(id).es;
const refused = computeSchedule([{ id: 'x', duration: 1, calendar: 'night-shift' }], [], { projectStart: '2027-01-04' });
return [span('office'), span('site'), t.get('site').calendar, t.get('office').calendar,
  refused.errors[0].code + ' ' + refused.errors[0].calendar].join(' | ');

Resource avatars (BACKLOG-0001977). A resource may carry an avatar image URL; the split view draws it as a round chip in the assignee column, the workload band's resource rows and the assignment picker, sized by the --lattice-gantt-avatar-size theme token (defaulting to the row height). The picture's alt text is the resource's name, and a resource without a picture — or whose image fails to load — keeps its initials glyph, so nothing shifts while a picture loads.

const { createTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');
const { root } = createTestDom();
const PICTURE = 'data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODlhAQABAIAAAAAAAP///yH5BAEAAAAALAAAAAABAAEAAAIBRAA7';
const plan = createGantt({
  projectStart: '2027-01-04',
  // Ali carries an avatar (a data URL, so the example runs offline); Bo has
  // none and keeps her initials.
  resources: [
    { id: 'ann', name: 'Ali Mohsin', avatar: PICTURE },
    { id: 'bo', name: 'Bo Chen' },
  ],
  tasks: [
    { id: 'a', name: 'Design', start: '2027-01-04', duration: 2, assignee: 'ann' },
    { id: 'b', name: 'Build', start: '2027-01-04', duration: 2, assignee: 'bo' },
  ],
});
const view = plan.mountSplit(root, { columns: ['name', 'assignee'], workload: true });
const NS = 'lat-gantt-split';
// The assignee column's first picture is Ali's, alt = her name; the band draws
// the same chip per resource, and Bo's row keeps her initials.
const assigneeAlt = root.querySelector('.' + NS + '__avatar-img').getAttribute('alt');
const band = [...root.querySelectorAll('.' + NS + '__wl-row')]
  .map((row) => {
    const img = row.querySelector('.' + NS + '__avatar-img');
    const glyph = row.querySelector('.' + NS + '__avatar-glyph');
    return img ? img.getAttribute('alt') : (glyph ? glyph.getAttribute('data-initials') : null);
  })
  .filter(Boolean);
view.destroy();
plan.destroy();
return `${assigneeAlt} | band ${band.join('+')}`;

Microsoft Project. importMSPDI reads a Project .xml (MSPDI) document into a model for createGantt, and exportMSPDI (or gantt.toMSPDI()) writes one back — tasks, typed dependencies with lag, constraints, baseline, %complete, resources (including per-resource working calendars) and assignments all round-trip.

const { createGantt, exportMSPDI, importMSPDI } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');
const plan = createGantt({
  tasks: [
    { id: '1', name: 'Design', duration: 5, assignee: 'Ada' },
    { id: '2', name: 'Build', duration: 3, assignee: 'Ada' },
  ],
});
const overBefore = plan.overAllocations.length;   // Ada is double-booked
plan.level();                                      // shift one task later
const overAfter = plan.overAllocations.length;     // now clear
const xml = exportMSPDI({ tasks: plan.tasks, dependencies: plan.dependencies, schedule: plan.schedule });
const back = importMSPDI(xml);
return [overBefore, overAfter, back.ok, back.tasks.length, xml.startsWith('<?xml')].join(' | ');

Earned-value analytics. With a captured baseline (captureBaseline) and per-task percentComplete, gantt.earnedValue({ statusDate }) (or the standalone computeEarnedValue(tasks, schedule, { statusDate })) reports EVM at a status date — Planned Value (PV/BCWS) from the baseline, Earned Value (EV/BCWP) from %complete, Actual Cost (AC/ACWP) from a per-task actualCost — and the derived Schedule Variance (EV−PV), Cost Variance (EV−AC), SPI (EV/PV) and CPI (EV/AC), per task and rolled up to summaries and the project. Give each task a cost for money-based EVM, or omit it for schedule-only EVM off the durations. The split view surfaces any metric through kind: 'evm' columns. This worked example reproduces the classic four-side fence: at the end of day 3, three sides are planned (PV 3000) but two are done (EV 2000) at a cost of 2500 (AC).

const { createGantt, computeEarnedValue } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');
const tasks = [
  { id: 's1', name: 'Side 1', duration: 1, cost: 1000, percentComplete: 100, actualCost: 1250, baselineStart: 0, baselineEnd: 1 },
  { id: 's2', name: 'Side 2', duration: 1, cost: 1000, percentComplete: 100, actualCost: 1250, baselineStart: 1, baselineEnd: 2 },
  { id: 's3', name: 'Side 3', duration: 1, cost: 1000, percentComplete: 0, baselineStart: 2, baselineEnd: 3 },
  { id: 's4', name: 'Side 4', duration: 1, cost: 1000, percentComplete: 0, baselineStart: 3, baselineEnd: 4 },
];
const deps = [{ from: 's1', to: 's2' }, { from: 's2', to: 's3' }, { from: 's3', to: 's4' }];
const plan = createGantt({ tasks, dependencies: deps, projectStart: 0 });
const evm = plan.earnedValue({ statusDate: 3 });                          // via the controller
const direct = computeEarnedValue(tasks, plan.schedule, { statusDate: 3 }); // or standalone
const p = evm.project;
return [p.pv, p.ev, p.ac, p.sv, p.cv, p.spi.toFixed(2), p.cpi.toFixed(2), direct.project.ev].join(' | ');

S-curve (BACKLOG-0001867). gantt.sCurve({ statusDate, bucket }) turns the same earned-value arithmetic into a cumulative curve — one { date, pv, ev, ac } point per bucket from the project start to the finish, with the final PV equal to the total budget; gantt.mountSCurve(container, opts) draws that curve (three lines, a status-date marker, a legend and a hidden table) and hands back a handle whose refresh() redraws it and destroy() detaches it.

const { createGantt, computeSCurve } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');
const tasks = [
  { id: 'a', duration: 2, cost: 100, percentComplete: 100 },
  { id: 'b', duration: 2, cost: 200, percentComplete: 50 },
  { id: 'c', duration: 2, cost: 300, percentComplete: 0 },
];
const plan = createGantt({ projectStart: 0, tasks });
const curve = plan.sCurve({ statusDate: 2, bucket: 'day' });                       // via the controller
const direct = computeSCurve(tasks, plan.schedule, { statusDate: 2, bucket: 'day' }); // or standalone
const last = curve[curve.length - 1];
// Two day buckets; the last point lands on the finish with the whole 600 budget planned.
return [curve.length, last.pv, last.date, direct.length, direct[direct.length - 1].pv].join(' | ');
const { createTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');
const { root } = createTestDom({ width: 640, height: 240 });
const plan = createGantt({
  projectStart: 0,
  tasks: [
    { id: 'a', duration: 3, cost: 100, percentComplete: 100, actualCost: 110 },
    { id: 'b', duration: 3, cost: 100, percentComplete: 50, actualCost: 40 },
  ],
});
const handle = plan.mountSCurve(root, { statusDate: 2, bucket: 'day', width: 400, height: 200 });
// The handle detaches the chart; the chart draws the three series and the status marker.
const series = [...root.querySelectorAll('.lat-gantt-scurve__series')].map((s) => s.getAttribute('data-series'));
const out = [typeof handle.destroy, typeof handle.refresh, series.join(','), root.querySelector('.lat-gantt-scurve__status') != null].join(' | ');
handle.destroy();
plan.destroy();
return out;

Resource-rate cost (BACKLOG-0001866).
