A resource may carry an hourly rate, dated rates: [{ from, rate }] changes, and a type ('work' default, 'material' cost per assignment unit, 'cost' a flat amount per assignment). gantt.cost() (or the standalone computeCost(tasks, schedule, { resources })) prices every task as Σ assignment effort × the rate in force each day, plus the task's own fixed cost, rolled up to summaries and the project — a task with no explicit cost and no rated assignment costs nothing, keeping a plan with no rate data unaffected. earnedValue() reads this same rate-derived figure as its BAC when a task carries no explicit cost. Ann is $50/h through day 2, then $60/h from day 2 on; task "a" (days 0–1) costs 16h at the old rate, task "b" (day 0 only, since it has no dependency on "a") costs 8h at the same old rate too.

const { createGantt, computeCost } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');
const tasks = [
  { id: 'a', duration: 2, assignee: 'Ann' },
  { id: 'b', duration: 1, assignee: 'Ann' },
];
const resources = [{ id: 'Ann', rate: 50, rates: [{ from: 2, rate: 60 }] }];
const plan = createGantt({ projectStart: 0, tasks, resources });
const cost = plan.cost();                                            // via the controller
const direct = computeCost(tasks, plan.schedule, { resources });     // or standalone
const evm = plan.earnedValue();                                      // EV's BAC falls back to this same figure
return [cost.byTask.get('a').cost, cost.byTask.get('b').cost, cost.project.cost, direct.project.cost, evm.byTask.get('a').bac].join(' | ');

Render the plan as an SVG timeline with mount(container, options) — bars on a time scale, dependency arrows with a per-link-type anchor and a lag/lead label, the critical path highlighted, a today line, optional non-working-day shading, milestones as diamonds, a progress bar-fill and configurable labels. The view redraws itself whenever the schedule recomputes; unmount() detaches it. All geometry is computed from the schedule, so it draws identically headless or in a browser.

import { createGantt } from '@toclocoinc/lattice-grid/modules/gantt';

const gantt = createGantt({ tasks, dependencies });
gantt.mount(document.querySelector('#plan'), {
  today: '2026-03-06',      // a day-number, ISO date or Date; draws the today line
  nonWorking: 'weekends',   // shade Saturdays and Sundays
  label: 'percent',         // bar label: 'name' | 'percent' | 'dates' | (task) => string
  dateAxis: true,           // axis ticks as calendar dates
  zoom: 'week',             // 'day' | 'week' | 'month' | 'quarter' | pixels-per-day; omit to fit width
  scrollToToday: true,      // scroll so the today line is in view
  groupBy: 'assignee',      // swimlanes by a task property or (task) => key
  rowHeight: 26,
  width: 'container',       // the default: fill the container, and keep following it
  markers: [                // vertical date markers (BACKLOG-0001943)
    { date: '2026-03-09', label: 'Freeze', colour: '#e2513b', dashed: true },
    { from: '2026-03-11', to: '2026-03-13', label: 'Review' },
  ],
});

Vertical date markers (BACKLOG-0001943). A markers array on mount or mountSplit draws display-only annotations through the whole plot: each { date, label } is a full-height line with its label in the time header, a { from, to } entry shades the range between the two dates with an edge line at each end, and colour/dashed restyle the line. Dates are day-numbers, ISO date strings or Dates; in the plain view a string/Date is rebased through projectEpoch like today. Markers are redrawn at every zoom and never touch the schedule; a marker that cannot resolve is skipped with a warning, and the rest of the plan still draws.

const { createTestDom, resetTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

// The plain view: a dashed, coloured line at a date and a shaded range.
const { root } = createTestDom();
const plain = createGantt({ tasks: [{ id: 'a', duration: 8 }] });
plain.mount(root, {
  markers: [
    { date: 2, label: 'Freeze', colour: '#e2513b', dashed: true },
    { from: 5, to: 7, label: 'Review' },
  ],
});
const line = root.querySelector('.lat-gantt__marker');
const plainOut = {
  lines: root.querySelectorAll('.lat-gantt__marker').length,
  labels: [...root.querySelectorAll('.lat-gantt__marker-label')].map((l) => l.textContent).join('+'),
  ranges: root.querySelectorAll('.lat-gantt__marker-range').length,
  dash: line.getAttribute('stroke-dasharray'),
  colour: line.getAttribute('stroke'),
};
plain.destroy();

// The split view draws the same markers, labels in its own time header.
const { root: root2 } = createTestDom();
const split = createGantt({ tasks: [{ id: 'a', duration: 8 }] });
split.mountSplit(root2, {
  markers: [{ date: 2, label: 'Freeze' }, { from: 5, to: 7, label: 'Review' }],
});
const splitOut = {
  lines: root2.querySelectorAll('.lat-gantt-split__marker').length,
  labels: [...root2.querySelectorAll('.lat-gantt-split__marker-label')].map((l) => l.textContent).join('+'),
  ranges: root2.querySelectorAll('.lat-gantt-split__marker-range').length,
};
split.destroy();
resetTestDom();

return `plain ${plainOut.lines} lines, ${plainOut.labels}, ${plainOut.ranges} range, ${plainOut.dash} ${plainOut.colour}; split ${splitOut.lines} lines, ${splitOut.labels}, ${splitOut.ranges} range`;

Sizing (BACKLOG-0001079). width defaults to 'container': the view measures the box it was mounted into and redraws itself whenever that box changes, so a plan in a tab, a drawer, an accordion, a responsive panel or a split pane fits without the host writing a ResizeObserver of its own. A container with no box — a hidden tab, or an element that has not been laid out yet — is not treated as a container of zero width: the view holds a 720px fallback and adopts the real width the moment there is one. Pass a number to take the decision yourself; a numeric width is honoured exactly, installs no observer, and keeps the eight-tick axis it always had — only a container-sized plot thins its tick labels to the width it was given, because only a container-sized plot can be somewhere it had not been before. zoom and a numeric width are mutually exclusive: zoom wins — it fixes the pixels-per-day and lets the plot scroll past the container — and passing both now warns rather than discarding the width in silence.

The time axis thins its own labels (BACKLOG-0001319). A Gantt picks its tick rhythm from the calendar — every day at zoom: 'day', every seven days at 'week', every month above that — and that rhythm knows nothing about how wide a date is. Where a label is wider than the gap between two ticks, only every nth label is drawn: the largest regular stride that leaves at least 6px of clear space between neighbours, so the axis keeps an even rhythm a reader can count on rather than the uneven gaps a greedy left-to-right fit would leave. The gridlines and the split view's week separators are not thinned with the text — the fine rhythm is information and costs nothing to read; it was only ever the text that collided. Both surfaces do it: mount's axis and mountSplit's timeline header, which is where a week-zoom header used to read Sun 05 Oct 2025 Sun 12 Oct 2025 Sun 19 Oct 2025 with each date painted across the next. Widening the Gantt now shows more dates rather than the same overlap, and the thinning is re-decided on every draw, so a resize or a zoom change re-fits the labels. An un-zoomed axis is untouched: it already chooses how many ticks to draw from the plot's own width, so its labels always fitted, and a host that passed an explicit width keeps exactly the drawing it had.

The project anchor (BACKLOG-0001079). A mount option, not a mountSplit one: the joined split view below takes neither projectEpoch nor a date-valued today, and its weekend shading is unanchored. The engine's time line is whole days since the Unix epoch, so a plan written as day offsets (0, 4, 9…) legitimately renders as January 1970 — day 0 is 1970-01-01, and the module cannot tell an offset from a real epoch day, so it cannot warn about it. projectEpoch says which calendar date plan day 0 stands for. It and today take an ISO date string, a Date or a day number. A Date is read as the calendar date its local wall clock shows (BACKLOG-0001104), the way a date column reads one: new Date(2026, 2, 2) is 2 March in every time zone, and a Date that carries a time of day is the local day it falls on. Before 1104 the UTC instant was floored, which put local midnight a day early everywhere east of Greenwich. A Date is therefore decided by the reader's zone; a string is the same day on every machine — '2026-03-02' is 2 March in Sydney and in New York alike — which is the form to prefer for an anchor stored with the plan. Recognise your own case: if you worked around the old behaviour by passing new Date(Date.UTC(y, m, d)), a reader west of Greenwich now sees the previous day, because UTC midnight is still the evening before in New York — pass new Date(y, m, d) or the ISO string instead. It is display-only: axis ticks, bar labels, tooltips, screen-reader text and the built-in weekend shading move with it, and nothing the scheduler, getState, the CSV or the MSPDI export produces does — every es/ef you read back is still the number you supplied. A host-supplied nonWorking function keeps receiving raw plan days, since it was written against your day numbers. Use projectStart instead when you want the model itself to be on calendar dates.

// A relative plan: offsets in the data, real dates on the screen.
gantt.mount(el, {
  projectEpoch: '2026-03-02',  // plan day 0 is this Monday
  nonWorking: 'weekends',      // so days 5-6 are the first weekend
  today: '2026-03-06',         // converted into plan space through the anchor
});
gantt.view.scrollToToday();  // also callable on demand
gantt.applyEdit({ id: 'design', duration: 7 }); // the view redraws automatically
gantt.unmount();

Dependency arrows (BACKLOG-0001072). A link is routed by geometry. When the successor's anchor is at or beyond the predecessor's, it goes straight down and then in — a plain zero-lag finish-to-start link, where the two anchors share an x, is a clean vertical. When the anchor is behind the predecessor's — a negative lag (a lead), or two overlapping tasks — "down then in" does not exist, so the link takes a deliberate detour: out on the predecessor's own side, along a lane between the two rows, down, and in. Either way the final segment runs in the direction the arrowhead points, which is what stops a link doubling back on itself. The anchors themselves differ per link type — FS finish→start, SS start→start, FF finish→finish, SF start→finish — and so does the side the arrow arrives on, so an FF or SF link comes in from the right of the successor's finish rather than running through the bar to get there.

Link shorthand. A dependency's type accepts the MS Project string form as well as the structured one: 'FS+2', 'SS-1'. It normalises to { type: 'FS', lag: 2 } on the way in, so gantt.dependencies, the scheduler, the lag label and the MSPDI export all see the one canonical form. A shorthand lag alongside an explicit lag that disagrees warns; the explicit field wins.

createGantt({ tasks, dependencies: [
  { from: 'design', to: 'build', type: 'FS+2' },   // same as { type: 'FS', lag: 2 }
  { from: 'build',  to: 'test',  type: 'SS-1' },   // a lead
] });

Bars are draggable: drag the body to move a task, drag the right edge to resize it. When a grid and a columns map are given, each drag writes the new dates back through the grid's public edit surface (grid.edit.setCells) and reconciles a reverted or conflicted write; autoSchedule: true cascades dependents. A task placed earlier than its predecessors allow is flagged (findViolations), not silently moved.

import { createGantt } from '@toclocoinc/lattice-grid/modules/gantt';

const gantt = createGantt({
  tasks, dependencies, grid,          // a Lattice grid over the same tasks
  columns: { start: 'start', duration: 'duration' }, // task field -> grid column
  autoSchedule: true,
});
gantt.mount(document.querySelector('#plan'), { editable: true });
// drag a bar -> gantt.applyEdit(..., { writeBack: true }) -> grid.edit.setCells(...)

Hovering a bar shows a tooltip with its dates, duration, % complete and slack. Tasks are flagged when they slip: overdue (incomplete and finishing before today) and at-risk (negative total float). Negative float needs a target: pass a deadline (a day-number) to createGantt and any task that cannot meet it gets negative slack and is drawn at-risk.

Export: gantt.toCSV() writes the scheduled tasks as CSV ({ dates: true } for ISO dates); when a grid is bound, the grid's own Excel/CSV export works too. gantt.view.toSVG() serialises the drawn chart to a standalone SVG string — the handoff for turning it into an image or PDF.

Accessibility: bars are focusable and carry an aria-label describing the task (name, dates, progress, slack, critical). With the keyboard, arrows move a focused task, Shift+arrows resize it, and L links two tasks (press it on the source, then on the successor) with a finish-to-start dependency; every edit is announced in a polite live region and focus follows the edited task. Set keyboard: false to opt out.

Two panes, two ways. There are two arrangements, and which one you want depends on whether the left pane is your grid or the Gantt's own. gantt.mountSplit(container, options) — the joined split view, whose options are listed with GanttController in the type reference — draws both panes itself as one row-aligned surface, and is what to reach for unless you specifically need your own grid beside the timeline. The arrangement described here is the other one: you create and place a normal Lattice grid over the same task rows, mount the timeline beside it with gantt.mount(), and the Gantt consumes the grid rather than building it.

Binding a grid you built yourself. It is two-way. A drag on the timeline writes the new dates back through the grid's public edit surface (grid.edit.setCells) whenever createGantt was given a grid and a columns map — without both, the drag moves the bar and writes nothing — and an edit made in the grid pane, from any editor, reflects on the timeline. Both panes must be given the same row height for the rows to line up, and view.linkVerticalScroll(el) mirrors vertical scroll between them. Neither is needed with mountSplit, which shares one scroll and one row height by construction.

<div class="split" style="display:grid;grid-template-columns:360px 1fr">
  <div id="tasks"></div>   <!-- the grid pane -->
  <div id="plan"></div>    <!-- the timeline pane -->
</div>
<script type="module">
import { createGrid } from '@toclocoinc/lattice-grid';
import { createGantt } from '@toclocoinc/lattice-grid/modules/gantt';

const grid = createGrid({
  element: document.querySelector('#tasks'),
  columns: [{ field: 'name' }, { field: 'start', type: 'number', edit: { enabled: true } },
            { field: 'duration', type: 'number', edit: { enabled: true } }],
  rows: tasks, rowKey: 'id', edit: { enabled: true },
});
const gantt = createGantt({ tasks, dependencies, grid, columns: { start: 'start', duration: 'duration' } });
gantt.mount(document.querySelector('#plan'));
gantt.view.linkVerticalScroll(grid.element);   // keep the two panes aligned
</script>

Split view: the resource histogram. mountSplit's workload option (BACKLOG-0001281) draws a table of hours; its sibling histogram option (BACKLOG-0001860) draws the SAME numbers as bars instead — one row per resource, bars per time bucket, a dashed capacity line, and a tooltip on hover or keyboard focus. Both bands can be mounted together, the histogram drawn below the workload table; either alone is fine too. true takes the defaults; an object overrides resources (which to draw, and in what order — omitted draws every resource, Unassigned last), unit ('hours', the default, or 'percent' of capacity — a resource with no capacity falls back to hours for its own row), stacked ('task' splits a bar into one coloured segment per contributing task; the default draws one solid bar), height and rowHeight.

A bar that exceeds its resource's capacity carries two parts: the normal fill up to capacity, and the over-allocation fill above it — present whether or not stacked is on, since the over-allocated portion is always one thing to see, never split per task. The bars are positioned with the SAME day-to-pixel mapping as the timeline above them, so a bucket's edges line up with the scale header's own columns exactly as the workload band's do. A visually-hidden table (resource, period, hours or percent, capacity, over-allocated) carries the identical figures for a screen reader, rebuilt on every redraw.

const { createTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom();
const gantt = createGantt({
  projectStart: '2027-01-04',
  resources: { Ann: 1 },            // one unit of capacity: an 8-hour day
  tasks: [
    { id: 'design', name: 'Design', start: '2027-01-04', duration: 2, assignee: 'Ann' },
    { id: 'review', name: 'Review', start: '2027-01-05', duration: 3, assignee: 'Ann', work: 6 },
  ],
});
// Tuesday carries both tasks: design's 8h plus review's 2h is 10h against
// Ann's 8h capacity — over, and (stacked: 'task') splits it into two segments.
const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, { zoom: 'day', histogram: { stacked: 'task' } });
const NS = 'lat-gantt-split';
const tuesday = root.querySelector('.' + NS + '__hg-bargroup[data-resource="Ann"][data-bucket="2027-01-05"]');
const segments = tuesday.querySelectorAll('.' + NS + '__hg-bar').length;
const overAllocated = root.querySelectorAll('.' + NS + '__hg-bar--overalloc').length;
// One header row plus one row per bucket the timeline drew — the timeline now
// extends the domain to fill the 800px test pane, so there are 31 bucket rows.
const tableRows = root.querySelectorAll('tr').length;
view.destroy();
return [segments + ' segments', overAllocated + ' over-allocated', tableRows + ' table rows'].join('; ');

Split view: hours or percent (BACKLOG-0001879). The workload band's own unit option — 'hours' (the default; an existing page is unchanged) or 'percent' of the resource's own available hours that bucket — reads booked ÷ available × 100, where available is the exact figure the over-capacity highlight already uses (the per-resource working calendar from BACKLOG-0001861 included), never a second calculation; a bucket with a KNOWN zero available hours (a part-timer's non-working day, a week wholly on leave) reads the en-dash '–', never Infinity/NaN, and a resource with no known capacity at all falls back to hours, the same rule the histogram band already shipped. An <button aria-pressed> switch in the band's header toggles the unit live — the histogram band's, too, when one is mounted — and is reachable off the view directly as view.setWorkloadUnit(unit)/view.getWorkloadUnit(); toggling never itself changes the plan. Editing a bucket in percent mode writes HOURS: typing 50 books half the resource's available hours there, through the identical applyEdit path an hours edit already takes.

const { createTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom();
const gantt = createGantt({
  projectStart: '2027-01-04',
  resources: { Ann: 1 },           // one unit of capacity: an 8-hour day
  tasks: [{ id: 'design', name: 'Design', start: '2027-01-04', duration: 1, assignee: 'Ann' }],
});
const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, { zoom: 'day', workload: { unit: 'percent' } });
const NS = 'lat-gantt-split';
// Re-queried fresh each time: a unit switch redraws the band onto new cells,
// so a reference held from before the switch would read stale content.
const cellText = () => root.querySelector('.' + NS + '__wl-timerow[data-resource="Ann"]')
  .querySelector('.' + NS + '__wl-bucket[data-bucket="2027-01-04"]').textContent;
const before = cellText();    // 8h booked of Ann's 8h available Monday: 100%
view.setWorkloadUnit('hours'); // the same toggle the band's own switch drives
const after = cellText();     // the identical booking, read back as hours: 8
view.destroy();
return [before, after].join(' | ');

Split view: a band's maximum height (BACKLOG-0001964). The workload and histogram bands used to keep one fixed height, so a long resource list squeezed the plan above them. workload.maxHeight (and histogram.maxHeight) changes what that height MEANS: instead of a fixed box, the band sizes itself to its rows — the header strip plus one row per resource (and the workload band's totals row and any expanded sub-rows) — capped at this many pixels. Past the ceiling the band scrolls vertically on its own, with its header pinned above the scrolling rows; under it the band shrinks to its content, so a two-resource plan gets a two-row band, not an empty box. Either way the plan keeps at least half the view whatever the ceiling (a ceiling that would not fit beside the plan is shrunk to what would), and the same is true in the grid-table mode. A band with no maxHeight keeps its fixed height, so an existing page is unchanged.

const { createTestDom, resetTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const NS = 'lat-gantt-split';
const flex = (root) => root.querySelector('.' + NS + '__wl').style.flex;
// Forty resources, one task each: content is (40 rows + totals + header) × 28px,
// so the 240px ceiling binds and the band scrolls past it.
const many = Array.from({ length: 40 }, (_, i) => ({ id: 't' + i, name: 'Task ' + i, start: '2027-01-04', duration: 1, assignee: 'r' + i }));
let { root } = createTestDom();
createGantt({ projectStart: '2027-01-04', tasks: many })
  .mountSplit(root, { height: 800, zoom: 'day', workload: { maxHeight: 240 } });
const capped = flex(root);
// Two resources: content is (2 + totals + header) × 28 = 112px — under the
// ceiling, so the band shrinks to its rows instead of keeping a fixed height.
resetTestDom();
({ root } = createTestDom());
createGantt({ projectStart: '2027-01-04', tasks: many.slice(0, 2) })
  .mountSplit(root, { height: 800, zoom: 'day', workload: { maxHeight: 240 } });
const sized = flex(root);
return capped + ' | ' + sized;

Split view: load from other projects (BACKLOG-0001940). A resource's real load is not always this plan's own tasks. Two equivalent spellings book load that counts in the workload band, the histogram and the band's own over-allocation highlight without ever becoming a row or a bar on the timeline, and without ever touching the critical path, the project finish or a summary: a task carrying loadOnly: true books its own start/end/duration and assignments exactly as any task would, but the controller keeps it out of the schedule entirely; the workload.external option books { resource, start, end, units | hours, label, group } directly, with no task behind it at all. Either way the booking is listed under its resource's expanded sub-rows as another project's booking (drawn distinctly, never editable — there is no bar to drag and no contour to write back to).

const { createTestDom, resetTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom();
const gantt = createGantt({
  projectStart: '2027-02-01',
  resources: { Ann: 1 },
  tasks: [
    { id: 'design', name: 'Design', start: '2027-02-01', duration: 1, assignee: 'Ann' },   // 8h Monday
    { id: 'review', name: 'Review', start: '2027-02-02', duration: 1, assignee: 'Ann' },   // 8h Tuesday
    // Another project's booking, carried as a task so it has its own dates,
    // but flagged load-only: counted in the band, never a row or a bar.
    { id: 'other', name: 'Other launch', start: '2027-02-02', duration: 1, assignee: 'Ann', work: 4, loadOnly: true },
  ],
});
const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, {
  zoom: 'day',
  workload: {
    // The non-task spelling of the same idea: no task at all behind it.
    external: [{ resource: 'Ann', start: '2027-02-01', end: '2027-02-02', hours: 2, label: 'Phoenix support' }],
  },
});
const NS = 'lat-gantt-split';
const bars = root.querySelectorAll('.' + NS + '__barrow').length; // design + review only
const total = root.querySelector('.' + NS + '__wl-row[data-resource="Ann"]')
  .querySelector('.' + NS + '__wl-cell--total').textContent;
// 8 (design) + 8 (review) + 4 (other, load-only) + 2 (external) = 22
view.toggleResource('Ann');
const otherProjectRows = root.querySelectorAll('.' + NS + '__wl-row--loadonly').length; // "other" + the external entry
const finish = gantt.schedule.projectFinish; // unaffected by "other" — it never reaches the CPM engine
view.destroy();
resetTestDom();
return `${bars} bars; total ${total}; ${otherProjectRows} other-project rows; finish unaffected: ${finish === gantt.schedule.tasks.get('review').ef}`;

Split view: resizable columns and divider (BACKLOG-0001924). Every left-table column header (__colhead) carries a draggable right border with a col-resize cursor; once it is focused (by pointer or Tab), the arrow keys step it 8px a press, 32px with Shift. A column's own minWidth/maxWidth clamp how far it goes, and resizable: false omits the border entirely. The table/timeline divider (gridWidth) is the same gesture — a thin strip between the two panels, focusable and keyed the same way. A settled drag or keystroke on either — never mid-drag — fires exactly one columnResize event: { key, width, widths }, the divider reporting key: 'grid' and widths carrying every column's current width plus the divider's own. The same widths are reachable any time off the view as view.getColumnWidths(), and round-trip through gantt.getState()/setState() — restoring a saved state calls view.setColumnWidths() for you.

const { createTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root, document } = createTestDom();
const columns = [
  { key: 'name', title: 'Task', width: 0, kind: 'name' },
  { key: 'duration', title: 'Days', width: 60, kind: 'duration', minWidth: 50, maxWidth: 90 },
  { key: 'locked', title: 'Locked', width: 40, resizable: false },
];
const gantt = createGantt({ projectStart: '2027-01-04', tasks: [{ id: 'design', name: 'Design', duration: 5 }] });
const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, { gridWidth: 300, columns });
const NS = 'lat-gantt-split';

let events = 0;
let lastKey = null;
gantt.on('columnResize', (e) => { events += 1; lastKey = e.key; });

// Drag the "Days" column's border 50px right — past its own 90px maxWidth.
const handle = root.querySelector('.' + NS + '__colresize[data-resize-key="duration"]');
handle.dispatchEvent({ type: 'pointerdown', target: handle, clientX: 0, clientY: 0, button: 0, bubbles: true });
document.documentElement.dispatchEvent({ type: 'pointermove', target: document.documentElement, clientX: 50, clientY: 0 });
document.documentElement.dispatchEvent({ type: 'pointerup', target: document.documentElement, clientX: 50, clientY: 0 });
const daysWidth = root.querySelectorAll('.' + NS + '__colhead')[1].style.width;

// `resizable: false` grows no border to drag at all.
const lockedHandle = root.querySelector('.' + NS + '__colresize[data-resize-key="locked"]');

// Shift+ArrowRight on the focused divider steps it 32px.
const divider = root.querySelector('.' + NS + '__divider--resize');
divider.dispatchEvent({ type: 'keydown', target: divider, key: 'ArrowRight', shiftKey: true, bubbles: true });

// Persist the resize, remount fresh, and restore it.
const saved = gantt.getState();
gantt.mountSplit(root, { gridWidth: 300, columns });
gantt.setState(saved);
const restored = gantt.getState();

view.destroy();
return [events, lastKey, daysWidth, lockedHandle ? 'handle' : 'none', restored.gridWidth, restored.columnWidths.duration].join(' | ');

Split view: the plain table keeps the name column legible (BACKLOG-0002111). In the hand-built table (no createGrid) every column keeps its declared or natural width, and the name column is never squeezed below its 160px floor — overridable per column with minWidth — to fit gridWidth; when the columns are wider than the pane the pane scrolls sideways (header and body together) instead of compressing them. A task name renders on one line with one trailing ellipsis, its full text in the cell's native title and accessible name, and every row stays rowHeight. Pass wrapNames: true to opt back into wrapping, which grows the row to fit.

const { createTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom();
const NS = 'lat-gantt-split';
const gantt = createGantt({ projectStart: '2027-01-04', tasks: [
  { id: 'a', name: 'Design the landing page with a long name that would wrap', duration: 5 },
] });

const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, { gridWidth: 300 });
const widths = view.getColumnWidths();
const nameText = root.querySelector('.' + NS + '__name-text');
const row = root.querySelector('.' + NS + '__row[data-task="a"]');
const oneLine = nameText.classList.contains(NS + '__name-text--nowrap');
const rowH = row.style.height;

// `wrapNames: true` opts back into wrapping, so the row grows to fit.
gantt.mountSplit(root, { gridWidth: 300, wrapNames: true });
const wrapped = root.querySelector('.' + NS + '__row[data-task="a"]').style.height !== rowH;

view.destroy();
return [widths.name, widths.start, oneLine ? 'one' : 'wrap', rowH, wrapped ? 'true' : 'false'].join('|');

Split view: the critical-path highlight (BACKLOG-0001953). By default every zero-float task and its dependency arrows are drawn in the critical colour. showCritical: false on mountSplit turns that off entirely, and view.setShowCritical(bool) toggles it live — a redraw in place, no remount. criticalStyle: 'fill' (the default) recolours a critical bar; criticalStyle: 'outline' keeps the bar's own fill and adds only the critical outline, so the marking sits over per-task colours. A kind: 'slack' left-panel column shows each task's total float in whole working days, read-only.

const { createTestDom, resetTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom();
const gantt = createGantt({
  projectStart: '2027-01-04',
  tasks: [
    { id: 'a', name: 'Design', duration: 3 },
    { id: 'b', name: 'Build', duration: 2 },
    { id: 'd', name: 'Docs', duration: 1 },        // 4 days of float
  ],
  dependencies: [{ from: 'a', to: 'b', type: 'FS' }],
});
const NS = 'lat-gantt-split';
const barOf = (id) => {
  const r = root.querySelector('.' + NS + '__barrow[data-task="' + id + '"]');
  return r ? r.querySelector('.' + NS + '__bar') : null;
};

const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, { columns: ['name', 'slack'] });
const criticalOn = barOf('a').classList.contains(NS + '__bar--critical');
const slack = root.querySelector('.' + NS + '__grid-body')
  .querySelector('.' + NS + '__row[data-task="d"]')
  .querySelector('.' + NS + '__cell[data-col="1"]').textContent;

view.setShowCritical(false);                      // redraw, no remount
const toggledOff = !barOf('a').classList.contains(NS + '__bar--critical');
view.setShowCritical(true);
const toggledBack = barOf('a').classList.contains(NS + '__bar--critical');

const outline = gantt.mountSplit(root, { columns: ['name'], criticalStyle: 'outline' });
const outlineClass = barOf('a').classList.contains(NS + '__bar--critical-outline');
const keepsOwnFill = !barOf('a').classList.contains(NS + '__bar--critical');
outline.destroy();
resetTestDom();

return [criticalOn, slack, toggledOff, toggledBack, outlineClass, keepsOwnFill].join('|');

Split view: the tables as real grids (BACKLOG-0001956). Inject your grid factory — mountSplit(el, { createGrid }), injected rather than imported so the Gantt bundle does not carry the grid — and the task table, the workload band’s resource table and the histogram band’s are mounted as real Lattice grids, configured with the normal column API: columns for the tasks, workload.columns and histogram.columns for the bands. Every built-in column (name, start, end, duration, progress, assignee, effort, units; a band’s resource and total) is an ordinary column: name it by id to show it, give an object with its id to override part of it (merged one level deep, so a reformatted start still reschedules the plan when edited), leave it out to remove it, and add any number of your own columns with the full grid spec. List order is display order; only name (tasks) and resource (bands) are required. Rows are drawn at the timeline’s own row heights and scroll with it; a grid sort or filter reorders the timeline (or the band’s plot) to match. Each table’s column order, widths, visibility, sort and filters round-trip through gantt.getState()/setState() under tables, and columnResize still fires. A band’s time buckets are grid columns too (BACKLOG-0002097) — one per timeline bucket, pinned under the timeline’s own scale — but every header control is switched off on them and a column definition wins over any restored state, so a bucket never carries a sort, filter, menu, drag, hide, resize or group control of any kind. The grids themselves are on view.grids.

const { createTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGrid } = await import('../packages/dom/src/index.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom();
const gantt = createGantt({ projectStart: '2027-01-04', tasks: [
  { id: 'design', name: 'Design', duration: 5, cost: 1200, assignee: 'Ann' },
  { id: 'build', name: 'Build', duration: 3, cost: 800, assignee: 'Bob', dependsOn: ['design'] },
] });
const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, {
  createGrid,
  columns: [
    'name',
    { id: 'start', title: 'Begins', format: { type: 'date', pattern: 'dd/MM/yyyy' } }, // an overridden default
    { id: 'cost', field: 'cost', title: 'Cost', type: 'number' },                         // a host column
  ],
  workload: { columns: [{ id: 'resource', title: 'Who' }, 'total'] },
});

const grid = view.grids.tasks;
grid.sort.set([{ col: 'cost', dir: 'asc' }]);     // the timeline follows the grid's order
const saved = gantt.getState();                     // saved.tables.task carries the sort

const out = [
  grid.columns.visible().map((c) => c.id).join(','),
  grid.rows.text('design', 'start'),
  view.rowGeometry().map((g) => g.id).join(','),
  saved.tables.task.sort.map((s) => s.col + ':' + s.dir).join(','),
  view.grids.workload.columns.visible().filter((c) => !/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/.test(c.id)).map((c) => c.title).join(','),
].join(' | ');
view.destroy();
return out;

Split view: the bands’ own columns (BACKLOG-0001951). workload.columns and histogram.columns are independent of the task table. Besides the built-in resource and total, a column is { key, title, width, align, value(resource, ctx), render(resource, ctx), subRows }. resource is the resource’s own spec from resources (a role, a team, a rate …) plus its key and display name; ctx carries the band’s figures for the row (capacity, total, cost, over, overCount, unit, tasks). A host can compute anything there: a cross-project peak, the weeks over-allocated. value is what the column sorts and filters on; render, when given, draws the cell (a string is set as text, a node is mounted). subRows is the rule for the task sub-rows beneath an expanded resource: 'blank' (default) shows nothing, 'inherit' repeats the resource’s value, and a function (task, resource, ctx) supplies the sub-row’s own. The totals row is blank. The grid header sorts and resizes the columns; a settled resize fires columnResize under a namespaced key (band:role for the workload band, histogram:role for the histogram), and getColumnWidths()/setColumnWidths() round-trip those keys. The time buckets are grid columns too (BACKLOG-0002097) but never get header controls — each is inert to sort, filter, resize and move whatever the config. Needs createGrid, as the other grid columns do.

const { createTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGrid } = await import('../packages/dom/src/index.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom();
const gantt = createGantt({
  projectStart: '2027-01-04',
  resources: [{ id: 'ann', name: 'Ann', role: 'Designer', capacity: 1 }],
  tasks: [{ id: 'a', name: 'Design', duration: 5, assignee: 'ann' }],
});
const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, {
  createGrid,
  workload: { columns: [
    'resource',
    { key: 'role', title: 'Role', width: 90, value: (r) => r.role, subRows: 'inherit' },
    { key: 'peak', title: 'Peak', width: 70, align: 'right', value: (r, ctx) => ctx.total },
  ] },
});
const grid = view.grids.workload;
view.toggleResource('ann');
view.setColumnWidths({ 'band:role': 120 });
const out = [
  grid.columns.visible().map((c) => c.id).filter((id) => !/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/.test(id)).join(','),
  grid.rows.text('ann\u0000', 'role'),
  grid.rows.text('ann\u0000a', 'role'),
  grid.rows.text('ann\u0000a', 'peak'),
  'band:role=' + view.getColumnWidths()['band:role'],
].join(' | ');
view.destroy();
return out;

Split view: the grid’s select and bounded-number editors (BACKLOG-0001939). A host column can ask for one of the grid’s own editors with the plain editor shorthand instead of a full edit/lookup spec: editor: { type: 'select', options } gives the select editor — a dropdown of the options in the order given, committing the chosen option’s value — and editor: { type: 'number', min, max, step } gives the number editor, which clamps to the bounds and steps by step (a spinner is added, so the step is keyboard-operable). An editor makes the column editable unless editable/edit says otherwise, and it needs createGrid: the lightweight tables have no editors. A host column written with a pre-grid key and no field writes its field back under that id.

const { createTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGrid, createEditor } = await import('../packages/dom/src/index.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root, document } = createTestDom();
const gantt = createGantt({ projectStart: '2027-01-04', tasks: [
  { id: 'design', name: 'Design', duration: 5, status: 'New', cost: 500 },
] });
const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, {
  createGrid,
  columns: [
    'name',
    { key: 'status', editor: { type: 'select', options: ['New', 'In progress', 'Done'] } }, // a dropdown column
    { key: 'cost', editor: { type: 'number', min: 0, max: 1000, step: 100 } },               // a bounded number column
  ],
});

const grid = view.grids.tasks;
const options = grid.columns.get('status').lookup.options().map((o) => o.label).join(',');

// The number editor clamps a typed value to the bound, and steps by `step`.
const number = createEditor({
  document, cell: document.createElement('div'), overlay: document.body,
  column: grid.columns.get('cost'), value: 500,
});
number.input.value = '2500';
const clamped = number.commit().value;

// The select editor commits the chosen option to the task's field.
grid.edit.setCells([{ key: 'design', colId: 'status', value: 'In progress' }], 'cell', { origin: 'user' });
await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 5));
const chosen = gantt.taskById('design').status;

view.destroy();
return [options, chosen, clamped].join(' | ');

Split view: workload thresholds, showUnassigned and the click event (BACKLOG-0001942). The workload band's thresholds option colours a cell — and the histogram band's bar for the same bucket — by how full the bucket is: each entry is { at, className | colour }, at a fraction of the bucket's available hours (0.8 = 80% booked), and either a className added to the cell/bar or a colour applied inline (spelled colour or color). The highest threshold a bucket reaches wins, and it applies in every unit — hours, percent and cost — because the fraction is read off the hours figures, which is also why it still works in cost mode, where the band has no capacity line. showUnassigned: false hides the Unassigned row. Clicking a cell fires workload:click: { resource, bucketStart, bucketEnd, booked, available, tasks }, the hour figures always hours whatever the band is drawing.

const { createTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom();
const gantt = createGantt({
  projectStart: '2027-01-04',
  resources: { Ann: 1 },            // one unit of capacity: an 8-hour day
  tasks: [
    { id: 'design', name: 'Design', start: '2027-01-04', duration: 1, assignee: 'Ann' },
    { id: 'loose', name: 'Loose', start: '2027-01-04', duration: 1 },   // no assignee
  ],
});
const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, {
  zoom: 'day',
  workload: { thresholds: [{ at: 0.5, colour: 'rgb(255, 0, 0)' }], showUnassigned: false },
});
const NS = 'lat-gantt-split';
// Monday: Ann books 8h of her 8h capacity — 100% meets the 0.5 threshold, so
// the configured colour lands on the cell (and would on the histogram bar).
const row = root.querySelector('.' + NS + '__wl-timerow[data-resource="Ann"]');
const cell = row.querySelector('.' + NS + '__wl-bucket[data-bucket="2027-01-04"]');
const coloured = cell.style.backgroundColor;
// The unassigned task's row is hidden.
const unassigned = root.querySelector('.' + NS + '__wl-timerow[data-resource=""]') ? 'shown' : 'hidden';
// A click on the cell reports the bucket's bookings.
let clicked = null;
gantt.on('workload:click', (e) => { clicked = e; });
cell.dispatchEvent({ type: 'click', target: cell, bubbles: true });
view.destroy();
return [coloured, unassigned, clicked.resource, clicked.bucketStart, clicked.booked, clicked.available, clicked.tasks.length, clicked.tasks[0].id].join(' | ');

Split view: the task table's own cell and row events (BACKLOG-0001981). The task table became a real Lattice grid (BACKLOG-0001956); this maps its own pointer and edit events onto the controller — cellClick/cellDblClick/cellContextMenu, rowClick/rowDblClick and the edit lifecycle beforeCellEditStart (vetoable) / cellEditStart / cellEditCommit / cellEditCancel — with id, rawTask, the scheduled task and the originating DOM event on every payload. A double-click, Enter or F2 on an editable cell with no editor already open raises beforeCellEditStart before the grid's own editor opens, for every one of those paths alike — Enter is also how a COMMIT is typed from inside an already-open editor, but that is told apart by whether an edit is already in progress, not by the key, so a commit is never mistaken for a fresh open attempt. A handler's preventDefault(reason) keeps the cell closed for that gesture — the grid's own cellDblClick is not raised either, since nothing reached the grid at all — and fires the paired cellEditStart:cancelled with that reason, the same before*/:cancelled contract every other gated Gantt event follows. Escape on an open editor raises cellEditCancel; Enter raises cellEditCommit with oldValue/newValue.

const { createTestDom, TestEvent, flushFrames } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGrid } = await import('../packages/dom/src/index.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom();
const gantt = createGantt({
  projectStart: '2027-01-04',
  tasks: [{ id: 'a', name: 'Alpha' }, { id: 'b', name: 'Beta' }],
});
const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, { createGrid, editable: true, keyboard: true, columns: ['name'] });
flushFrames();
const seen = [];
for (const name of ['cellClick', 'rowClick', 'cellDblClick', 'beforeCellEditStart', 'cellEditStart', 'cellEditStart:cancelled', 'cellEditCommit', 'cellEditCancel']) {
  gantt.on(name, (e) => seen.push([name, e.id, e.columnKey, e.reason]));
}
const cellOf = (key) => { const row = root.querySelector(`.lat-row[data-key="${key}"]`); return row ? row.querySelector('.lat-cell[data-col="name"]') : null; };

// A click raises cellClick, then rowClick for the row under it.
cellOf('a').dispatchEvent(new TestEvent('click'));
const [click, rowClick] = seen.splice(0);

// A host veto on beforeCellEditStart keeps row b's cell closed for the
// gesture: no editor opens, cellDblClick is not raised for a gesture the
// gate claimed before the grid's own handler ever saw it, and the paired
// cellEditStart:cancelled carries the veto's reason.
gantt.on('beforeCellEditStart', (e) => { if (e.id === 'b') e.preventDefault('locked'); });
cellOf('b').dispatchEvent(new TestEvent('dblclick'));
const vetoed = !cellOf('b').querySelector('input');
const vetoBatch = seen.splice(0);
const vetoSeen = vetoBatch.map((e) => e[0]).join(',');
const vetoReason = vetoBatch.find((e) => e[0] === 'cellEditStart:cancelled')[3];

// Row a's double-click raises beforeCellEditStart, then cellDblClick and
// cellEditStart, and the grid's own editor opens.
cellOf('a').dispatchEvent(new TestEvent('dblclick'));
const opened = !!cellOf('a').querySelector('input');
const openSeen = seen.splice(0).map((e) => e[0]).join(',');

// Enter commits — cellEditCommit carries the old and new value.
const input = cellOf('a').querySelector('input');
input.value = 'Alpha v2';
input.dispatchEvent(new TestEvent('keydown', { key: 'Enter', bubbles: true }));
await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 5));
const newName = gantt.taskById('a').name;
seen.length = 0;

// Escape abandons an open edit — cellEditCancel, no write.
cellOf('a').dispatchEvent(new TestEvent('dblclick'));
cellOf('a').querySelector('input').dispatchEvent(new TestEvent('keydown', { key: 'Escape', bubbles: true }));
const cancelled = seen.some((e) => e[0] === 'cellEditCancel');
seen.length = 0;

// Row a has no editor open now: Enter on it is an OPEN attempt, not a
// commit, so it raises beforeCellEditStart then cellEditStart exactly like
// the double-click above, and the grid's own editor opens — a key reaching
// an open editor is the only case Enter is ever read as a commit instead.
cellOf('a').dispatchEvent(new TestEvent('keydown', { key: 'Enter', bubbles: true }));
const enterOpened = !!cellOf('a').querySelector('input');
const enterOpenSeen = seen.splice(0).map((e) => e[0]).join(',');

view.destroy();
return [
  `click ${click[0]}+${rowClick[0]}`,
  `veto ${vetoed} (${vetoSeen}) ${vetoReason}`,
  `open ${opened} (${openSeen})`,
  `commit ${newName}`,
  `cancel ${cancelled}`,
  `enterOpen ${enterOpened} (${enterOpenSeen})`,
].join(' | ');

Split view: workload cell and resource events (BACKLOG-0001996). The workload band's bucket cells and the histogram band's bars raise the same pointer vocabulary on the controller (gantt.on), with resource, resourceName, the half-open bucketStart/bucketEnd, the hours booked/available, the percent of capacity (when a capacity is known), the over flag, the band's unit, the view, the originating DOM event and the contributing tasks ({ id, name, hours, units }) — hour figures always hours, whatever the band is drawing. A per-task sub-row cell adds id, rawTask (the task as you supplied it) and the scheduled task; a resource-name cell raises workloadResourceClick with just resource/resourceName. workloadCellDblClick and workloadCellContextMenu are claimable with preventDefault() (a host's own editor or menu), pairing with no :cancelled — nothing was refused, only claimed. Keyboard focus and Enter/Space on a histogram bar raise the same events as pointer ones, and none fire for the host's own setTasks/rows.apply. Hovering a cell shows a built-in tooltip listing the contributing tasks; set workload.tooltip: false to hide it or workload.tooltip: (payload) => node | string to replace it. The Lattice names, against Bryntum Gantt's:

LatticeBryntumWhen, and what it carries
workloadCellClickcellClickA click on a workload bucket cell or histogram bar: { resource, resourceName, bucketStart, bucketEnd, booked, available, percent, over, unit, tasks, id, rawTask, task }.
workloadCellDblClickcellDblClickA double click; preventDefault() claims it so a host opens its own editor (and the band's own inline hours editor stays shut). Two workloadCellClick events precede it.
workloadCellContextMenucellContextMenuA right click; preventDefault() suppresses the browser's own menu.
workloadCellHover / workloadCellHoverEndcellMouseOver / cellMouseOutOnce per entry / exit of a cell or bar (moving within the same cell is not a re-entry). Keyboard focus reaching or leaving a histogram bar raises the same pair.
workloadResourceClickresourceClickA click on a workload-band resource-name cell: { resource, resourceName }.
 
const { createTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom();
const gantt = createGantt({
  projectStart: '2027-01-04',
  resources: { Ann: 1 },            // one unit of capacity: an 8-hour day
  tasks: [
    { id: 'design', name: 'Design', start: '2027-01-04', duration: 1, assignee: 'Ann' },
    { id: 'extra', name: 'Extra', start: '2027-01-04', duration: 1, assignee: 'Ann', work: 2 },
  ],
});
const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, { zoom: 'day', workload: true });
const NS = 'lat-gantt-split';
// Monday: Ann books 10h of her 8h capacity — over-allocated, 125%.
let clicked = null;
gantt.on('workloadCellClick', (e) => { clicked = e; });
const row = root.querySelector('.' + NS + '__wl-timerow[data-resource="Ann"]');
const cell = row.querySelector('.' + NS + '__wl-bucket[data-bucket="2027-01-04"]');
cell.dispatchEvent({ type: 'click', target: cell, bubbles: true });
// The hover tooltip lists the contributing tasks.
cell.dispatchEvent({ type: 'pointerover', target: cell, bubbles: true });
const tip = root.querySelector('.' + NS + '__wl-tooltip');
const tipHasExtra = tip && /Extra/.test(tip.textContent) ? 'Extra' : '';
view.destroy();
return [clicked.resource, clicked.percent, clicked.over, clicked.tasks.length, tipHasExtra].join(' | ');

Split view: scroll and zoom with no remount (BACKLOG-0001947). Three methods drive the view off a dashboard's own controls — a row picker, a date search, a zoom slider — without tearing it down and rebuilding it. view.scrollToTask(id) scrolls the least each axis needs to bring a task's row and bar fully into view: vertically to the row itself, horizontally to its bar's start/finish (a milestone's single point), leaving an axis alone when the row/bar is already fully shown on it. view.scrollToDate(date) (a day-number, ISO string or Date) centres that date in the timeline's own viewport. view.setZoom(level) takes the same preset ('day'/'week'/'month'/'quarter') or raw pixels-per-day mountSplit's own zoom option does, and repaints in place: collapsed summaries, every column width and the date centred in the timeline before the call are all exactly as they were after it, since nothing here unmounts the view or loses the state the DOM itself does not carry. All three fire the same draw event any other repaint does (BACKLOG-0001960).

const { createTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom({ width: 300, height: 50 });
const gantt = createGantt({ projectStart: '2027-01-04', rowKey: 'id', tasks: [
  { id: 'proj', name: 'P' },
  { id: 'a', name: 'A', parent: 'proj', start: '2027-01-04', duration: 2 },
  { id: 'b', name: 'B', parent: 'proj', start: '2027-01-08', duration: 2 },
  { id: 'c', name: 'C', start: '2027-03-01', duration: 2 },
] });
const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, { zoom: 'day', rowHeight: 30, gridWidth: 200 });
view.toggle('proj');                 // collapse the summary before any scroll/zoom
view.setColumnWidths({ name: 240 }); // and widen a column

const scroller = view.scroller;
view.scrollToTask('c');              // brings row c's row and bar into view
const taskScroll = scroller.scrollTop + ',' + scroller.scrollLeft;

view.scrollToDate('2027-01-20');     // centres that date in the timeline
const dateScroll = scroller.scrollLeft;

view.setZoom('week');                // no remount: both of the above still hold
const out = [
  taskScroll,
  dateScroll,
  scroller.scrollLeft,                      // the centred date followed the rescale
  [...view.collapsed].join(','),            // still collapsed
  view.getColumnWidths().name,              // still widened
  view.options.zoom,
].join(' | ');
view.destroy();
return out;

Split view: a built-in zoom control (BACKLOG-0001963). The zoomControl option draws day / week / month / quarter / fit-to-plan buttons straight into the view’s own header — no host toolbar to build. Each button is a real <button> (so keyboard Enter/Space reaches it) carrying its catalogue label and an aria-pressed that tracks the level currently in effect; clicking one switches the zoom in place through the same setZoom/fitZoom a host would call, so collapsed summaries, every column width and the centred date all survive the change. fit picks the pixels-per-day that shows the whole plan and resets the scroll to its start. Pass an object instead of true to reorder or subset the buttons (levels) or to build the control detached and expose it as view.zoomControl for a host toolbar to place (position: 'toolbar', the default is 'header').

const { createTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom({ width: 300, height: 50 });
const gantt = createGantt({ projectStart: '2027-01-04', rowKey: 'id', tasks: [
  { id: 'proj', name: 'P' },
  { id: 'a', name: 'A', parent: 'proj', start: '2027-01-04', duration: 2 },
  { id: 'b', name: 'B', parent: 'proj', start: '2027-01-08', duration: 2 },
  { id: 'c', name: 'C', start: '2027-03-01', duration: 2 },
] });
// zoomControl: true draws the five buttons into the view's own header.
const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, { zoom: 'day', rowHeight: 30, gridWidth: 200, zoomControl: true });

const NS = 'lat-gantt-split';
const buttons = [...root.querySelectorAll('.' + NS + '__zoom-btn')];
const labels = buttons.map((b) => b.textContent).join('|');
const pressed = buttons.find((b) => b.getAttribute('aria-pressed') === 'true').getAttribute('data-zoom');

// Each is a real keyboard-operable

The object form (BACKLOG-0001963). position: 'toolbar' builds the buttons detached from the view — nothing is drawn in the header — and hands them back as view.zoomControl for a host toolbar to place; levels keeps only the named buttons, in order. The detached control still tracks the view’s zoom, and its own buttons still drive the view, exactly as the header form does.

const { createTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom({ width: 300, height: 50 });
const gantt = createGantt({ projectStart: '2027-01-04', rowKey: 'id', tasks: [
  { id: 'a', name: 'A', start: '2027-01-04', duration: 2 },
  { id: 'b', name: 'B', start: '2027-01-08', duration: 2 },
] });
// position: 'toolbar' builds the buttons detached; levels keeps only these two.
const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, { zoom: 'day', gridWidth: 200, zoomControl: { position: 'toolbar', levels: ['month', 'fit'] } });

const NS = 'lat-gantt-split';
const control = view.zoomControl;   // the host places this where its toolbar lives
const levels = [...control.querySelectorAll('.' + NS + '__zoom-btn')].map((b) => b.getAttribute('data-zoom')).join('|');
const inHeader = root.querySelector('.' + NS + '__zoom') ? 'header' : 'toolbar';
view.destroy();
return [levels, inHeader].join(' | ');

View and viewport events (BACKLOG-0001983, BACKLOG-0002005, BACKLOG-0001960). The view reports its own lifecycle and viewport on the controller (gantt.on), so a host can persist a zoom, follow a scroll, or build an overlay that lives exactly as long as the view. zoomChange fires once each time the split view’s time scale changes — a host view.setZoom/view.fitZoom (cause: 'api') or a click on its built-in zoom control ('control') — carrying { from, to, cause, pxPerDay }; pxPerDay is the pixels-per-day the repaint actually drew with, so a host can reproduce the scale without asking twice. visibleRangeChange fires the ISO dates now under the timeline’s left and right edges, and scroll the settled { top, left } offsets, each coalesced to at most one per animation frame — a burst of horizontal scroll, a resize and a zoom settle into one report, not dozens — and a scroll that settles where it already was is silent. draw fires once after every completed paint (BACKLOG-0001960). viewMount fires once from mount or mountSplit after the first paint, and viewDestroy once as that view is torn down (a remount, unmount() or destroy()), both carrying the view and its root element. All six are reports, not gates — there is no before* form — and none fire for your own setTasks/rows.apply. The Lattice names, against Bryntum Gantt’s:

LatticeBryntumWhen, and what it carries
zoomChangezoomChangeOnce per change of the split view’s time scale: { from, to, cause, pxPerDay }, the level before and after, what drove it, and the resolved pixels-per-day.
visibleRangeChangevisibleRangeChangeThe ISO { start, end } now under the timeline’s left and right edges, coalesced to once per frame and once after the first mount.
scrollscrollThe settled { top, left } viewport offsets, coalesced to once per frame; silent when it settles where it already was.
drawrenderOnce after every completed paint, coalesced across synchronous invalidations.
viewMountviewMountOnce from mount/mountSplit after the first paint: { view, element }.
viewDestroyviewDestroyOnce as the view is torn down, before its root is removed: { view, element }.
 
const { createTestDom, resetTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom({ width: 300, height: 50 });
const gantt = createGantt({ projectStart: '2027-01-04', rowKey: 'id', tasks: [
  { id: 'proj', name: 'P' },
  { id: 'a', name: 'A', parent: 'proj', start: '2027-01-04', duration: 2 },
  { id: 'b', name: 'B', parent: 'proj', start: '2027-01-08', duration: 2 },
  { id: 'c', name: 'C', start: '2027-03-01', duration: 2 },
] });

const heard = { viewMount: 0, zoomChange: [], viewDestroy: 0 };
gantt.on('viewMount', () => { heard.viewMount += 1; });
gantt.on('zoomChange', (e) => { heard.zoomChange.push(`${e.to}:${Math.round(e.pxPerDay)}`); });
gantt.on('viewDestroy', () => { heard.viewDestroy += 1; });

const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, { zoom: 'day', rowHeight: 30, gridWidth: 200 });
const mounted = heard.viewMount;               // one, synchronously from mountSplit

// The scroll event reports the settled offsets once per frame; the scroller
// exposes the same pair synchronously, so a burst of three scrolls reads 0,300.
view.scroller.setScroll(0, 100);
view.scroller.setScroll(0, 200);
view.scroller.setScroll(0, 300);
const scrolled = `${view.scroller.scrollTop},${view.scroller.scrollLeft}`;

view.setZoom('week');                          // one zoomChange, with the resolved pxPerDay
const zoomed = heard.zoomChange.join(';');

view.destroy();                                // one viewDestroy, root still attached
resetTestDom();

return `mount ${mounted} | scroll ${scrolled} | zoom ${zoomed} | destroy ${heard.viewDestroy}`;

Split view: the band label column sizes itself (BACKLOG-0001950). The workload and histogram bands’ resource-name column no longer follows the task table’s first column — a narrow first column (a 52px WBS column, say) used to truncate every resource name to an ellipsis. It sizes itself instead: its own default width, or workload.labelWidth/histogram.labelWidth in pixels — and always carries the full name as a native title. totals: false drops the totals column and row entirely, rather than leaving an empty cell to take the rest of the grid width.

const { createTestDom } = await import('../packages/dom/src/renderer/testdom.js');
const { createGantt } = await import('../packages/modules/gantt/index.js');

const { root } = createTestDom();
const gantt = createGantt({
  projectStart: '2027-01-04',
  resources: { Ann: 1 },
  tasks: [{ id: 'design', name: 'Design', start: '2027-01-04', duration: 2, assignee: 'Ann' }],
});
// A narrow first task column; the bands below are sized on their own, not by it.
const view = gantt.mountSplit(root, {
  zoom: 'day',
  gridWidth: 400,
  columns: [
    { key: 'progress', title: '%', width: 52, kind: 'progress' },
    { key: 'name', title: 'Task', width: 0, kind: 'name' },
  ],
  workload: { labelWidth: 200, totals: false },
  histogram: { labelWidth: 160 },
});
const NS = 'lat-gantt-split';
const wlName = root.querySelector('.' + NS + '__wl-row').querySelector('.' + NS + '__wl-cell--name');
const width = wlName.style.width;
const title = wlName.querySelector('.' + NS + '__wl-name-text').getAttribute('title');
const totals = root.querySelector('.' + NS + '__wl-cell--total') ? 'present' : 'none';
const hgName = root.querySelector('.' + NS + '__hg-row').querySelector('.' + NS + '__hg-cell--name');
view.destroy();
return [width, title, totals, hgName.style.width].join(' | ');

Live data. The Gantt exposes the same consumer surface as the grid — gantt.rows.apply({ add, update, remove }), keyed by its rowKey (default id) — so a Data Router attaches to it exactly as it does a grid, a board or a chart. One arriving stream can hydrate and update a whole screen, the Gantt included; each change recomputes the schedule and redraws.

import { createDataRouter } from '@toclocoinc/lattice-grid/modules/data-router';

const router = createDataRouter({ key: 'kind', rowKey: 'id' });
router.attach(grid, 'order').attach(gantt, 'task');  // one feed drives both
router.load(snapshot);                                // task rows land in the Gantt
router.apply(deltas);                                 // updates recompute the schedule

Type reference

Generated from the type declarations, so it always matches the release. Each surface lists its properties, its methods and the events it raises as three tables; an option or value type lists its members once.

The gantt module

GanttNamedCalendar

A named working calendar (BACKLOG-0002009), the value of an entry in the `calendars` option: the plan calendar's shape, with the resource-calendar and exception spellings accepted. A member left out is inherited from the plan calendar.

PropertyTypeDescription
workdaysnumber[]The working weekdays, 0=Sunday … 6=Saturday; defaults to the plan's. (optional)
workingDaysnumber[]The same as `workdays`, in the resource-calendar spelling. (optional)
holidaysArray<string | number | Date | { from: string | number | Date; to: string | number | Date }>Days off: ISO dates, `Date`s, day-numbers or inclusive `{ from, to }` ranges; defaults to the plan's. (optional)
exceptionsArray<string | number | Date | { from: string | number | Date; to: string | number | Date }>The same as `holidays`, named as exceptions to the working week. (optional)
hoursPerDaynumber | Record<number, number>The working hours in a day: a flat number or a per-weekday map; defaults to the plan's `hoursPerDay`. (optional)

GanttResolvedCalendar

One resolved calendar in a schedule (BACKLOG-0002009): the working time a task was scheduled under.

PropertyTypeDescription
idstringThe calendar id the tasks report in `calendar`.
workdaysnumber[]The working weekdays, 0=Sunday … 6=Saturday.
holidaysnumber[]The days off, as day-numbers.
MethodSignatureParametersReturnsDescription
isWorking(day: number): booleanday: numberbooleanWhether a calendar day-number is a working day under this calendar.

GanttZoomControlOptions

The split view's built-in zoom control (BACKLOG-0001963): a row of day / week / month / quarter / fit-to-plan buttons drawn in the view's own header. `position` picks where they go; `levels` subsets or reorders them.

PropertyTypeDescription
position'header' | 'toolbar'`'header'` (default) draws the buttons in the split view's own header; `'toolbar'` builds them detached and exposes them as `view.zoomControl` for a host toolbar to place. (optional)
levelsGanttZoomLevel[]Which levels to draw, in order; defaults to all five (`day`, `week`, `month`, `quarter`, `fit`). Unknown names are ignored. (optional)

GanttTooltipContext

What a host's own `tooltip` render function is given: the same scheduled task `label`'s own function gets, plus a `{ t, formatDate, dateAxis, rawTask }` context — `t` the resolved translator, `formatDate` the SAME locale-formatted-date helper the default card's Start/End/Date rows use, `dateAxis` the view's own option, and `rawTask` the host's original task object (for a custom field the scheduled shape does not carry).

PropertyTypeDescription
dateAxisbooleanWhether the view draws a date axis (the view's own `dateAxis` option); when false, days are plain numbers.
rawTaskGanttTask | nullThe host's original task object for this bar, for custom fields the scheduled shape does not carry; null if it cannot be found.
MethodSignatureParametersReturnsDescription
t(key: string, params?: Record<string, unknown>) => stringkey: string
params?: Record<string, unknown>		=> stringThe Gantt's message function: resolves a catalogue key (such as `gantt.tip.start`) in the active locale, with `{name}` parameters filled in.
formatDate(day: number) => stringday: number=> stringFormats a schedule day number as a date in the grid/plan locale, the way the built-in card shows Start and End (e.g. "May 1, 2026").

GanttMarker

A vertical date marker (BACKLOG-0001943): a labelled line drawn through the whole plot at a date, or — given `from`/`to` — a shaded range between two dates. Display-only: it annotates the timeline and never changes the schedule. Pass the `markers` option to `mount` or `mountSplit`.

PropertyTypeDescription
datenumber | string | DateWhere the line is drawn: a day-number (plan space), an ISO date string or a `Date`. In the plain view a string/`Date` is rebased through `projectEpoch`. Ignored when `from`/`to` are given. (optional)
fromnumber | string | DateThe range's start, in the same forms as `date`; with `to`, shades from here to there. (optional)
tonumber | string | DateThe range's end, in the same forms as `date`; with `from`, shades from there to here. (optional)
labelstringThe label drawn in the time header at the marker's line (centred over a range). (optional)
colourstringThe marker's colour (any CSS colour); defaults to the theme's muted foreground token. (optional)
dashedbooleanDraw the line(s) dashed rather than solid. (optional)

GanttWorkloadExternal

One load-only booking from another project (BACKLOG-0001940), named on the `workload.external` option rather than as a task: its hours count in the resource workload band, the histogram and the band's own over-allocation highlight, and it is listed as an "other project" sub-row under its resource, but it is never scheduled, never drawn as a row or a bar, and never touches the critical path, the project finish or a summary — there is no task for any of those to see. The equivalent per-task spelling is a task's own `loadOnly: true` flag.

PropertyTypeDescription
resourcestringThe resource the booking is against, matching a name on `resources` or a task's `assignee`.
startnumber | string | DateThe booking's start, in the same forms as a task's `start`.
endnumber | string | DateThe booking's end (exclusive), in the same forms as a task's `end`.
unitsnumberThe booking's load as a units multiplier (1 = full-time), spread over its working days at `hoursPerDay`; ignored when `hours` is given. (optional)
hoursnumberThe booking's total hours, spread evenly over its working days; takes precedence over `units`. (optional)
labelstringThe sub-row's label; defaults to a generic "other project" name. (optional)
groupstringAn arbitrary tag carried through to the sub-row, for the host's own display or sorting. (optional)

GanttTask

A task in a Gantt plan. Give a `duration` or a `start`+`end` (a day-number, ISO date string or `Date`; one is derived from the other). `milestone: true` (or `duration: 0`) is a zero-duration point. `parent` nests a task under a summary, whose window and progress are DERIVED from its children. `baselineStart`/`baselineEnd` (host-stored) drive planned-vs-actual variance; `constraint` pins or pulls the task; `assignee` and `height` feed the split view's grid panel.

PropertyTypeDescription
idstring | numberThe task's identity, used by dependencies, edits and `rows.apply`. Stringified; a duplicate id fails the schedule with `duplicate-id`.
namestringThe task's label in the table and on its bar. Defaults to the id. (optional)
colourstringThe task's own bar colour (BACKLOG-0001944), drawn on its bar, summary bar or milestone with a matching darker progress fill. A CSS colour — `#rrggbb`, `rgb()`/`rgba()`, or a named colour; `barColour` on `mount`/`mountSplit` wins over this field. `color` is accepted as the American spelling. (optional)
calendarGanttCalendarRefThe task's own working calendar (BACKLOG-0002009): a spec in the plan calendar's shape, or the id of an entry in the `calendars` option. Its duration and lags count that calendar's working days, its bar is shaded by it, and the workload band and `level()` book the days it works. A task without one follows the plan calendar. (optional)
startnumber | string | DateWhere the task is placed — a day-number, an ISO date or a `Date`. It is a floor, not a pin: the forward pass never starts the task earlier, but a predecessor may push it later. Use a `constraint` to pin it. (optional)
endnumber | string | DateThe task's finish, in the same forms as `start`. Given with `start` and no `duration`, the duration becomes `end − start`. (optional)
durationnumberHow long the task takes, in working days (the plan's time unit). Negative fails the schedule with `bad-duration`; a leaf with no duration and no start/end pair is unscheduled (BACKLOG-0002001: listed, but given no dates or bar until it has one), while a summary's is ignored because its window comes from its children. (optional)
percentCompletenumberProgress, 0-100, drawn as the filled part of the bar and used as the earned-value multiplier. A summary's is the duration-weighted mean of its descendant leaves; anything unparseable reads as null. (optional)
milestonebooleanMarks a zero-duration point: the task is scheduled as an instant (start equals finish) and drawn as a diamond. `duration: 0` does the same. (optional)
loadOnlybooleanMarks a load-only booking from another project (BACKLOG-0001940): its own `start`/`end`/`duration` and assignments still count in the resource workload band, the histogram and the band's own over-allocation highlight, listed as an "other project" sub-row under its resource, but the task is kept out of the CPM engine entirely — never scheduled, never drawn as a row or a bar, and never part of a summary, the critical path or the project finish. Not editable; the equivalent non-task spelling is the `workload.external` option. (optional)
parentstring | numberThe id of the summary task this one sits under. A summary is never scheduled in its own right — its window, progress and criticality are derived from its children — and a parent chain that loops fails with `parent-cycle`. (optional)
baselineStartnumber | string | DateThe planned start the task is measured against, in the same forms as `start`. With a baseline the schedule reports `startVariance` (actual − planned; positive is a slip). (optional)
baselineEndnumber | string | DateThe planned finish. With both baseline dates the schedule reports `finishVariance` and `durationVariance` too. (optional)
baseline{ start?: number | string | Date; end?: number | string | Date }The planned window as one object, read when `baselineStart`/`baselineEnd` are absent. (optional)
constraintGanttConstraintTypePins, floors or ceilings the task. `must-start-on`/`must-finish-on` place it on `constraintDate`; `start-no-earlier-than`/`finish-no-earlier-than` only delay it; `start-no-later-than`/`finish-no-later-than` are a ceiling the schedule reports in `schedule.conflicts` rather than obeying; `as-late-as-possible` pulls it into its late window; `as-soon-as-possible` is the explicit spelling of the default. Each code (`MSO`, `SNET`, …) is accepted too. (optional)
constraintDatenumber | string | DateThe date the constraint applies to — a day-number, ISO date or `Date`. Unused by `as-late-as-possible` and `as-soon-as-possible`. (optional)
deadlinenumber | string | DateA per-task deadline (BACKLOG-0002002) — a day-number, ISO date or `Date`. It is NOT a constraint: the scheduler never moves the task because of it. A finish after the deadline sets the record's `late` flag and drives the task's total float negative (the at-risk signal), and the view draws a deadline marker on the task's row at that date. (optional)
lockedbooleanHard-locks the task (BACKLOG-0001954): the scheduler never moves it, no matter what a predecessor does. The task is pinned at its own `start`, or — when it has none — at the date of an `MSO`/`MFO` constraint, if it carries one; a locked task with neither has nothing to lock to and keeps floating with its dependencies. A dependency that would otherwise push it later is left unhonoured and reported in `schedule.conflicts` (as a `'LOCKED'` conflict naming the violated link) instead, and the drawn dependency arrow is marked a conflict. A drag on the task itself is refused by default. See also the plan-level `lockWhen` (on `createGantt` and `computeSchedule`), which locks a task by rule — e.g. 100% complete — without setting this on every task. (optional)
constraintMode'hard'An alternative spelling of `locked: true`, read the same way; any other value is ignored. (optional)
inactivebooleanTakes the task out of the schedule's arithmetic while keeping it listed and drawn, muted (BACKLOG-0002013): it is skipped by the CPM passes, the project finish, the critical path, summary rollups, the workload band, cost and earned value. A link through it is bridged rather than broken — `A -> B(inactive) -> C` schedules `C` from `A` directly — and it keeps its OWN dates/duration rather than losing them, so its bar still draws where it was last placed. Round-trips through MSPDI as `<Active>` (`0` is inactive). Never drawn with the unscheduled marker (BACKLOG-0002001) at once — inactive wins when a task is both. (optional)
readOnlybooleanRefuses every user-driven edit on the task (BACKLOG-0002011): pointer and keyboard drag, resize, progress drag, a dependency create/delete touching it, and a task-table cell edit on it — each refused before its own `before*` event fires, with the paired `:cancelled` event carrying reason `'readOnly'`. Unlike `locked`, this says nothing about the scheduler (a predecessor still pushes the task, unless it is also locked) and nothing about the host's own code — `applyEdit`, `setDependencies` and `deleteDependency` called directly still work. See also the plan-level `readOnlyWhen` (on `createGantt`), which refuses by rule without setting this on every task. The row and bar carry a `--readonly` class and an accessible description. (optional)
assigneestring | string[]Who is booked on the task: one name or a list. Each name is a full-time booking (units 1) for resource load, over-allocation and the split view's avatars. Ignored when `assignments` is present. (optional)
assigneesstring[]An alternative spelling of `assignee`, read when that is absent. (optional)
ownerstringA third spelling of `assignee`, read when neither `assignee` nor `assignees` is present. (optional)
assignmentsArray<{ resource?: string; name?: string; id?: string; units?: number }>Explicit resource assignments with fractional units (BACKLOG-0000948): `units` is a multiplier where 1 is a full-time booking. Use this when a task books a resource at less (or more) than 100%; a bare `assignee` is `units: 1`. (optional)
assignmentPickerGanttAssignmentPickerHow the split view's assignment picker behaves for this task (BACKLOG-0001938). `max: 1` limits the roster to one resource — choosing a second replaces the first — and `units: true` shows the units (percent) field per assignment. Both are read when the picker opens; the roster is still committed as one `applyEdit({ assignments })`. (optional)
worknumber | Array<{ date: number | string | Date; hours: number }>The task's effort, in one of two forms (BACKLOG-0001281/1282). A **number** is the task's TOTAL hours; the workload band divides it between the assignments in proportion to their units and spreads each share evenly over the working days the task spans. (`hours` is accepted as the same field under its other common name.) An **array** is an explicit per-day contour — what a planner types into a workload cell — and states each day's hours itself: the task's total is the sum of the entries, nothing is spread, and the contour is authoritative for the span, so `applyEdit` derives the task's `start` and `duration` from its first and last day. An EMPTY array means "no hours booked", which is how clearing every bucket is expressed without reviving the even spread. A bar move re-times the contour onto the new days unchanged; a resize stretches it across the new span at the same daily levels. `date` is an ISO date, a `Date` or a plan day-number; the module writes ISO dates back. (optional)
schedulingModeGanttSchedulingMode 'fixedDuration' | 'fixedEffort' | 'fixedUnits'The task's scheduling mode (BACKLOG-0001862); defaults to the plan's `schedulingMode`, then `'fixedDuration'`. Under `fixedEffort` the duration is derived from the effort and the assigned resources' hours; under `fixedUnits`/`fixedDuration` the stated duration is kept. (optional)
effortDrivenbooleanWhether adding a resource keeps the effort by splitting the units (BACKLOG-0001862). Only meaningful on a `fixedDuration` task; defaults to the plan's `effortDriven`. (optional)
effortnumberThe task's total effort (work) in hours (BACKLOG-0001862), the `W` in `effort = duration × Σ units × hours per day`. A scalar `work`/`hours` is read as the same value; a `work` CONTOUR array contributes its summed hours. Required for a `fixedEffort` task to drive its duration. (optional)
prioritynumberLeveling priority: a higher value is delayed last (default 0). (optional)
heightnumberAn explicit row height (px) for the split view; applied to both panels. (optional)
costnumberThe budgeted cost (BAC) for earned-value analysis (BACKLOG-0000958) and the task's own fixed cost for `gantt.cost()` (BACKLOG-0001866). When omitted, `earnedValue()`'s BAC falls back to the rate-derived cost from the task's resource assignments when any resolved to a rate, else to the task's duration, giving schedule-only EVM. (optional)
actualCostnumberThe actual cost incurred (ACWP) for earned-value analysis (BACKLOG-0000958). Left out, the task's cost variance/CPI are `null`. (optional)
