wbsstringA WBS outline code to honour verbatim (BACKLOG-0002008), rather than deriving one from the task's position in the tree — what `importMSPDI` sets from a source plan's own `<WBS>` so a non-standard code round-trips through `exportMSPDI` unchanged. Prefixed onto this task's own children exactly as a derived code would be. Omit it (the normal case) to have the task's code derived fresh on every compute from `1`, `1.1`, `1.2`, `2`, … — see `GanttScheduledTask.wbs`. (optional)

GanttAssignmentPicker

The assignment picker's per-task behaviour (BACKLOG-0001938). A task states this to shape the picker the split view's assignee cell opens for it; the roster it produces is committed as one `applyEdit({ assignments })`.

PropertyTypeDescription
maxnumberThe most resources the picker holds; `1` makes it single-assignee. (optional)
unitsbooleanWhether each assignment shows its units (percent) field (default true). (optional)

GanttResourceCalendar

A single resource's working calendar (BACKLOG-0001861). Anything it leaves out is inherited from the plan calendar, so a resource that states only `holidays` keeps the plan's working week.

PropertyTypeDescription
workingDaysnumber[]The resource's working weekdays, 0=Sunday … 6=Saturday; defaults to the plan's. (optional)
hoursPerDaynumber | Record<number, number>The resource's working hours in a day: a flat number, or a per-weekday map (`{ 1: 7.5, 5: 4 }`). Defaults to the plan's `hoursPerDay`. This sizes the resource's own capacity, not the task's effort (which stays in plan days). (optional)
holidaysArray<string | number | Date | { from: string | number | Date; to: string | number | Date }>Leave and holidays: ISO date strings, `Date`s, plan day-numbers, or inclusive `{ from, to }` ranges. A day here is non-working for the resource. (optional)

GanttResourceRateChange

A resource's dated rate change (BACKLOG-0001866): from `from` onward the rate in force is `rate`, replacing whatever was in force before it. `from` is a day-number, ISO date or `Date`.

PropertyTypeDescription
fromnumber | string | DateThe day the new rate takes effect, inclusive — a day-number, ISO date or `Date`.
ratenumberThe rate in force from `from` onward, replacing whatever was in force before it.

GanttDependency

A typed dependency between two tasks (by id), with optional lag/lead. `type` defaults to `'FS'`; either endpoint may be a leaf or a summary. `type` also accepts the MS Project string shorthand — `'FS+2'`, `'SS-1'` (BACKLOG-0001072). It is normalised to the structured form on the way in, so `gantt.dependencies` always reads back `{ type, lag }` and there is no second internal representation. Giving both a shorthand lag and a conflicting `lag` field warns; the explicit field wins.

PropertyTypeDescription
fromstring | numberThe predecessor task's id. A link naming a summary is expanded to its descendant leaves before scheduling.
tostring | numberThe successor task's id.
typeGanttLinkType | `${GanttLinkType}${'+' | '-'}${number}`Which ends the link ties together — finish-to-start (the default), start-to-start, finish-to-finish or start-to-finish — optionally with the MS Project lag shorthand, `'FS+2'` or `'SS-1'`. It is normalised on the way in, so `gantt.dependencies` always reads back as `{ type, lag }`. (optional)
lagnumberA signed offset on the link in working days; a negative value is a lead. Given alongside a shorthand lag in `type`, this field wins and the mismatch is warned about. (optional)

GanttScheduledTask

The computed CPM values for one task (a leaf is scheduled, a summary derived).

PropertyTypeDescription
idstringThe task's id, as a string.
namestringThe task's name, defaulting to its id.
colourstring | nullThe task's own bar colour (BACKLOG-0001944), or null when it states none.
durationnumber | nullThe task's length in working days. A summary's is its derived window, `ef − es`. Null on an unscheduled task.
esnumber | nullEarly start: the earliest day the task can begin once every predecessor and its own placement floor are honoured. Null on an unscheduled task.
efnumber | nullEarly finish, `es + duration` (mapped back to calendar days when a working-time calendar is in use). Null on an unscheduled task.
calendarstringThe id of the calendar the task was scheduled under (BACKLOG-0002009): its own calendar's id, `'plan'` when it follows the plan calendar. Present only on a plan that uses the `calendars` option or a task `calendar`. (optional)
lsnumber | nullLate start: the latest the task can begin without pushing the project finish (or the deadline) out. Null on an unscheduled task.
lfnumber | nullLate finish, `ls + duration`. A task pinned by a constraint has `lf` equal to its `ef`, so it has no float. Null on an unscheduled task.
totalFloatnumber | nullSlack in working days, `ls − es`. Zero means critical; a deadline earlier than the natural finish drives it negative, which is the at-risk signal. Null on an unscheduled task.
unscheduledtrueTrue when the task is unscheduled (BACKLOG-0002001): a leaf with no duration and no start/end pair to derive one from, or a summary whose leaves are all such. It is kept and listed, with null dates, duration and float, but draws no bar and is left out of the critical path, the project finish, summaries, workload, cost and earned value; its links stay dormant until a duration or date is given. Absent on a scheduled task. (optional)
inactivetrueTrue when the task is inactive (BACKLOG-0002013): kept and listed, drawn muted, with its OWN `es`/`ef`/`duration` (not computed by the CPM passes), null `ls`/`lf`/ `totalFloat` (it has no float — it is not part of the network) and never critical. Out of the project finish, the critical path, summary rollups, the workload band, cost and earned value; a link through it is bridged so a predecessor still reaches its successor. Absent on an active task. (optional)
criticalbooleanTrue when the total float is zero or negative. A summary is critical when any child is; an as-late-as-possible task is always marked critical.
percentCompletenumber | nullThe task's progress, or null when it states none. A summary's is the duration-weighted mean of its descendant leaves, and null when every one of them is a milestone.
parentstring | nullThe id of this task's summary, or null at the top level.
wbsstringThe task's WBS outline code (BACKLOG-0002008) — `1`, `1.1`, `1.2`, `2`, … — derived from its position among its plan siblings and recomputed fresh every time the schedule recomputes, so a reorder, an indent / outdent, an add or a delete always reads the current number rather than a stale one. A task whose own `wbs` field supplied a code (an MSPDI import that carried one) reads that verbatim instead, and its own children are still numbered under it. Look a task up by its code with `gantt.taskByWbs(code)`.
schedulingModeGanttSchedulingMode 'fixedDuration' | 'fixedEffort' | 'fixedUnits'The scheduling mode the task was computed under (BACKLOG-0001862), present only when the task takes part in effort-driven scheduling. A plain fixed-duration plan carries no such field. (optional)
effortDrivenbooleanWhether the task is effort-driven (BACKLOG-0001862); present only on effort-scheduled tasks. (optional)
effortnumber | nullThe task's effort in hours (BACKLOG-0001862), `duration × Σ units × hours per day`. Present only on effort-scheduled tasks. (optional)
unitsnumberThe task's total assigned units (BACKLOG-0001862); present only on effort-scheduled tasks. (optional)
isSummarybooleanTrue when the task has children, and so was derived from them rather than scheduled.
isMilestonebooleanTrue when the task's duration is zero — a point in the plan.
lockedbooleanTrue when the task is hard-locked (BACKLOG-0001954) — by its own `locked`, `constraintMode: 'hard'`, or the plan's `lockWhen` — so the engine fixed it at its own placement regardless of any predecessor. Always false on a summary.
childrenstring[]A summary's direct children, by id, in input order. Empty for a leaf.
baselineStartnumber | nullThe planned (baseline) window, present only when the task carries a baseline. (optional)
baselineEndnumber | nullThe planned finish day-number, or null when only a baseline start was given. (optional)
startVariancenumber | nullVariance vs the baseline (actual − planned, day-numbers); a positive value is a slip. (optional)
finishVariancenumber | null`ef − baselineEnd` in days; positive means finishing later than planned. Null without a baseline finish. (optional)
durationVariancenumber | nullHow much longer the task runs than its baseline window, in days. Null unless both baseline dates were given. (optional)
deadlinenumberThe task's deadline (BACKLOG-0002002) as a calendar day-number, present only when the task states one. It anchors only the late dates — never the early ones — so a deadline never moves the bar. (optional)
latebooleanTrue when the task's early finish runs past its deadline (BACKLOG-0002002). Its total float is then negative by construction. Present only on a task that has a deadline, so a plain plan's records are unchanged. (optional)

GanttConflict

An unhonourable scheduling constraint, reported rather than obeyed.

PropertyTypeDescription
idstringThe task whose constraint could not be honoured (the locked task itself, for `'LOCKED'`).
typestringThe constraint that was refused, as its normalised code (`MSO`, `MFO`, `SNLT` or `FNLT`), or `'LOCKED'` (BACKLOG-0001954) when a hard-locked task's predecessor would have pushed it later.
datenumberThe date the constraint asked for, as a day-number, snapped to a working day. For `'LOCKED'`, the task's own fixed start.
schedulednumberThe start (for `MSO`/`SNLT`) or finish (for `MFO`/`FNLT`) the engine actually scheduled instead. It never places a task before its predecessors, and never silently moves it to satisfy a start/finish-no-later-than ceiling. For `'LOCKED'`, the start the violating predecessor would have forced, had the task not been locked.
atnumberThe same date as `date`; kept so hosts written before 1.88 keep working.
earliestFeasiblenumberThe earliest START the predecessors allow (for every constraint type); kept from before 1.88.
fromstringThe predecessor whose link forced the push; present only on a `'LOCKED'` conflict. (optional)
tostringThe locked task itself (same as `id`); present only on a `'LOCKED'` conflict. (optional)

GanttSchedule

A CPM schedule result: per-task dates/float and the critical path, or an error.

PropertyTypeDescription
okbooleanWhether the schedule computed. False leaves every other field absent except `error`, and the controller keeps its previous schedule.
errorGanttScheduleErrorWhy the schedule was refused — the same object the `error` event carries. (optional)
tasksMap<string, GanttScheduledTask>Every task's computed values, keyed by id — leaves scheduled, summaries derived. (optional)
orderstring[]Every task id in input order, which is the order a table or WBS tree walks. (optional)
criticalstring[]The ids of the leaf tasks with no float, in input order. (optional)
criticalPathsstring[][]Each zero-float chain through the network as its own list of ids, so a plan with several critical routes shows all of them. (optional)
projectStartnumberThe day the plan is anchored to — the `projectStart` option, or the calendar's first working day when one is set. When no explicit `projectStart` is given, it is derived from the earliest task start so the timeline opens on the tasks (BACKLOG-0002154). (optional)
projectFinishnumberThe latest early finish across every task, as a calendar day-number. (optional)
projectDurationnumber`projectFinish − projectStart` in days — calendar days when a working-time calendar stretched the plan, not the sum of the durations. (optional)
conflictsGanttConflict[]Constraints a predecessor made infeasible (empty when all are satisfied). (optional)
calendarbooleanWhether a working-time calendar was applied. (optional)
errorsGanttScheduleError[]Every reason the schedule was refused (BACKLOG-0002009); `error` is the first. An unknown calendar id on a task or resource is listed here by name, one entry each. (optional)
calendarsMap<string, GanttResolvedCalendar>The calendars the plan's tasks were scheduled under, keyed by calendar id (BACKLOG-0002009). Present only when a task follows a calendar other than the plan's. (optional)
unscheduledstring[]The ids of the unscheduled tasks (BACKLOG-0002001), in input order: leaves with no duration or dates, and summaries of only such leaves. Empty when every task is scheduled. (optional)
dependenciesGanttDependency[]The dependencies with their lags derived from the stored task dates, present only when the schedule was computed with `honourDates` (BACKLOG-0001948). (optional)
overAllocationsGanttOverAllocation[]The resource over-allocations for this schedule (BACKLOG-0000948). (optional)
resourceLoadGanttResourceLoadThe full resource-load report for this schedule (BACKLOG-0000948). (optional)

GanttResourceSegment

One contiguous load segment for a resource: how many units are booked over a span.

PropertyTypeDescription
startnumberThe day the segment begins (inclusive), as a calendar day-number.
endnumberThe day the segment ends (exclusive). A task that finishes as another starts does not double-count the boundary.
loadnumberThe units booked across the whole segment — the sum of the covering tasks' assignment units, where 1 is one full-time booking.
taskIdsstring[]The tasks active during the segment, which is what makes a heavy stretch explainable.

GanttOverAllocation

A resource booked beyond its capacity across concurrent tasks (BACKLOG-0000948).

PropertyTypeDescription
resourcestringThe over-booked resource's name.

startnumberThe day the over-allocation begins, as a calendar day-number.
endnumberThe day it ends (exclusive).
loadnumberThe units booked over that stretch — strictly greater than `capacity`, which is what makes it an over-allocation.
taskIdsstring[]The tasks competing for the resource over that stretch.

GanttResourceLoad

The per-resource load and the over-allocations across a schedule (BACKLOG-0000948).

PropertyTypeDescription
okbooleanWhether the load could be computed. False — with empty lists — when there is no successful schedule to read.
resourcesArray<{ resource: string; capacity: number; peak: number; segments: GanttResourceSegment[] }>One entry per resource that anything is booked on, sorted by name, each with its capacity, peak load and load segments. A resource named only in the capacities, with no booking, does not appear.
overAllocationsGanttOverAllocation[]Every stretch where a resource is booked beyond its capacity, earliest first. Empty when the plan fits.
byResourceMap<string, { capacity: number; peak: number; segments: GanttResourceSegment[] }>The same entries as `resources`, keyed by resource name for a direct lookup.

GanttBeforeEvent

What every cancellable Gantt event carries on top of its own context (BACKLOG-0000947). The controller's bus mirrors the grid core's `emitBefore` contract exactly, so a host writes the same handler shape against a Gantt as against a grid: a handler refuses the action by calling `preventDefault(reason?)`, by returning `false`, or by throwing, and may be `async` — every thenable return is awaited before the decision, so a confirm dialog or a server check can hold the write. Veto wins. On a veto the matching `<action>:cancelled` fires with the reason and the model is untouched; an action re-validated after an await and no longer applicable is cancelled as `'stale'`. Only the user-initiated paths are gated. The live/router `rows.apply` path is remote truth and raises none of these.

PropertyTypeDescription
typestringWhich event this is — `beforeTaskMove`, `beforeTaskDelete` and the rest.
defaultPreventedbooleanTrue once a handler has refused the action.
reasonstring | nullThe reason given to `preventDefault`, or null while nothing has refused it.
MethodSignatureParametersReturnsDescription
preventDefault(reason?: string): voidreason?: stringvoidRefuse the action; the optional reason is carried on the `<action>:cancelled` event.

GanttTaskEditEvent

An edit about to be applied to one task: the payload of `beforeTaskEdit`, `beforeTaskMove`, `beforeTaskResize`, `beforeProgressChange` and `beforeMilestoneMove`. One payload for the five, because they are one choke point — `applyEdit` classifies the patch and names the event, so which of the five fires says what kind of edit it is and the context below says what the edit is.

PropertyTypeDescription
idstringThe task being edited, by id.
taskGanttTaskThe task as it stands before the edit, as a shallow copy.
patch{ id: string | number; start?: number; end?: number; duration?: number; percentComplete?: number; work?: unknown }The edit itself: only the fields it changes.
originstringAlways `user`: only the user-initiated path is gated.
fromnumber | string | DateWhere the task starts now, from the plan, or its computed early start when it holds no start. (optional)
tonumber | string | DateWhere the patch would move it to; absent unless the patch sets `start`. (optional)
durationnumberThe duration the patch asks for; absent unless the patch sets one. (optional)
valuenumberThe progress the patch asks for; present only on a `beforeProgressChange`. (optional)
oldValuenumberThe progress before it; present only on a `beforeProgressChange`. (optional)

GanttTaskEditCancelledEvent

An edit that was refused: the payload of `taskEdit:cancelled`, `taskMove:cancelled`, `taskResize:cancelled`, `progressChange:cancelled` and `milestoneMove:cancelled`. The same context the before-event carried, plus the reason; a notification, so it carries no `preventDefault`.

PropertyTypeDescription
idstringThe task that was not edited.
taskGanttTaskThe task, unchanged.
patch{ id: string | number; start?: number; end?: number; duration?: number; percentComplete?: number; work?: unknown }The edit that was not applied.
originstringAlways `user`.
fromnumber | string | DateWhere the task starts, still. (optional)
tonumber | string | DateWhere it would have gone. (optional)
durationnumberThe duration that was asked for. (optional)
valuenumberThe progress that was asked for. (optional)
oldValuenumberThe progress before it. (optional)
reasonstringThe reason given to `preventDefault`, `'prevented'` when none was, or `'stale'` when the task had gone by the time a handler settled.
phase'start' | 'drag'On a resize gesture's own cancellation (BACKLOG-0001987): `'start'` when `beforeTaskResizeStart` refused it, `'drag'` when Escape abandoned it; absent for a refused commit. (optional)

dependenciesGanttDependency[]The list that was not adopted; the controller kept the one it had.
originstringAlways `user`.
reasonstringThe reason given to `preventDefault`, `'prevented'` when none was, or `'stale'` when the link had already gone.

GanttDependenciesChangedEvent

Links that changed: the payload of `dependencies`, fired once a `setDependencies` call or a `deleteDependency` call actually changes the list (BACKLOG-0001936) — after `beforeDependencyCreate`/`beforeDependencyDelete` have already let it through, so a host that only wants to know what changed does not have to diff the whole list on every `schedule` event. A lag-only edit of an existing link (same `from`/`to`/`type`) is neither an add nor a remove and does not fire this.

PropertyTypeDescription
addedGanttDependency[]The links that are new in this call.
removedGanttDependency[]The links that dropped out in this call.
dependenciesGanttDependency[]The whole list, as it now stands.
originstringAlways `user`.

GanttTaskDeleteEvent

A task about to be deleted, with its incident links: the payload of `beforeTaskDelete`.

PropertyTypeDescription
idstringThe task being deleted, by id.
taskGanttTaskThe task itself, as a shallow copy — the only chance a handler has to read it.
originstringAlways `user`: the live/router `rows.apply` remove is never gated.

GanttTaskDeleteCancelledEvent

A task that was not deleted: the payload of `taskDelete:cancelled`. A notification, so it carries no `preventDefault`.

PropertyTypeDescription
idstringThe task that stayed.
taskGanttTaskThe task itself.
originstringAlways `user`.
reasonstringThe reason given to `preventDefault`, `'prevented'` when none was, or `'stale'`.

GanttRowMoveEvent

GanttRowMovedEvent

GanttDataBand

One linked data band under the split view (BACKLOG-0001958): a measure per row per timeline bucket — forecast cost per task per week, say — laid out like the workload band and scroll- and zoom-locked to the timeline. The bucket cells carry no header controls under any configuration; the left-hand columns are a real grid when `createGrid` is injected.

PropertyTypeDescription
idstringThe band's id: names it in events, state and `getDataBandTable`.
titlestringThe band's title, shown in its header strip. (optional)
rows'tasks' | 'summaries' | 'resources' | ((tasks: Array<Record<string, unknown>>, ctx: { band: string }) => Array<{ key: string; label?: string; tasks: string[] }>)Which rows: the task tree (`'tasks'`, default, collapsing in step with the gantt), `'summaries'` only, `'resources'`, or a host grouping `fn(tasks) => [{ key, label, tasks: ids }]`. (optional)
seriesstringThe task field carrying a `[{ date, value }]` series, re-bucketed to the zoom; used instead of `value`. (optional)
interpolate'none' | 'spread'How a series fills the days between its points: `'none'` (default) or `'spread'` (spread over its days, re-summed per bucket). (optional)
aggregate'sum' | 'avg' | 'last' | 'min' | 'max' | ((values: number[], ctx: object) => number | null)How children roll up to summaries, the totals row and column: `'sum'` (default), `'avg'`, `'last'`, `'min'`, `'max'` or a function. (optional)
format'number' | 'currency' | 'percent' | { type: 'number' | 'currency' | 'percent'; currency?: string; decimals?: number } | ((value: number, ctx: object) => string)How a value displays: `'number'` (default), `'currency'`, `'percent'`, `{ type, currency, decimals }` or a function. (optional)
currencystringThe ISO currency code for `format: 'currency'`; default `'USD'`. (optional)
decimalsnumberFraction digits; defaults by format. (optional)
totalsboolean | { row?: boolean; column?: boolean }The totals row and column: `true` (default) both, `false` neither, or `{ row, column }`. (optional)
heightnumberThe band's height in pixels; default 160. (optional)
rowHeightnumberA band row's height in pixels; default 26. (optional)
columnsArray<string | GanttGridColumn | GanttBandColumn>The left-hand columns: `'name'`, `'total'`, overrides of them, grid columns and {@link GanttBandColumn}-style `{ value(row, ctx), render(row, ctx) }` columns. (optional)
thresholdsGanttDataBandThreshold[]Ascending thresholds that style a bucket cell by its value. (optional)
editablebooleanDouble-click (or Enter/F2) a task cell to type its value into the `series`, through `beforeDataBandEdit`; series bands only. (optional)
onReschedule'shift' | 'stretch' | 'keep'What a moved or resized task does to its series: `'shift'` (default), `'stretch'` or `'keep'`. (optional)
collapsedbooleanStart collapsed to the header strip. (optional)
MethodSignatureParametersReturnsDescription

bucketStart: string
bucketEnd: string
ctx: GanttDataBandContext		=> number | null | undefinedA leaf task's value for one bucket: `(task, bucketStart, bucketEnd, ctx)`, dates as ISO strings, end exclusive. (optional)

ctx: GanttDataBandContext		=> string | Record<string, string | number> | null | undefinedA cell's own style: a class name, or `{ className, ...css }`. (optional)

GanttRowDropContext

The context a dropped row carries (BACKLOG-0001993): the shared shape of `beforeRowDrop`, `rowDrop` and `rowDrop:cancelled`. It is `rowMove`'s payload plus where the row landed — `position` against `target` — the two fields that make `rowDrop` the Lattice name for Bryntum's `gridRowDrop`.

PropertyTypeDescription

oldIndexnumberIts 0-based index among its old parent's children, before the drop.
newIndexnumberIts 0-based index among its new parent's children, once the drop applies.
position'before' | 'after' | 'inside'Where it lands relative to `target`: before or after it as a sibling, or inside it as a child.
targetstring | nullThe row the drop anchors to — the row under the pointer, or the keyboard sibling/parent — or `null`.
originstringAlways `user`.
eventunknownThe originating DOM event (a drop or a keypress), or `null`.

fieldsRecord<string, GanttFieldChange>Each changed field, keyed by name, old and new.

GanttChanges

The plan changes accumulated since the last `commit()` or load (BACKLOG-0002003), as `gantt.changes()` returns it: diffed fresh against the committed baseline on every call, so an add immediately removed, or an edit undone back to the baseline, nets to nothing rather than being read off a running total.

GanttTaskChangeEvent

The payload of `taskAdd`, `taskRemove` and `taskUpdate` (BACKLOG-0001984), raised once per task a committed change added, removed or altered. The scheduled-date fields of `changes` come from the schedule, so a successor the scheduler moved reports its date change even though its raw row did not.

eventunknownThe originating DOM event, when a gesture drove the change; else `null`.
viewunknownThe mounted view the change is reachable from, or `null` when none is mounted.

GanttDatesChangedEvent

GanttTaskPointerContext

What every task-bar pointer event carries (BACKLOG-0001985 … 0001992): the task by id, as the host supplied it and as the scheduler placed it, the view it happened in, and the DOM event that caused it. Dates in these payloads are ISO dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`) on a calendar axis and plan day numbers under `dateAxis: false`; `end` is the scheduled finish boundary, the same `ef` the schedule carries. None of these fire for a host's own `setTasks` or `rows.apply` loads.

PropertyTypeDescription
idstringThe task, by id.
rawTaskGanttTask | nullThe task as the host supplied it (a shallow copy), or `null` when it is not a host task.

viewunknownThe view the gesture happened in.
eventunknownThe originating DOM event (pointer, mouse, focus or key), or `null` where there is none.

GanttCellPointerContext

The context a task-table cell event carries (BACKLOG-0001981): the shared shape of `cellClick`, `cellDblClick`, `cellContextMenu` and `beforeCellEditStart`. The table became a real Lattice grid (BACKLOG-0001956); this maps its own cell events onto the controller with the task-centric payload every Gantt event carries, plus the column and value under the cell.

PropertyTypeDescription

valueunknownThe cell's current value.
elementunknownThe cell element, or `null` for a keyboard context-menu with no element to anchor to.

GanttCellEditStartEvent

A task-table cell's editor opened (BACKLOG-0001981): the payload of `cellEditStart`, once `beforeCellEditStart` allowed it (or for a programmatic `grid.edit.start`/the auto-advance to the next cell after a commit, neither of which raises the `before` gate).

PropertyTypeDescription

GanttCellEditStartCancelledEvent

An open attempt that did not open an editor (BACKLOG-0001981): the payload of `cellEditStart:cancelled`, fired when a `beforeCellEditStart` handler calls `preventDefault(reason)` or defers (a reason of `'deferred'`). The same context the gate carried, plus why.

PropertyTypeDescription

GanttTaskDragStartEvent

GanttBeforeTaskDragEvent

A move drag about to start (BACKLOG-0001985): the payload of `beforeTaskDrag`. `preventDefault(reason)` cancels the drag before anything moves and fires `taskDrag:cancelled`. A gesture cannot wait, so a handler that defers (returns a Promise) cancels it as `'deferred'`.

PropertyTypeDescription

GanttTaskDragCancelledEvent

GanttTaskDragEvent

GanttTaskClickEvent

Two `taskClick` events precede it (one per click of the pair). `preventDefault()` claims it — the view runs no default of its own and the browser's default (selecting text) is prevented — so a host opens its own editor instead.

PropertyTypeDescription

GanttTaskMouseEvent

The pointer entered or left a task (BACKLOG-0001992): the payload of `taskMouseOver` and `taskMouseOut`, once per entry and exit of a bar, milestone or summary bar — moving between parts of the same task is not a re-entry, and empty row space is not a task. Keyboard focus reaching or leaving a bar raises the same pair, with the focus event as `event`.

PropertyTypeDescription
part'bar' | 'progress' | 'label' | 'baseline' | 'milestone' | 'summary'The part entered (on a focus, the task's own kind).

GanttTaskContextMenuEvent

A context-menu request on a task (BACKLOG-0001980): the payload of `taskContextMenu`, raised on a right-click or the Menu key / Shift+F10 on a focused row. `preventDefault()` claims it — the split view's built-in menu (when `contextMenu` is configured) is not opened, and the browser's own is suppressed either way — so a host shows its own instead.

PropertyTypeDescription
part'bar' | 'progress' | 'label' | 'baseline' | 'milestone' | 'summary'Which part of the task's drawing the request landed on.

GanttLinkPointerContext

What every dependency-link pointer event carries (BACKLOG-0001980): the link's identity — predecessor id, successor id, the normalised type and the lag in working days (0 when none, read back from `gantt.dependencies` since it is not drawn on the link itself) — the view it happened in, and the originating DOM event.

PropertyTypeDescription
fromstringThe predecessor task's id.
tostringThe successor task's id.
linkTypestringThe link's type, normalised to `'FS'`/`'SS'`/`'FF'`/`'SF'`. Named `linkType`, not `type`: every Gantt event payload's `type` is the EVENT's own name (`'linkClick'`, `'linkHover'`, …), so the link's own type is carried under a different key to avoid colliding with it.
lagnumberThe link's lag in working days; 0 when none.
viewunknownThe view the gesture happened in.
eventunknownThe originating DOM event (pointer or key), or `null` where there is none.

GanttTooltipShowEvent

A tooltip about to be shown for a task (BACKLOG-0001980): the payload of `tooltipShow`, raised once per distinct task shown (not on every pointer frame a view re-resolves the same hovered task's card on). `preventDefault()` vetoes it — the built-in card is not shown — so a host's own tooltip replaces it.

PropertyTypeDescription
textstringThe tooltip's plain-text content, the same text an `aria-describedby` reader gets.

GanttSchedulingConflict

One normalised scheduling conflict (BACKLOG-0002004): a single problem with the current plan, named in host terms rather than the engine's. It is the shape `gantt.schedulingConflicts` reports and each `schedulingConflict` event carries, distinct from {@link GanttConflict}, which is the engine's raw per-edge constraint breach.

PropertyTypeDescription
kindstringThe kind of problem: `'cycle'`, `'constraint'`, `'lock'` or `'deadline'`.
tasksstring[]The task ids the conflict involves — the cycle's members, or the single task for a constraint, lock or deadline.
linksArray<{ from: string; to: string }>The dependency links the conflict involves — the edges that close a cycle, or the one offending predecessor of a lock; empty for a constraint or deadline.
messagestringA ready-to-show message from the catalogue, already localised.

GanttSchedulingConflictEvent

The payload of `schedulingConflict` (BACKLOG-0002004), fired after a recompute only when the conflict set changed (compared by a stable key). It carries the whole current set plus the delta, so a host never has to diff the set itself. A host load (`setTasks`, `rows.apply`) seeds the set silently and fires nothing.

PropertyTypeDescription
conflictsGanttSchedulingConflict[]Every conflict in the plan now, cycle-first then constraint, lock and deadline.
addedGanttSchedulingConflict[]The conflicts new in this recompute.
resolvedGanttSchedulingConflict[]The conflicts that cleared in this recompute.

GanttSelectionChangeEvent

The task selection after a change: the payload of `selectionChange` (BACKLOG-0001995), fired once per change — a click, a Ctrl/Cmd or Shift click, an arrow key, or `select`/`deselect`. A selected task that is collapsed away or filtered out stays in `selectedIds`; `gantt.selectedHidden()` reports which are off-screen.

PropertyTypeDescription

GanttTaskFilteredEvent

The payload of the `task:filtered` event (BACKLOG-0001976), fired when `scrollToTask` is asked for a task the task table's filter is hiding. The filter is the user's, so it is left exactly as it was.

PropertyTypeDescription

GanttColumnResizeEvent

The payload of the `columnResize` event (BACKLOG-0001924), fired once a split-view column-border or table/timeline-divider drag or keyboard resize settles — never mid-drag, so one gesture is exactly one event.

PropertyTypeDescription

widthnumberThe settled width, in pixels, after the column's/divider's own `minWidth`/`maxWidth` clamp.
widthsRecord<string, number>Every left-panel column's current rendered width, keyed by its own `key`, plus the divider's own width under `'grid'` — a full snapshot a host can persist directly, e.g. alongside `gantt.getState()`'s own `columnWidths` (which carries the same shape, minus `grid`).

GanttWorkloadClickEvent

The payload of the `workload:click` event (BACKLOG-0001942), fired when a workload-band bucket cell is clicked: the resource, the half-open bucket span, the hours booked and available in it, and the tasks that booked there. `booked`/`available` are always HOURS — whatever unit the band is drawing in.

PropertyTypeDescription
resourcestring | nullThe resource the cell belongs to, or `null` for the Unassigned row.
bucketStartstringThe bucket's start, as an ISO date (`YYYY-MM-DD`).
bucketEndstringThe bucket's end (exclusive), as an ISO date (`YYYY-MM-DD`).
bookednumberThe hours booked in this bucket (0 when empty).
availablenumber | nullThe resource's available hours in this bucket, or `null` when no capacity is known.
tasksArray<{ id: string; name: string; hours: number }>The tasks that booked in this bucket, with the hours each contributed.

GanttWorkloadCellEvent

The payload of the workload-band cell events (BACKLOG-0001996): fired on a click, double-click, context menu, hover or hover-end of a workload-band bucket cell or a histogram bar. It carries the resource and its display name, the half-open bucket span, the hours booked and available, the percent-of-capacity (when a capacity is known), the over-allocation flag, the unit the band is drawing in, and the tasks that booked there. `booked`/`available`/`task.hours` are always HOURS — whatever the unit. A per-task sub-row cell adds the task's `id`/`rawTask`/`task`; an aggregate resource cell leaves those `null`. None of these fire for the host's own `setTasks`/`rows.apply` loads.

PropertyTypeDescription
resourcestring | nullThe resource the cell belongs to, or `null` for the Unassigned row.
resourceNamestringThe resource's display name, or "Unassigned" for `resource: null`.
bucketStartstringThe bucket's start, as an ISO date (`YYYY-MM-DD`).
bucketEndstringThe bucket's end (exclusive), as an ISO date (`YYYY-MM-DD`).
bookednumberThe hours booked in this bucket (0 when empty).
availablenumber | nullThe resource's available hours in this bucket, or `null` when no capacity is known.
percentnumber | nullThe booking as a percentage of capacity, or `null` when no capacity is known.
overbooleanWhether the booking exceeds the resource's available hours in this bucket.
unit'hours' | 'percent' | 'cost'The unit the band is drawing in: `'hours'`, `'percent'` or `'cost'`.
tasksArray<{ id: string; name: string; hours: number; units: number | null }>The tasks that booked in this bucket, with the hours and this assignment's units each contributed.
idstring | nullThe task this sub-row belongs to, or `null` on an aggregate resource cell.
rawTaskGanttTask | nullThe task as the host supplied it (a shallow copy), or `null` when the cell names no task or the booking is load-only.
taskGanttScheduledTask | nullThe task as the scheduler placed it, or `null` without a schedule or a task.
viewunknownThe view the cell belongs to.
eventunknownThe originating DOM event (pointer, mouse, focus or key), or `null` where there is none.

GanttWorkloadResourceClickEvent

A click on a workload-band resource-name cell (BACKLOG-0001996): the payload of `workloadResourceClick`, fired for an aggregate resource row (never a per-task sub-row or the totals row).

PropertyTypeDescription
resourcestring | nullThe resource, or `null` for the Unassigned row.
resourceNamestringThe resource's display name, or "Unassigned" for `resource: null`.
viewunknownThe view the cell belongs to.
eventunknownThe originating DOM event, or `null` where there is none.

GanttCellClickEvent

The payload of the `cell:click` event (BACKLOG-0001946), fired when a cell of the split view's task table is clicked: the task the row belongs to, the column's key, and the cell element that was clicked. The key is the grid column id — for a host column that is the `key`/`id` it was given, and for a built-in column its own id (`name`, `start`, …).

PropertyTypeDescription
taskGanttTask | nullThe raw task the row belongs to, as a shallow copy; null when no task carries the row's id.
columnKeystringThe column's key: the host column's `key`/`id`, or a built-in column's id.
elementunknownThe cell element that was clicked.

GanttHistoryEvent

The payload of the `history` event (BACKLOG-0001864), fired whenever the session undo/redo timeline changes — an edit recorded, an undo, a redo, or a clear. A host wires its undo and redo controls off `canUndo`/`canRedo` and can show `label` in a toast ("Undone: move").

PropertyTypeDescription
canUndobooleanWhether there is now an edit that `undo()` would reverse.
canRedobooleanWhether there is now an edit that `redo()` would re-apply.
labelstring | nullA short label for the action that triggered this event — e.g. `'move'`, `'resize'`, `'link'`, `'delete'`, `'level'`, `'add'` — or `null` after a clear. English and stable (host-facing data, not an announced string).

GanttDrawEvent

The payload of the `draw` event (BACKLOG-0001960), fired once after every completed paint of the timeline and bands — the initial paint, a data change, a zoom change, a band toggle, or a column resize. Several synchronous invalidations in one turn are coalesced into a single event, and it never fires before the DOM has been updated. This is the supported replacement for wrapping `view.draw`.

PropertyTypeDescription
viewunknownThe view that just painted — the plain `mount` view, or the `mountSplit` split view.
elementunknownThe view's root element, already reflecting the completed paint.
range{ start: number; end: number }The axis range the paint drew, in plan-day numbers — `start` and `end` are the same numbers the scale maps onto the timeline.
zoomstring | nullThe resolved zoom level (`'day'`, `'week'`, `'month'`, `'quarter'`, or `'custom'` for a numeric pixels-per-day), or `null` for a fit-to-width plain view.

GanttZoomChangeEvent

The payload of the `zoomChange` event (BACKLOG-0002005), fired once each time the split view's time scale changes — a host `view.setZoom`/ `view.fitZoom` (`cause: 'api'`) or a click on the view's own zoom control (`cause: 'control'`) — carrying the level before and after and the resolved pixels-per-day. A report, not a gate.

PropertyTypeDescription
viewunknownThe view whose zoom changed — the `mountSplit` split view.
fromGanttZoomValueThe zoom level before the change.
toGanttZoomValueThe zoom level after the change.
causeGanttZoomCause 'api' | 'control' | 'gesture'What drove the change: a host call (`'api'`), the built-in zoom control (`'control'`), or a future pinch/wheel gesture (`'gesture'`).
pxPerDaynumberThe pixels-per-day the view resolved for the new level, exactly what the repaint drew with.

GanttVisibleRangeChangeEvent

The payload of the `visibleRangeChange` event (BACKLOG-0002005), fired with the ISO dates now under the split view's timeline left and right edges and the resolved zoom, coalesced to at most one per animation frame across a burst of horizontal scroll, resize and zoom work, and once after the view first mounts. A report, not a gate.

PropertyTypeDescription
viewunknownThe view whose visible range changed — the `mountSplit` split view.
startstringThe ISO date (`YYYY-MM-DD`) under the timeline's left edge.
endstringThe ISO date (`YYYY-MM-DD`) under the timeline's right edge.
zoomstringThe resolved zoom level (`'day'`, `'week'`, `'month'`, `'quarter'`, or `'custom'` for a numeric pixels-per-day).

GanttScrollEvent

The payload of the `scroll` event (BACKLOG-0001983), fired by the split view at most once per animation frame while its viewport scrolls, with the offsets read from the settled position. A scroll that settles where it already was is silent. A report, not a gate.

PropertyTypeDescription
viewunknownThe view whose viewport scrolled — the `mountSplit` split view.
topnumberThe viewport's vertical offset, in content pixels.
leftnumberThe viewport's horizontal offset, in content pixels.

GanttViewMountEvent

The payload of the `viewMount` event (BACKLOG-0001983), fired once by the plain `mount` view or the `mountSplit` split view after its first paint. A report, not a gate.

PropertyTypeDescription
viewunknownThe view that mounted — the plain `mount` view or the `mountSplit` split view.
elementunknownThe view's root element, already attached and painted.

GanttViewDestroyEvent

The payload of the `viewDestroy` event (BACKLOG-0001983), fired once by a mounted view as it is torn down — a remount, `unmount()` or `destroy()` — before its root is removed, so `element` is still readable. A report, not a gate.

PropertyTypeDescription
viewunknownThe view being torn down — the plain `mount` view or the `mountSplit` split view.
elementunknownThe view's root element, still attached at the moment the event fires.

GanttColumnEditor

The `editor` shorthand on a split-view grid column (BACKLOG-0001939): ask for one of the grid's own editors with the split view's plain keys, rather than spelling out the full `edit`/`lookup` spec. `{ type: 'select', options }` is the select editor — a dropdown of the options in the order given, committing the chosen option's value — and `{ type: 'number', min, max, step }` is the number editor, which clamps to the bounds and steps up/down by `step` (a spinner is added, so the step is keyboard-operable). An `editor` makes the column editable unless `editable`/`edit` says otherwise. It needs the view's `createGrid`: the lightweight tables have no editors.

PropertyTypeDescription
type'select' | 'number'The grid editor to use: `'select'` for a dropdown of options, `'number'` for a bounded number.
optionsOption[] | ReadonlyArray<string | number> | (() => Option[] | Promise<Option[]>)The select editor's options, in the order the dropdown shows them: an array of `{ id, label }` options, of bare strings or numbers (each becomes its own value and label), or a function returning either (synchronously or as a promise). (optional)
minnumberThe lowest value the number editor accepts; lower typed values clamp to it. (optional)
maxnumberThe highest value the number editor accepts; higher typed values clamp to it. (optional)
stepnumberThe increment the number editor steps by; a spinner is added for it. (optional)

GanttGridColumn

A split-view table column when the tables are real grids (BACKLOG-0001956): the full grid column spec. An `id` naming a built-in column (`name`, `start`, `end`, `duration`, `progress`, `assignee`, `effort`, `units`; a band's `resource`/`total`) overrides that column, merged over it one level deep so the built-in behaviour it does not replace is kept.

PropertyTypeDescription
keystringThe pre-grid column key, read as `id`. (optional)
editFieldstringThe task field an edit in this column writes, when it is not `field`. (optional)
editorGanttColumnEditorThe grid editor shorthand for this column (select or bounded number). (optional)
dateFormatstring | ((p: { value: unknown; locale?: string }) => string)How this column's date is formatted (BACKLOG-0001955), overriding the view's `dateFormat`: a token pattern (`'dd/MM/yyyy'`) or a function, as on the view's `dateFormat`. The column's `format` still wins when both are given. (optional)

Gantt

A headless Gantt controller: holds the model, recomputes on edits, emits changes.

Properties
PropertyTypeDescription
tasksGanttTask[]The current tasks, as fresh shallow copies — mutating them changes nothing; call `applyEdit` or `setTasks`. (read-only)
dependenciesGanttDependency[]The current links, as fresh copies, always in the normalised `{ from, to, type, lag }` form. (read-only)
scheduleGanttSchedule | nullThe latest schedule result. It keeps the last successful one when a recompute fails, so a cycle does not blank the view. (read-only)
criticalstring[]The critical task ids from the latest schedule; empty when the last compute failed. (read-only)
conflictsGanttConflict[]Constraints the latest schedule could not honour (empty when all are satisfied). (read-only)
schedulingConflictsGanttSchedulingConflict[]The normalised scheduling-conflict set (BACKLOG-0002004): one entry per cycle, unhonoured constraint, blocked lock or missed deadline, the shape the `schedulingConflict` event and the issues panel consume. A copy; empty when the plan is clean. (read-only)
autoSchedulebooleanWhether the controller was asked to cascade an edit down the dependency chain rather than only recomputing. (read-only)
gridunknownThe grid this controller is bound to, or null for a standalone plan. (read-only)
weekStartDaynumberThe resolved week-start weekday (0-6, Sunday=0; BACKLOG-0002006): the plan's own `weekStartDay` option, else the bound grid's locale default (Monday for en-GB and most locales, Sunday for en-US), else Sunday. (read-only)
overAllocationsGanttOverAllocation[]The over-allocations from the latest schedule (BACKLOG-0000948). (read-only)
resourceLoadGanttResourceLoad | nullThe latest resource-load report, or null before a successful schedule (BACKLOG-0000948). (read-only)
resourceNamesMap<string, string>The display name for each resource key (BACKLOG-0001941): a `resources` entry `{ id, name }` is keyed by its `id` — what assignments refer to — but labelled by its `name`, so the views show the human name while every lookup stays keyed on the id. A resource with no distinct `name` maps its key to itself. (read-only)
titlestring | nullThe plan's own title, from `createGantt({ title })`; `null` when none was given. (read-only)
rows{ /** * A load resets `changes()`/`hasChanges` to this plan (BACKLOG-0002003) * unless `{ keepChanges: true }` keeps accumulating against the * baseline already held. */ apply(change: { add?: GanttTask[]; update?: GanttTask[]; remove?: Array<string | GanttTask> }, loadOpts?: { keepChanges?: boolean }): { added: GanttTask[]; updated: GanttTask[]; removed: string[]; }; }The live consumer surface, mirroring `grid.rows.apply`, so a Data Router can drive the Gantt like any other view. previewEdit(patch: { id: string | number; start?: number; duration?: number }): GanttSchedule | nullpatch: { id: string | number; start?: number; duration?: number }GanttSchedule | nullDry-run a start or duration edit and return the schedule it would produce, without changing the model, emitting `schedule` or writing back. applyEdit(patch: { id: string | number; start?: number; end?: number; duration?: number; percentComplete?: number; work?: number | Array<{ date: number | string | Date; hours: number }>; effort?: number; units?: number; addResource?: string | { resource?: string; name?: string; id?: string; units?: number }; removeResource?: string | { resource?: string; name?: string; id?: string }; assignments?: Array<{ resource: string; units?: number }> }, editOpts?: { writeBack?: boolean; event?: unknown }): GanttSchedulepatch: { id: string | number; start?: number; end?: number; duration?: number; percentComplete?: number; work?: number | Array<{ date: number | string | Date; hours: number }>; effort?: number; units?: number; addResource?: string | { resource?: string; name?: string; id?: string; units?: number }; removeResource?: string | { resource?: string; name?: string; id?: string }; assignments?: Array<{ resource: string; units?: number }> }
editOpts?: { writeBack?: boolean; event?: unknown }		GanttScheduleApply one task edit and recompute — the single gated choke point every drag, keypress, table cell and workload cell commits through. A `work` ARRAY is the task's per-day contour (BACKLOG-0001282). Given without an explicit `start`/`end`/`duration` it SETS the span: the task starts on the contour's first day and runs through its last, so booking hours beyond the bar extends it and clearing an edge bucket pulls it back. Conversely, a `start` or `duration` in the patch re-times an existing contour rather than discarding it — a move keeps its shape, a resize stretches it across the new span at the same daily levels. `assignments` replaces the task's whole roster in ONE edit (BACKLOG-0001868) — what the split view's assignment picker commits — so several adds, removes and units changes are a single change event. It reschedules as a `units` edit to the roster's total under the task's `schedulingMode` (`units` is 1 for 100%); on a fixed-duration, effort-driven task a changed set of resources splits the held total as `addResource` does. An empty roster clears the assignments and leaves the duration alone.

on(event: GanttEventName, fn: (payload: GanttEventPayloads[GanttEventName]) => void): () => voidevent: GanttEventName
fn: (payload: GanttEventPayloads[GanttEventName]) => void		() => voidRegister an event listener; returns a function that unsubscribes. What each event carries is {@link GanttEventPayloads}; the listener is declared with the widest of them, so narrow on the name inside it.

undo(): boolean—booleanUndo the most recent plan edit (BACKLOG-0001864), restoring the exact prior plan — tasks, dependencies, assignments and work contours — and re-running scheduling to the identical result. A coalesced drag and a whole `level()` run each undo as one step. Emits `schedule` (views redraw) and `history`. Returns `false` when there is nothing to undo. History is session only: it is cleared by `setTasks`/`setState` and lost on reload, and is NOT part of `getState`/`setState`.
redo(): boolean—booleanRedo the most recently undone edit (BACKLOG-0001864). Returns `false` when there is nothing to redo; the redo branch is discarded the moment a new edit is made.
canUndo(): boolean—booleanWhether there is an edit to undo (BACKLOG-0001864).
canRedo(): boolean—booleanWhether there is an edit to redo (BACKLOG-0001864).
clearHistory(): void—voidForget the whole undo/redo timeline (BACKLOG-0001864) and emit `history`. History is also cleared by `setTasks`/`setState` and lost on reload; this is the explicit reset after loading a fresh plan through `rows.apply`.
changes(): GanttChanges—GanttChangesThe plan changes accumulated since the last `commit()` or load (BACKLOG-0002003): diffed fresh against the committed baseline on every call, so an add immediately removed, or an edit undone back to the baseline, nets to nothing.
commit(): void—voidMark the plan clean (BACKLOG-0002003): the tasks, dependencies and assignments as they stand now become the new baseline `changes()` diffs against, and `hasChanges` drops to `false`, firing `dirtyChange` when it was `true`. Leaves the undo/redo timeline untouched.
select(ids: string | string[]): string[]ids: string | string[] — one task id or severalstring[] the selection now, in selection orderSelect one or more tasks by id (BACKLOG-0001995). Adds to the current selection rather than replacing it (the inverse of {@link deselect}), with the last id becoming the range anchor a later Shift gesture grows from. Fires one `selectionChange` with `cause: 'api'` when the selection actually changes, and the mounted view reflects it onto the grid rows and the timeline bars. Unknown or already-selected ids are harmless.
deselect(ids: string | string[]): string[]ids: string | string[] — one task id or severalstring[] the selection now, in selection orderDeselect one or more tasks by id (BACKLOG-0001995): removes them, leaving the rest — call `gantt.deselect(gantt.selected())` to clear. Fires one `selectionChange` with `cause: 'api'` when the selection changes.
selected(): string[]—string[]The selected task ids, in selection order (BACKLOG-0001995). The whole selection, including tasks the current view has collapsed away or filtered out — they stay selected (see {@link selectedHidden}).
selectedHidden(): string[]—string[]The selected task ids that are not currently visible (BACKLOG-0001995): still selected, but collapsed under a summary row or hidden by a filter. Empty when no view has drawn yet.
copyTasks(ids: Array<string | number>): string | nullids: Array<string | number> — the task ids to copystring | null the text form, or `null` when nothing was copiedCopy tasks and their subtrees to the Gantt clipboard (BACKLOG-0002012): every field with assignments, and the links between two copied tasks (a link to an outside task is dropped on paste). The tab-separated text form (name, start, end, duration, percent) is also written to the system clipboard where the browser allows it. Ctrl/Cmd+C does the same on the selection.

addTask(task?: Partial<GanttTask>, target?: { above?: string | number; below?: string | number; child?: string | number; successorOf?: string | number; predecessorOf?: string | number; event?: unknown; interactive?: boolean }): string | null | Promise<string | null>task?: Partial<GanttTask>
target?: { above?: string | number; below?: string | number; child?: string | number; successorOf?: string | number; predecessorOf?: string | number; event?: unknown; interactive?: boolean }		string | null | Promise<string | null> the new id, `null` on a veto or an unknown anchor, or a Promise of either when a handler deferredAdd a task above, below, as a child of, as a successor of or as a predecessor of another, as one undo step, gated on the vetoable `beforeTaskAdd` (BACKLOG-0002000). A successor or predecessor is linked finish-to-start. Omitted fields default: a catalogue name, a duration of 1, and the anchor's start (a successor is placed by its link). The new task is selected. /** * Draw a small corner mark (BACKLOG-0002003, theme token * `--lattice-dirty-mark`) on every built-in task-table cell whose field * differs from the committed baseline — cleared the instant `commit()` * runs or the plan loads clean. An `<button * aria-pressed>` switch in the band’s header toggles it (and the * histogram band’s, when one is mounted) live; the same switch is * reachable off the view as `setWorkloadUnit`/`getWorkloadUnit`. * Editing in percent mode writes HOURS: typing `50` books half the * resource’s available hours in that bucket, through the identical * `applyEdit` path an hours edit uses. `'cost'` draws hours × the * resource’s rate in force each day (BACKLOG-0001866), read off the * SAME `computeWorkload` pass, falling back to hours for a resource * with no rate data; cost has no capacity concept, so there is no * over-highlight or capacity line in this unit. */ unit?: 'hours' | 'percent' | 'cost'; /** * Utilisation thresholds colouring each cell (and the histogram * band’s bars, when one is mounted) by how full the bucket is * (BACKLOG-0001942): each entry is `{ at, className | colour }`, * `at` a fraction of the bucket’s available hours (0.8 = 80% * booked), and either a `className` added to the cell/bar or a * `colour` applied inline (spelled `colour` or `color`). The highest * threshold a bucket reaches wins. Applies in every unit — hours, * percent and cost — because the fraction is always read off the * hours figures. Omitted, no threshold colouring. */ thresholds?: Array<{ at: number; className?: string; colour?: string; color?: string }>; /** Draw the Unassigned row (default true); `false` hides it (BACKLOG-0001942). */ showUnassigned?: boolean; /** * Group the resource rows by a resource field (e.g. `'department'`) or * a function of the resource spec (BACKLOG-0001949): one collapsible * header row per group, carrying the group's summed load and available * hours, with that group's resources beneath it. Unset, no grouping. */ groupBy?: string | ((resource: Record<string, unknown>) => unknown); /** * Load-only bookings from other projects (BACKLOG-0001940): hours * that count in this band, the histogram and the band’s own * over-allocation highlight, and are listed as "other project" * sub-rows under their resource, but that name no task — never drawn * as a row or a bar, and never touching the critical path, the * project finish or a summary. See {@link GanttWorkloadExternal}. * The equivalent per-task spelling is a task’s own `loadOnly: true` * flag. */ external?: GanttWorkloadExternal[]; /** * The resource-name column’s width in pixels (BACKLOG-0001950), * sized independently of the task table’s first column (which is what * it followed before, so a narrow WBS column truncated every name). * Defaults to the band’s own label width; the full name is always * carried as a title. */ labelWidth?: number; /** * The hover/focus tooltip on a bucket cell (BACKLOG-0001996): `false` * hides it, and a function supplies the host's own content, receiving * the cell payload ({@link GanttWorkloadCellEvent}) and returning a * `string`, a DOM node, or `null` to show nothing. Omitted, the * built-in card lists the contributing tasks. */ tooltip?: boolean | ((payload: GanttWorkloadCellEvent) => string | Node | null | undefined); }; /** * A resource histogram band beneath the split view, below the workload * band when both are mounted (BACKLOG-0001860): one row per resource, * drawn as bars per time bucket rather than a table of numbers, with a * capacity line and a hover/focus tooltip. `true` takes the defaults * below; an object overrides them; omitted, no band is drawn. * * The bars come from the SAME derivation the workload band uses * (`computeWorkload`, driven off `work`/`hours`/`assignments` exactly as * documented on `workload` above) — this is a second PICTURE of the same * numbers, not a second source of them. The non-stacked bar for a bucket * over capacity carries both the normal fill up to capacity and the * over-allocation fill above it; `stacked: 'task'` additionally splits * the within-capacity portion into one coloured segment per contributing * task, still capped by the one over-allocation segment on top. An * accessible table (resource, period, hours/percent, capacity, * over-allocated) is rebuilt alongside the bars on every redraw. * * Mounted alongside `workload` at a fixed `height`, the two bands share * it rather than squeezing the timeline: the timeline keeps at least * half the view, the bands shrink in proportion when both do not * otherwise fit, and — rather than ever reading as an unreadably thin * sliver of bars — the histogram collapses to its header strip (with a * console warning naming the height it needs) when even its * proportional share would leave too little room for the plot. */ histogram?: boolean | { /** The band's resource-table columns when `createGrid` is injected (BACKLOG-0001956): the built-in id `'resource'` (required), an override of it, and host columns, including {@link GanttBandColumn} ones with their own `value`/`render` (BACKLOG-0001951). */ columns?: Array<string | GanttGridColumn | GanttBandColumn>; /** Which resources to draw, and in what order; omitted draws every resource `computeWorkload` finds, Unassigned last. */ resources?: string[]; /** `'hours'` (default) draws raw hours; `'percent'` draws hours as a percentage of capacity, falling back to `'hours'` for a resource with no capacity; `'cost'` draws hours × rate (BACKLOG-0001866), falling back to `'hours'` for a resource with no rate data, with no capacity line in this unit. */ unit?: 'hours' | 'percent' | 'cost'; /** `'task'` stacks one segment per contributing task; `false` (default) draws one solid bar per bucket. */ stacked?: 'task' | false; /** The band’s total height in pixels; default 160. */ height?: number; /** * The band's maximum height in pixels (BACKLOG-0001964), exactly as the * workload band's `maxHeight`: the band sizes to its rows and caps at * this many pixels, scrolling on its own past it instead of squeezing * the plan. Omitted, the band keeps its fixed `height`. */ maxHeight?: number; /** A band row’s height in pixels; default 48. */ rowHeight?: number; /** * The resource-name column’s width in pixels (BACKLOG-0001950), * sized independently of the task table’s first column, exactly as * the workload band’s `labelWidth`; the full name is always carried * as a title. */ labelWidth?: number; }; /** * The row/bar context menu (BACKLOG-0001945): a function returning the * menu items for a task, shown in the grid’s own menu component on a * right-click (or Menu key / Shift+F10) over that task’s table row or * timeline bar. Omit it and no context menu is installed — the grid’s * own cell menu is left alone. See {@link GanttContextMenu}. */ contextMenu?: GanttContextMenu; /** * A collapsible scheduling-conflicts issues panel inside the view chrome * (BACKLOG-0002004): lists each conflict with a count badge, keyboard * operable, a click that scrolls to and highlights the tasks, and the fix * actions safe for each kind (remove the offending link, set the * constraint to as-soon-as-possible, unlock the task, clear the deadline), * each one undo step. `false` (the default) draws no panel. */ issuesPanel?: boolean; /** * Linked data bands under the gantt and the resource bands * (BACKLOG-0001958): each a measure per task (or summary, resource or * host group) per timeline bucket, with totals, formats, thresholds, * optional editing and a series that follows its task when it moves. * See {@link GanttDataBand}. */ dataBands?: GanttDataBand[]; }		GanttSplitViewMount the JOINED split view (BACKLOG-0000938): one continuous, row-aligned surface with a left task-grid panel — by default the Task Name tree with expand/collapse, start, finish, duration, assignee avatars and a circular % ring (BACKLOG-0001285), plus any host columns — and the right timeline, sharing a single vertical scroll so every grid row lines up exactly with its bar row. The timeline scrolls horizontally on its own. Composes the controller's schedule; makes no change to grid core. The plan is editable from BOTH panes (BACKLOG-0001280): every gesture `mount` has — pointer drag to move, drag on the right edge to resize, arrow-key move, Shift+arrow resize, `l` to link, Delete — works on the timeline here, and a `start`/`end`/`duration`/`progress`/`name` column in the left panel is inline-editable on a double-click. Both routes commit through the same `applyEdit` choke point, so `beforeTaskMove`, `beforeTaskResize`, `beforeProgressChange` and `beforeTaskEdit` stay the single veto whichever pane the edit came from. The three switches that govern it carry the same meaning and the same defaults as `mount`'s: `editable` (default true) turns every edit on or off, both panes at once; `keyboard` (default true) turns off the focusable bars, the arrow-key gestures and the ARIA announcements while leaving pointer editing alone; and `resizeZone` (default 6) is how many pixels in from a bar's right edge begin a resize rather than a move. `workload` adds the resource band beneath the plan (BACKLOG-0001281), which is display-only — it reports hours, it does not accept them.
captureBaseline(): Array<{ id: string; baselineStart: number; baselineEnd: number; baselineDuration: number }>—Array<{ id: string; baselineStart: number; baselineEnd: number; baselineDuration: number }>Capture a baseline (planned) snapshot of the current schedule as HOST data (this does not mutate the tasks). Store it and feed it back as `baselineStart`/`baselineEnd` task fields to get variance and ghost bars.
earnedValue(evmOpts?: { statusDate?: number | string | Date; costField?: string; actualCostField?: string }): GanttEarnedValueevmOpts?: { statusDate?: number | string | Date; costField?: string; actualCostField?: string }GanttEarnedValueCompute earned-value (EVM) metrics for the current plan at a status date (BACKLOG-0000958): PV/EV/AC and the derived SV/CV/SPI/CPI per task, rolled up to summaries and the project. Budget (BAC) is the task's `cost`, or its duration when no cost is given; AC comes from `actualCost`.
sCurve(scurveOpts?: { statusDate?: number | string | Date; bucket?: 'day' | 'week' | 'month'; costField?: string; actualCostField?: string }): GanttSCurvePoint[]scurveOpts?: { statusDate?: number | string | Date; bucket?: 'day' | 'week' | 'month'; costField?: string; actualCostField?: string }GanttSCurvePoint[]Compute the cumulative S-curve for the current plan (BACKLOG-0001867) — one `{ date, pv, ev, ac }` point per time bucket from the project start to the project finish. The same arithmetic `earnedValue` performs, evaluated over time: PV climbs to the total budget at the finish, EV and AC stop at the status date.
mountSCurve(container: unknown, scurveOpts?: { width?: number; height?: number; bucket?: 'day' | 'week' | 'month'; statusDate?: number | string | Date; costField?: string; actualCostField?: string }): { refresh(): void; destroy(): void }container: unknown
scurveOpts?: { width?: number; height?: number; bucket?: 'day' | 'week' | 'month'; statusDate?: number | string | Date; costField?: string; actualCostField?: string }		{ refresh(): void; destroy(): void }Mount a cumulative PV/EV/AC S-curve chart (BACKLOG-0001867): three lines, a vertical status-date marker, a legend with the project SPI and CPI, and a visually hidden table of the points. Redraws on every schedule recompute; the returned view's `refresh()` redraws it now and `destroy()` detaches it.
unmount(): void—voidDetach the mounted view, if any. The host still owns the container.
destroy(): void—voidDestroy the mounted view, drop the grid subscriptions and clear the listeners. The controller then refuses further edits with a warning; the host still owns the grid and the container.

GanttMSPDIModel

The model {@link importMSPDI} returns and {@link exportMSPDI} takes.

PropertyTypeDescription
tasksGanttTask[]The plan's tasks, written out with their outline level, summary and milestone flags, constraints, baseline and progress.
dependenciesGanttDependency[]The typed links, written as predecessor links with their lag on the successor task. (optional)
resourcesGanttResourceSpecThe resource list, written with each resource's capacity as `MaxUnits`. Only the array form is read here: a name-to-capacity map is ignored, and its resources then appear only through the tasks' assignments, at capacity 1. (optional)
projectStartnumber | string | DateThe project's start date. Defaults to the schedule's own start when a schedule is supplied. (optional)
calendarGanttCalendar | nullThe working-time calendar written as the project's base calendar — a `weekends` preset or explicit workdays and holidays. Null writes no calendar. (optional)
calendarsGanttCalendarsThe named calendars tasks and resources may reference (BACKLOG-0002009); each is written as its own calendar. (optional)
weekStartDaynumberThe project's week start (0-6, Sunday=0; BACKLOG-0002006), written as MSPDI's own `<WeekStartDay>`. Omitted writes no element. (optional)
scheduleGanttScheduleA computed schedule, so the written start and finish dates are the scheduled ones. Without it (or with a failed one) the tasks' own placements are used. (optional)
